# Volume 5 — साहित्यम् · Literature & Mastery (B2 → C1) — the plan

> **Status: draft, not yet binding (2026-11-18).** Designed by three independent candidate syllabi, judged by three
> readers, synthesised, critiqued (80 gaps), repaired, and checked (6 gaps left open at the check; its 50 exact
> follow-up fixes applied, and the one length statement it left open corrected by the editor). Still to come before
> it binds the chapter fact bases of Volume 5, as the Volume 4 plan had them: the editor's decisions on the deputy's
> answers in §7.3, a line-by-line review by chapter block with every finding challenged, and a post-check. The
> scripts it cites as `work/…` are kept in `research/evidence/vol5-plan/` as they were used; they read the session's
> local corpora and are made runnable from the repository at the review.

The binding lesson-by-lesson plan for the last volume of Sanskritam 2.0, written in the
structure of `research/notes/vol4-plan.md`. It was synthesised from three candidate designs
scored by three judges: the base is the design "texts first" (ranked first), with grafts from
"skill first" (the dictionary lesson, the self-check toolkit, retroversion, the Rāmāyaṇa's own
account of the śloka, true yamakas, the upamā/rūpaka compounds) and "tradition first" (one
commentary line per text, Kedāra's lines scanned on themselves, Whitney §113b for the gāyatrī,
samasyā and Rājaśekhara's after-examination, the help stages, the travelling verse Panc 4.14 =
2.44); every judge's finding on the base was re-verified and fixed or answered (§7). It was then repaired by the
editor-in-chief's deputy against a critique of 80 gaps (§7.3, §7.4; the synthesis text is kept as
`vol5-plan.synth.md`). Spine:
**the texts first.** The canon was chosen
before anything else, by measurement against the 627 words the learner owns at entry and the
forms Volumes 3–4 taught; each lesson is then built round one text (or one small set of
verses), and the skills — anvaya, scansion, figures, composition — are attached where a text
calls for them. The volume is a graded reader with its apparatus, and it ends in a capstone
that composes, recites, derives and translates.

**Sources re-checked for this plan (local, read-only).** Every reading below was re-found by
line in a local GRETIL e-text (file and line given with each text, §5); the edition statement
printed for a text is the one in its e-text header and nothing more. Scansions were made by a
script (`work/v5tools.py`, Kedāra's weight rule of KedV 1.9, below; every
scansion line of §5 regenerated by `work/scan5.py` at synthesis, verse by verse, and printed at the repair with × for
every pāda-final syllable) and checked by hand; metres were identified against Kedāra's gaṇa formulas
(`work/metres.py`). Gloss ratios were counted by script
(`work/measure.py`) from the pause forms (generated by `render.py`; not printed in §5), names excluded, with every
token's status set by hand (§5 lists them; re-coded at the repair by `work/rep/repair_state.py`, §7.4 G4, G6). The
Apte headword each word not owned leads to was found by `work/aptecheck.py`, rewritten at the repair to generate its
candidates by the six steps of c02.l02 (§7.4 G7). Ownership was checked against
`v5/owned-at-entry.tsv` (1,066 rows, Volumes 1–4) with `owned5.py`; every new headword was
looked up in Monier-Williams (`<k1>` lookup; the four particle headwords are phrase entries
under *kim*, *kadā*, *yadi*, MW). Sūtra numbers and texts were looked up in the Aṣṭādhyāyī data
file, and three Kāśikā lines were read for them (on 3.1.40, 3.1.110 and 6.4.56, `jvkasipu.htm`). Whitney was
read by chapter file (§579 on the prohibitive; §533b and §578 on the first-person "imperative", an old subjunctive;
§363a on लक्ष्मीः; §§1070–1072 on the periphrastic perfect; §113b on resolving y and v in the mantras). The 1.x book's literature, metre, figures, composition and capstone
pages (pp. 47–51, 56–60, 64–68, 119–137) were re-read with pdftotext.

Status codes as in the Volume 4 plan: **✓** productive · **◐** recognition (parse when met)
· **○** labelled preview or display. In inventories: **T** taught (owned lemma or taught
formation) · **C** chunk · **L** recognised under a card, a taught formation or an owned
upasarga · **G** glossed · names apart. Sūtra tiers as in the Volume 4 plan §1.4.1: **Q**
quoted, recognised by gist · **D** a derivation step's authority, printed · **K** on a card ·
**R** read with a commentary · **TN** Teacher Notes only. Text labels follow decision Q12 of
the Volume 4 plan: **S** subhāṣita (c01), **P** Pañcatantra / Hitopadeśa prose (c02), **E**
epic, **G** Gītā, **U** Upaniṣad (c03), **M** metre text (c04), **X** unseen exam texts.

---

## 0. How to use this plan

1. It is the binding contract for five chapter fact bases, in the way the Volume 4 plan binds its
   six: each copies its chapter block from §4 as its section 0 and may not change a lesson's
   teaching point, texts, sūtras and tiers, headwords or ledger figures without an editor
   decision. The decisions taken at synthesis are in §7.1–§7.2; the deputy's decisions on the open questions, for
   the editor to confirm, are in §7.3; the critique the plan was repaired against, and its fixes, gap by gap, in §7.4.
2. Each text is printed **once**, in §5, and is referred to elsewhere by its label. A fact base
   re-runs the scripts named above on every line it prints.
3. The ledger chain (§3) is **627 → 659 · 691 · 714 · 733 · 751** (124 new headwords).
4. The memory budget (§1.3) is canonical: four memorised verse sets, two metre patterns and one
   definition (KedV 1.9) by heart, the other definitions read with the card open and recognised
   "According to …", seven new Q sūtras.
5. Nothing follows Volume 5. Every item this plan does not teach is named in §6 as lying
   beyond the course, with what the learner would need to go on.
6. Every count in this plan (words, gloss ratios, syllables, corpus figures, the ledger) is
   re-derivable by a script in `v5/work/` (§4.0.10 names each). A fact base re-runs them; where
   a figure changes, every place that states it changes together.

---

## 1. Volume principles, what becomes allowed when, and the memory budget

### 1.1 The principles (how Volume 5 differs from Volume 4)

1. **The text first.** Volume 4's recipe was "a form you own → the rule that made it". Volume 5's
   is "a text worth reading → what it takes to read it". Each teaching lesson runs one recipe:
   - **(1)** the text, printed as its e-text has it, joined (STYLE_GUIDE §3.1), with its source
     line (collection, number, e-text file and edition statement);
   - **(2)** a first read with the gloss line covered: the learner marks what they can and
     cannot read (the gloss ratio of §5 says what to expect);
   - **(3)** the lesson's one teaching point, taught on this text;
   - **(4)** the learner performs it: the pause form, then (for verse) the anvaya, then the
     translation, each written before the course's version is uncovered;
   - **(5)** production: sentences, then (from c05) paragraphs and verse, with the lesson's
     forms;
   - **(6)** the text recited or read aloud, from c04 on in metre.
   No lesson opens on a rule, a table or a list of terms.
2. **Every text the course prints is one the project opened itself.** A reading, a locus or a
   number prints only from a local e-text, re-found by line (§4.0.5, §5). Where an e-text reading
   looks wrong the plan says so and **chooses another text instead of emending it** — and it
   never normalises a reading silently in the pause form. The passages rejected on this ground
   are listed in §4.0.5 (c), among them Hitopadeśa 0.18 (a nine-syllable pāda in the e-text),
   Nītiśataka 1.71 (two unmetrical quarters), Hitopadeśa 0.46 (*upeyāḥ* where the sense needs
   *apeyāḥ*) and the mandākrāntā line of Kedāra's own e-text (*guru* where syllable 16 must be heavy; Sulhaṇa's
   e-text of the same verse, which reads *gurū*, is printed instead, W20); the metre lesson uses this
   as its own evidence that scansion is a tool of reading. An e-text's own departure from full sandhi, or its own
   markup, is printed as it stands and noted beside it, as STYLE_GUIDE §3.1 asks (S3c2, W20); the normalised reading
   stands only in the course's pause form.
3. **The anvaya is performed, not watched** (§6 of the Volume 4 plan, row "Anvaya as a method in
   depth → v5.c01"). The six moves are taught in v5.c01.l01 and every later verse is first put in
   prose order by the learner:
   - **1** pause form (Volume 3's padaccheda); **2** find the finite verb, or decide that there is
     none and supply it (asti, bhavati) in brackets; **3** find the subject in agreement with it;
     **4** attach every other case form to the word that governs it (object, instrument, place,
     the genitive to its noun); **5** attach every qualifier to its head by gender, number and
     case, and every relative (yaḥ, yathā, yatra) to its correlative (saḥ, tathā, tatra),
     supplying the missing one; **6** write the anvaya with brackets for supplied words, then
     translate from the anvaya, not from the verse.
   - The check is mechanical: every word of the verse appears exactly once in the anvaya, and
     every bracketed word is one the grammar requires. Self-checkable.
   - The bracket has the tradition's own name: supplying a word is *adhyāhāra*, and a commentator
     says so when he does it (Śaṅkara on BG 4.17: त्रिष्वप्यध्याहारः कर्तव्यः "in all three a word
     must be supplied", `bhgsbh_u.htm` 1523; TRADITION, shown in c01.l01).
4. **One teaching point per lesson; a second item only as a card with no production target.**
   In the reading chapters (c01–c03) the point is a reading skill or one form that the text needs;
   the ◐ forms Volume 4 handed over are met as cards. Where Volume 5 makes one productive inside a
   reading lesson whose point is something else, the card carries listed ✓ cells, as Volume 4's
   V4-D6 did: decisions **V5-D1** (the causative's kṛt forms, c02.l01), **V5-D2** (the
   periphrastic perfect, c03.l01), **V5-D3** (the Ā perfect, c03.l02), and, recorded at the repair, **V5-D4** (the
   feminines in -वी, -इनी, -मती, c01.l04) and **V5-D5** (the laṅ passive, c02.l03) (§1.3.3, §7.2, §7.3 Q2). The
   perfect forms are never a lesson's teaching point: the Volume 4 plan's §6 sends them to the
   epic reading, "not a lesson of their own", and decision Q18 binds v5.c03 to that.
5. **Four registers, as before.**
   - What a text does is **FACT**, cited to the text by locus, or to Whitney (e.g. Whitney §579:
     the prohibitive is "mā with an augmentless past form, prevailingly aorist"; with an imperative
     it "begins to appear" in the epics).
   - What a grammarian, a metrician or a theorist of poetry says is **TRADITION**, attributed and
     assessed only "According to Kedāra …", "According to Mammaṭa …", "According to Pāṇini …".
   - Where they differ, both print. The decisive case is metre: Kedāra names four vipulās (bha,
     ra, na, ta: KedV 2.27–2.30); the Gītā and the Nala text, counted by script, use bha, ra and
     na, use no ta-vipulā at all, and use a fourth pattern, three heavy syllables, that Kedāra does
     not name (§4.4, v5.c04.l02).
   - Dates are **HISTORY**, hedged, keyed; only two print in this volume: Mammaṭa's century (c04.l04 §17) and the
     manuscript limit for Kedāra (c04.l01 §17; §4.0.9). The Pañcatantra's estimated date does not print: no page
     quoting its translator's own words was found (`olivelle1997`, not confirmed). No date prints for
     Bhartṛhari, Daṇḍin or Kālidāsa unless a fact base finds a citable source (§7.3 Q4).
   - The tradition's praise of a poet is reported only as quoted TRADITION (the verse
     upamā kālidāsasya…, MSS 7104), never in the course's own voice.
6. **No claims of scale or beauty.** Every number printed has its method: "all 444 pādas of the
   Meghadūta in S.K. De's edition (rev. V. Raghavan), as typed in the GRETIL e-text, scan as
   mandākrāntā (script count)"; "88.4% of the odd pādas of the Bhagavadgītā (MBh 6.23–40, 1,289
   half-verses of eight-syllable pādas) have ⏑ − − in syllables 5–7 (script count)". The 1.x
   lines "90% of classical verse", "the most majestic", "the densest, richest [poetry] in any
   language", "Shakespeare of India", "5,000-year-old tradition" print only as quoted, sourced
   1.x errors (§4.0.4).
7. **Composition is graded and self-checkable** (c05). The course cannot mark free composition;
   it marks what can be checked — form, agreement, sandhi, the pause form, the metre, the anvaya —
   by a fixed checklist the learner runs on their own text, and it asks for a reader (a peer, a
   teacher, or the learner a week later) for what cannot: whether it says what was meant, and
   whether it sounds like Sanskrit. It says so in every composition lesson (§4.5, the checklist).
8. **Production in every lesson.** Two productive tasks at least: writing the anvaya, the pause
   form and the translation counts as one; the second is always sentences or verse of the
   learner's own.
9. **The reading help shrinks by stage** (§2.3), and each stage is taught before it is examined:
   marginal glosses for every non-owned word (c01–c02 texts) → from c02.l02, which teaches the
   way from a form to its Apte entry, every gloss line is split into "look up" (words the taught
   procedure reaches) and "glossed" (the rest) → a lemma glossary in dictionary form, with decoys,
   in which the learner finds the lemma behind each form (practised in c03.l02; the c03 and c04 exams) → an Apte extract
   with decoys and course glosses only for what the procedure cannot reach (the capstone: none). The C1
   move is not "fewer unknown words" — a new text always has them — but "the learner finds them".
   Reading is therefore reported two ways (§2.2): ledger coverage, and the course help still
   needed after Apte.
10. **Joined text throughout.** Authentic text keeps its e-text's word division, joined in
    Devanagari where the first word ends in a consonant or the second begins with an avagraha;
    the pause form beneath is labelled as the course's; course-written Sanskrit is joined with its
    pause form beneath.
11. **Exams have stated lengths, and examine only what has been taught.** Chapter exams are one
    45-minute sitting; the capstone is three sittings (45 + 45 + 25 minutes, v5.c05.l05). No exam
    asks for composition, scansion, a figure or a lookup before the lesson that teaches it.
12. **One commentary line per text, where a local file has one** (TRADITION, tier R, read with
    the course's gloss): Śaṅkara on BG 4.17 (adhyāhāra, c01.l01); on Kaṭha 6.19 (why the śāntiḥ is said three
    times, c01.l05, W14); on BG 2.47 (his analysis of कर्मफलहेतुः as कर्मफलप्राप्तेर्हेतुः, and the consequence he
    draws, कर्मफलस्यैव जन्मनो हेतुः, c03.l03); and on Bṛhad. 1.3.28 (the Brāhmaṇa explains its own mantra, and the
    formulas' accent, c03.l04, Teacher Notes); Kedāra's own definition lines, scanned by the learner as verses
    of the metre they define (c04.l01–l03). The headers' warnings are printed with them (the
    Bṛhadāraṇyaka commentary "is not proofread"; the Gītā commentary is "ascribed to Saṃkara"; the Kaṭha commentary is
    "Samkara (attrib.)"; the Kāvyamīmāṃsā "is not proof-read").

Everything else is read with its card open: the gaṇa table (KedV 1.7, as a card), the
vipulā names (KedV 2.27–2.30, with 2.25, Kedāra's name for the even-pāda rule), the other metres' formulas, the
figure definitions.

#### 1.3.2 What is recognised by gist (assessed "According to …")

- **Sūtras, 7 new Q** (§4.0.8): 3.1.40, 3.1.96, 2.3.38 (D in Volume 4, now Q), 3.4.81, 3.3.175,
  6.4.74, 8.1.23. Memory load at exit for Volume 5: these 7 by gist; no new numbers by heart
  (Volume 4's 53 numbers stay the only ones).
- **Definitions, read with the card open** (§4.0.7): Kedāra 1.9 (also by heart), 2.21, 2.22,
  2.27–2.30 (the odd-pāda vipulās, one card) with 2.25 (the even-pāda rule), 3.28, 3.29, 3.79, and the mandākrāntā
  line in Sulhaṇa's text (KVrk 3.100, W20; ◐ card); Mammaṭa K79a, K81b, K83a, K87a, K93a (as
  numbered in the GRETIL Kārikāvalī); Daṇḍin KA 2.66 as the rūpaka example and KA 1.56–57 as the anuprāsa
  examples (W22); the Nirukta's line on the gāyatrī (7.12). Read, never assessed: Rāmāyaṇa 1.2.17 (the text's own
  naming of the śloka, W4). In Teacher Notes, never assessed: Mammaṭa K109 (arthāntaranyāsa), Daṇḍin KA 2.221 and
  2.234 (utprekṣā), Vāmana VKS 1.1.1–2 and 1.2.6 (§7.4 G30).

| -अनीय gerundive | **✓** | c02.l03 | regular (guṇa + -अनीय) |
| -य gerundive | ◐ parse-only (lexicalised members glossed: शक्यम् owned) | c02.l03 | irregular formation |
| Genitive absolute | **✓** | c02.l04 | a Δ of the owned locative absolute |
| Future participle; -अम् absolutive | ◐ parse-only; **not planned in Volume 5**: no reading prints one | — | rare (§6) |
| Class-3 cells beyond ददाति; athematic Ā cells (ब्रूते) | class 3 beyond ददाति: **not planned in Volume 5** (no lesson text prints a finite form; हीनः is a participle; the one finite form printed, संदधाति in W22, KA 1.56, is a display translated there); ब्रूते recognised (C) | throughout c02 | ब्रूते is the Hitopadeśa's speech verb |
| Root nouns beyond the listed cells; अदस् (असौ) | ◐ parse-only | c02 readings | — |
| Numeral derivatives (-धा, -शः: एकैकशः) | ◐ parse-only; **not planned in Volume 5**: no reading prints one (E2's 3.50.24, with एकैकशः, is not printed; एकैकम् in S1b is a compound) | — | — |
| Further taddhitas (-ईयस्/-इष्ठ, apatya), avyayībhāva, cvi (उपहारीकरोषि) | ◐ parse-only | c02.l03 (cvi met in P3) | — |
| Accent | one TRADITION card | c03.l04 (the accents read on Śvet. 4.6 in its accented witness AV 9.9.20; the padapāṭha shown on RV 3.62.10; §7.3 Q11) | Q18 |
| The feminines of u-adjectives, -इन् and -मत् (गुर्वी, लघ्वी; क्षयिणी; वृद्धिमती) | **✓** (Δ card with listed ✓ cells, decision V5-D4) | c01.l04 §15 | Δs of owned tables (Whitney §344b) |

#### 1.3.4 What stays parse-only at C1 (said in the exit profile)

The aorist and the rare lakāras, the perfect's 1st and 2nd persons, the secondary conjugations,
the -य gerundive, the participles of the perfect and future, root nouns beyond the listed cells,
अदस्, the class-3 and athematic Ā cells beyond the listed, numeral derivatives, the further
taddhitas, avyayībhāva and cvi. All are recognised and named when met; none is produced.

### 1.4 Conventions for printing a text, a pause form, an anvaya, a translation, a scansion, a figure and a commentary line

**1.4.1 An authentic text.** Label (§5), locus, e-text file and line, and the edition statement of
the e-text's header — nothing more about the edition. The Devanagari is made from the e-text's
IAST by the joining rule of STYLE_GUIDE §3.1 (`work/render.py`); the IAST is the e-text's, word
division and spelling kept. Beneath it, the **pause form, labelled "(ours)"**. The course never
emends: an e-text reading that looks wrong (a syllable too many, an ending that cannot agree, an
evident slip) means the passage is not used, and §4.0.5 (c) lists every such case. Where a reading
looks odd but was checked and kept, the plan says why (X3's *nasvasthām*, §5.3). The pause form
never normalises a reading silently (the e-text's सिद्ध्यति in S3c stays, with a note). Where an e-text departs from
full sandhi or uses its own markup (the Yājñavalkya e-text's m for anusvāra, its dots and brackets; Sulhaṇa's unjoined
visarga), the line is printed as it stands, the departure noted beside it (STYLE_GUIDE §3.1), and the normalised
reading stands only in the pause form (S3c2, W20).

**1.4.2 An anvaya.** Prose order, written by the learner before the course's is uncovered (the six
moves, §1.1 principle 3). Supplied words in round brackets (adhyāhāra); from c01.l04, a
compound's analysis in square brackets beside it, in the commentators' form (आरम्भगुर्वी [आरम्भे
गुर्वी]). Check: every word of the verse once; every bracket required by the grammar or by the
commentator the lesson reads.

**1.4.3 A translation.** The course's own, made from the anvaya and checked word by word against
it; no published translation is copied or paraphrased. Labelled "(ours)".

**1.4.4 A scansion.** ⏑ light, − heavy, by Kedāra's rule (KedV 1.9: long vowel, anusvāra,
visarga, or a following conjunct, counted across word boundaries within the half-verse); the last
syllable of a pāda is printed × everywhere, in §5, in the scripts' output and in the lessons: by वा पादान्ते it
counts either way, so a key may not mark it light or heavy (§7.4 G47). An
anuṣṭubh odd pāda is named by its syllables 5–7 with Kedāra's names (pathyā ⏑ − −; na-, bha-, ra-,
ta-vipulā) and, for − − −, as "ma-type, not in Kedāra's list". Counts over a text are FACT with
their method and script; the treatise's statements are TRADITION, printed beside them.

**1.4.5 A figure.** Identified by the parts its definition names, in a parts table; the definition
is printed and attributed ("According to Mammaṭa (K87a) …"). Where the treatise does not itself
cite the verse, the identification is labelled "the course's application of Mammaṭa's
definition". Figures are never praised in the course's voice.

**1.4.6 A commentary line.** Printed as its e-text has it (the Sansknet e-texts often run words
together: "word boundaries are usually not marked"), with the course's pause form and translation;
tier R; the header's warning printed with it; assessed only "According to Śaṅkara …".

**1.4.7 A gloss line and a ratio.** Each word token of the pause form is T, C, L or G (status
codes above), names apart; from c02.l02 the G items are split into **look up** (the procedure of
c02.l02 reaches the Apte headword) and **glossed** (it does not). Ratios are counted **by token,
never by type**. A word is L only under an owned lemma, a taught formation or a card the learner
has met; a word that occurs only inside a sūtra or an exam is not owned. A new sense of an owned lemma (rule 27) is
glossed in the gloss line and counted L (X1a's वंश "bamboo", U1's वा "indeed").

**1.4.8 The self-check toolkit** (from the design "skill first"; used from c01, gathered in the
c05 checklist). What a learner working alone can check, and what only a reader can:

| Check | What it catches | Objective? | From |
|---|---|---|---|
| K1 answer key | anvaya, gloss, scansion, figure items with one right answer | yes | c01.l01 |
| K2 reverse anvaya: re-order one's own sentence into prose order and name each word's role and case against the verb | wrong case for the role; a missing verb | yes, against the roles map | c01.l01 |
| K3 agreement table: every adjective and participle beside its noun | agreement | yes | c01.l04 |
| K4 form audit: every form traced to an owned table or a taught formation | invented forms | yes | c02.l01 |
| K5 sandhi round-trip: join the pause form, un-join it by the six steps of v3.c04.l04, compare (the fact bases run it by script over every joined model line of this plan, §7.4 G22) | sandhi | yes | c01.l01 |
| K6 scansion and the pāda test | metre | yes | c04.l01 |
| K7 retroversion against the original: errors counted by the K2–K5 list, not by identity | case, verb, order habits | partly (the original is one good answer) | c05.l01 |
| K8 cold back-translation, at least 24 hours later, without notes | meaning, ambiguity | partly | c05.l02 (never inside a timed sitting) |
| K9 a reader (peer, teacher, or the learner a week later), with the three-question form | idiom, style, whether it is good | no — the course says so | c05.l01 |

## 2. Exit profile and entry requirements

### 2.1 Entry (checked by v4.c06.l04)

- **Ledger:** 627 readable words (Volume 4 plan §3).
- **Paradigms:** the Volume 4 ✓ list (§2.2 there): the perfect 3rd persons P, the future, the
  passive laṭ/loṭ, the present-system causative, the participles -त, -तवत् (nom.), -मान, -अत्
  (all cells), absolutive, infinitive, -तृ, -तव्य, the listed consonant stems, the numerals,
  the taught taddhitas; ◐ as listed in §1.3.3 above.
- **Sūtras:** 25 Q + 25 B of Volume 4 by gist; 53 numbers.
- **Reading, measured:** an unseen Hitopadeśa passage of 63 words (X-b of the Volume 4 plan,
  Hit 1, after 1.72) with marginal glosses for 10 words, **1 in 6.3**, names excluded. Decision Q10
  of the Volume 4 plan (a confirmed editor decision) hands Volume 5 the target "one gloss in eight to ten"; this plan
  proposes a replacement, put to the editor in §7.3 Q1.
- **Verse:** the learner has read three verses in prose order (Volume 3 c06.l04, decision D23), the thread
  verses, and quarters (MBh 3.50.11a); has never scanned or written an anvaya alone.

### 2.2 How the reading figure is measured in Volume 5

- **The count.** Word tokens of the course's pause form; a compound is one word; names excluded;
  a word is **G** if its lemma is not owned at that lesson and no card or taught formation makes it
  recognisable. Counted by `measure.py`; every token's status is printed in §5.
- **Both ways** (Volume 4 rule 22, kept): for every prepared text, the ratio with the lesson's
  own new headwords counted as known, and without them; for every unseen text, the ratio at the
  ledger of that point, and the list of any headword chosen earlier for frequency that also occurs
  in it.
- **Genre matters, and is reported.** Gnomic verse is denser than narrative prose. The measured
  figures of this plan (§5, recounted at the repair) are: prepared verse 1 in 2.0 to 1 in 27 per verse (chapter 1
  overall 18 of 140, 1 in 7.8, with the lesson headwords, its verses 1 in 2.0 to 1 in 21; G1 1 in 27, M1 1 in 6.0); prepared prose 1 in 6.5 to
  1 in 10.7 (P3 13 of 85, P1 10 of 107); prepared epic verse 1 in 4.3 to 1 in 6.6 (E1 16 of 69, E2 17 of 113); the
  Upaniṣad prose U1 1 in 68; unseen exam texts 1 in 3.6 (X4, 8 of 29) to 1 in 6.5 (X6) by ledger coverage.
- **Two figures from c02.l02 on.** Ledger coverage (the words not owned) and **course help after
  Apte** (the words the taught lookup procedure cannot reach and the course must still gloss). The
  first measures the vocabulary; the second measures independence, which is the C1 claim.
- **Why the ratio is not the target (proposed: §7.3 Q1, for the editor to confirm).** In the DCS Hitopadeśa's prose-like
  sentences (2,767 words, `work/cover5.py`, lemma-based and so stricter than the printed-word
  count), the words with a lemma not owned fall only from 990 to 915 (1 in 2.8 → 1 in 3.0) with
  all 124 Volume 5 headwords. No headword budget a self-learner can carry moves an unseen text
  to one gloss in eight to ten; the help has to change, and the learner has to be taught to
  change it (c02.l02).

### 2.3 What help an unseen text may carry, by stage

| Stage | Unseen text | Help printed | Learner's task |
|---|---|---|---|
| c01.l05 | X1, two verses (34 words) | marginal glosses for 8 (1 in 4.2) | pause form, anvaya, translation |
| c02.l05 | X2, Pañcatantra prose (69 words; 13 not owned, 1 in 5.3) | marginal glosses for 8 (1 in 8.6); the other 5 (उच्छेदम्, स्वजातीयः, सर्पवाणी, सन्धानम्, मर्त्यलोके) looked up in a printed Apte extract with 5 decoys by the c02.l02 procedure (the procedure would reach 5 of the 8 glossed as well; the exam keeps the lookup to five) | questions, parsing, lookup |
| c03.l05 | X3, epic ślokas (37 words; 9 not owned, 1 in 4.1) | **lemma glossary**: the 8 lemmas of those 9 words in dictionary form (स्वयंवरः, पार्थिवः, शासनम् …), alphabetical, with 8 decoys; no marginal glosses | find the lemma behind each form, then read |
| c04.l05 | X4, two verses (29 words; 8 not owned, 1 in 3.6) | lemma glossary (9 lemmas + 9 decoys) | scan, name metre and figure, translate |
| c05.l05 (capstone) | X5 prose (78 words; 21 not owned, 1 in 3.7) and X6 verse (13 words; 2 not owned) | **no marginal glosses**: the course glossary of Volumes 1–5, and an **Apte extract** with the entries the passages need and 10 decoys; course glosses: **none** — the procedure reaches every word not owned, श्वानम् (Apte श्वानः) and जागरयसि (Apte जागृ, the root recovered from the -अय- stem) among them (`work/aptecheck.py`) | translate, parse, scan |

### 2.4 Exit (after passing v5.c05.l05, the end of the course)

CEFR-informed functional labels only; Sanskrit has no official CEFR mapping [REF:cefr2020].
Each can-do names the task that tests it.

**Read**
1. Read an unseen passage of classical narrative prose of 60–120 words (Volume 4's exit was
   60–150 words at one gloss in six; this can-do shortens the length to 60–120, X5 being 78 words, because the
   words not owned are now looked up, not glossed), printed joined, finding the words not owned in Apte by the
   procedure of c02.l02, with **at most one course gloss in 20 words**, and answer gist and detail questions,
   parsing every finite verb, participle, absolutive and absolute, in 40 minutes. Measured two ways on X5 (the
   capstone, Hit 2, story 2, 78 words, truly unseen): ledger coverage 21 not owned, **1 in 3.7** at
   the exit ledger (25, 1 in 3.1, without the Volume 5 headwords it contains); course help after Apte **0 of
   78** (`work/aptecheck.py`). X-b of Volume 4, re-measured at the exit ledger: 9 of 63, **1 in 7.0** (1 in 6.3
   at entry). *Tested: c05.l05 task D.*
2. Read an unseen anuṣṭubh verse: write its pause form, anvaya and translation (X6, Hit 2.36: 2
   of 13 words not owned, 1 in 6.5). *Tested: c05.l05 task D.*
3. Read epic narrative ślokas (the Nala story) and name every perfect form, including the Ā
   perfect and the periphrastic perfect, and the person of any 1st/2nd-person perfect met.
   *Tested: c03.l05 task B (X3, and the seen half-verse E2 3.50.23cd for the Ā perfect उपचक्रमे) and task E.*
4. Translate BG 2.47–48 and Bṛhadāraṇyaka 1.3.28 word by word from their anvaya, give Śaṅkara's analysis of
   कर्मफलहेतुः ("according to Śaṅkara": कर्मफलप्राप्तेर्हेतुः) and the course's bahuvrīhi (कर्मफलं हेतुर्यस्य सः),
   and say which मा is the prohibitive and which the enclitic "me", according to Pāṇini (3.3.175 with 6.4.74;
   8.1.23). *Tested: c03.l05 (task A, with its translation and analysis items; task C).* (Reciting them is can-do 5.)

**Hear and recite**
5. Scan any line of anuṣṭubh or of the eleven-syllable metres syllable by syllable (Kedāra's
   rule, KedV 1.9), name the anuṣṭubh pāda as pathyā or by its vipulā according to Kedāra, count
   the gāyatrī (RV 3.62.10: 23 syllables as written, 24 read with वरेणियम्, the reading Whitney
   §113b describes for the mantras), and recite the memorised verses in metre. *Tested: c04.l05 (task B, with the
   gāyatrī count), c05.l05 task B.*
6. Identify upamā (with its four parts), rūpaka, anuprāsa, lāṭānuprāsa and yamaka in a verse read
   in the course, "according to Mammaṭa", and tell yamaka from a word repeated in the same sense.
   *Tested: c04.l05.*

**Write**
7. Write a connected paragraph of 8–10 sentences of narrative in joined Sanskrit with its pause
   form, using the perfect, the periphrastic perfect, a locative and a genitive absolute, an
   absolutive chain, a passive and a causative, and check it with the course checklist.
   *Tested: c05.l02, c05.l05 task A.*
8. Compose an anuṣṭubh śloka of their own that scans (pathyā, or a vipulā named according to
   Kedāra), on a set theme or to complete a given fourth pāda (samasyā), with its anvaya and
   translation. *Tested: c05.l04, c05.l05 task A.*

**Derive and know about**
9. Derive, according to Pāṇini, a form met in the capstone texts and not rehearsed in any
   exam (कृत्वा or प्रसुप्तः, drawn by lot) in the Volume 4 format from a printed card of the Volume 4 tools the two
   derivations use, with as many distractors. *Tested: c05.l05 task C.*
10. Sort statements about texts, metres and poets into FACT, TRADITION, HISTORY, legend and
    banned claim, and give the source line of every text read in the volume. *Tested: c05.l05
    task E (scored, 5 points: eight statements and two source lines).*

Not a can-do (it is not scored): the learner is told what lies beyond the course (§6), and the
capstone's §21 homework asks them to choose one row and say why, in Sanskrit.

**Paradigm ledger at exit.** Volume 4's ✓ list, plus: the periphrastic perfect (3rd persons),
the Ā perfect (3rd persons), the causative's kṛt forms and passive, the laṅ passive, the -अनीय
gerundive, the genitive absolute, and the feminines in -वी, -इनी, -मती of owned adjectives (c01.l04, V5-D4). ◐ as §1.3.4.

**Readable words at exit: 751** (§3).

## 3. Ledger policy (continues STYLE_GUIDE §8, rules 1–23)

Rules 1–23 stay in force; the guiding test is unchanged: a word counts once, when the learner
first owns its lemma as a headword. Volume 5 reads more words than any earlier volume and
owns few of them; rules 24–30 say which of them count.

| # | Rule | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| 24 | **Words of a read text are gloss-line items** (rule 5) unless headworded. A word is headworded because the lesson's text or production needs it, or because it ranks among the commonest lemmas of the DCS Hitopadeśa not yet owned (rule 22's list, `hitlem.txt`). It is never headworded because it occurs in an exam's unseen passage (rule 22); where a word chosen for a lesson also occurs in an unseen passage, that passage's ratio is printed both ways. | प्राणः (24 DCS tokens) counts; वृषभः does not (glossed). अधुना (15 tokens) is headworded in c05.l02 by frequency; X5 also has it, and X5 is reported both ways. |
| 25 | **Terms of metre and poetics** count once when headworded as nouns the learner uses in Sanskrit sentences (rule 20): छन्दः, मात्रा, संयोगः, यतिः, वृत्तम्, अलङ्कारः, उपमा, उपमानम्, उपमेयम्, रूपकम्, सादृश्यम्. **Labels never count, even when a sentence uses one** (as rule 20's labels never do, and as a proper name never does, rule 26): metre names (अनुष्टुभ्, गायत्री, इन्द्रवज्रा, उपजातिः, वसन्ततिलका …), gaṇa letters (य म त र ज भ न स), vipulā names (पथ्या, भविपुला …), and every figure name except उपमा and रूपकम्, which are headworded as words of the language ("likeness", "identification"): यमकम्, अनुप्रासः, लाटानुप्रासः are labels. | "इदं वृत्तं वसन्ततिलका", "अनुष्टुभि चत्वारः पादाः", "पथ्यायां पञ्चमं लघु", "… इत्यत्र यमकम्" — वृत्तम्, पादः, लघु count; the labels do not. |
| 26 | **Proper names never count** (rule 12) and are excluded from gloss ratios: नलः, दमयन्ती, भीमः, भासुरकः, करटकः, गङ्गदत्तः, वीरवरः, शूद्रकः, the Aśvins, Vidarbha, Niṣadha, and the patronymic vocative भारत ("descendant of Bharata"). The other epic vocative formulas (विशां पते, राजेन्द्र) are gloss-line items, or built compounds of owned words (rule 10). | राजेन्द्र "best of kings": इन्द्र is not owned, so it is glossed (E1); 0. |
| 27 | **A new sense of an owned lemma adds 0.** गुरु "heavy" (owned गुरुः "teacher"), लघु "light (syllable)" (owned "small"), स्वरः "accent" (owned "vowel"), वरम् "better" beside वरः "boon" (c03.l01). | गुर्वी in S4b adds 0. |
| 28 | **Forms of a formation made ✓ in Volume 5 belong to their verb** (rule 17): periphrastic and Ā perfect cells, causative kṛt forms, -अनीय gerundives. A lexicalised one counts once when headworded: प्रसन्नः "gracious" (c03.l01), इष्टः "dear", हृष्टः "glad", उत्पन्नः "arisen" (rule 18). | चिन्तयामास, उपचक्रमे add 0. |
| 29 | **Particle combinations** count once as a headword when lexicalised (rule 15): किमु "how much more", कदाचन "ever (with न/मा: never)", यद्यपि "even if", किन्तु "but". Their pattern partners (तथापि = तथा + अपि) add 0. | — |
| 30 | **What the learner looks up for their own composition does not count.** The ledger counts what the course teaches; the learner's composition word-list (c05) is theirs. Variant readings (c01.l03) are display. | — |

**Budgets.** 3–8 new headwords in a teaching lesson; 0 in exams. Volume 5 adds 124
(fewer per lesson in c04–c05, where the load is metre, figures and composition).

| Chapter | Entering | l01 | l02 | l03 | l04 | l05 | New | Exit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| c01 सुभाषितसङ्ग्रहः | 627 | 8 | 8 | 8 | 8 | 0 | 32 | **659** |
| c02 पञ्चतन्त्रम् | 659 | 8 | 8 | 8 | 8 | 0 | 32 | **691** |
| c03 महाकाव्यम् उपनिषदश्च | 691 | 8 | 7 | 4 | 4 | 0 | 23 | **714** |

### 4.0.1 Station 0 chain

- v5.c01.l01's Station 0 runs v4.c06.l04's +1-day item (the भवति derivation once more, timed) and
  v4.c06.l03's +1-week item (the three Kāśikā lines on the meta-rules); v5.c01.l02 runs
  v4.c06.l04's +1-week item (X-a and X-b re-read — now with the anvaya habit for its one verse).
- From c02 on, each chapter's l01 runs the previous exam's +1-day item and the previous chapter's
  last teaching lesson's +1-week item; its l02 runs the previous exam's +1-week item. Within a
  chapter, lesson n's §3 runs n−1's +1-day and n−2's +1-week item [REF:cepeda2006].
- **The recitation spiral.** From c01.l02 every lesson's §3 opens with the memorised verses
  recited (V1 from c01.l02; V2 from c03.l04; V3 from c03.l05; V4 from c04.l04), and from c04.l02
  recited in metre with the scansion written above.
- **The derivation spiral.** Every chapter exam re-derives, according to Pāṇini, one form of the
  Volume 4 set from the toolbox card (c01.l05 नयति; c02.l05 कृतम्; c03.l05 जगाम; c04.l05 पठ्यते),
  so that task C of the capstone is not a cold start. The capstone itself derives a form **not
  rehearsed** in any exam, with no new sūtra (कृत्वा or प्रसुप्तः, drawn by lot, from a printed card of the Volume 4
  tools the two derivations use, with as many distractors; c05.l05 task C; जगाम is excluded because c03.l05
  rehearsed it).

Learner-text loci follow the existing REFERENCES conventions: Hitopadeśa by GRETIL numbering;
Pañcatantra by book and story (Jha numbers in research notes only); MBh by the critical edition;
Bhartṛhari as "Nītiśataka n (the GRETIL e-text's numbering)".

(b) **Joins the course makes** (the e-texts' word division kept; Devanagari joined by the §3.1
rule): every case is generated by `render.py` from the e-text line; none changes a word.
**Two lines printed with the e-text's own departures, noted beside them, not joined or normalised by the course:**
S3c2 (the Yājñavalkya e-text writes m for anusvāra, dots between compound members and "(" before verbs: its header's
remarks (2), (3) and (5)) and W20 (Sulhaṇa's e-text leaves the visarga unjoined before म्भौ, where the second pause
falls, after syllable 10; full sandhi °गैर्म्भौ). The normalised reading of each stands only in the pause form.
**A reading checked and kept:** X3's *nasvasthām* (MBh 3.51.5c). The critical text writes the
negative compound *na-svastha* joined in both places it occurs (3.51.1c *nasvasthā*, 3.51.5c
*nasvasthāṃ*, `mbh_03_u.htm` 4818, 4827); the pāda scans (eight syllables, a pathyā: ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ − − ×), the sense
"not well" is clear, and the reading is the edition's, not a slip. It is printed as the e-text
has it, glossed "not well (na + svastha)", and the pause form keeps it one word.

(c) **Texts rejected because the e-text's reading looks wrong (principle 2):**

| Text | The problem (found by scansion or reading) | Instead |
|---|---|---|
| Hit 0.18 (varam eko guṇī putro …) | pāda c *ekaś candramas tamo hanti*: nine syllables | X1a = Hit 0.23 |
| Nītiśataka 1.71 (bhavanti namrās taravaḥ …) | pāda b *navāmbubhir dūrāvalambino ghanāḥ* does not scan as vaṃśastha (6th syllable heavy); pāda d *svabhāva eṣa paropakāriṇām* has eleven syllables | not used; vaṃśastha left as a card metre |
| Panc 1.439ab (upāyaṃ cintayet …) | second quarter *tathāpāyam api cintayet*: nine syllables | only 1.439cd printed in P4 |
| MBh 3.51.8 | second half *anubhūyatām ayaṃ vīrāḥ svayaṃvara iti prabho*: a nine-syllable pāda (the critical text's reading) | cut from X3 |
| Panc 1, story 1 (the monkey and the wedge) | several evident typos (*avyāpareṣu*, *belāyām*, *utpādayitum*) and the story's end missing from the e-text | Bhāsuraka (P1) opens c02 |
| Hit 4, story 5 (the hermit and the mouse) | *muniā* for *muninā*; the closing verse number missing | X5 = Hit 2, story 2 |
| X5's original last sentence (*… gardabhaṃ laguḍena tādayāmāsa*) | *tādayāmāsa* for *tāḍayāmāsa* | X5 stops one sentence earlier |
| KedV 1.7 (the gaṇa verse) | *jmau* for *jbhau* | the gaṇas as a table |
| Hit 0.46 (guṇā guṇajñeṣu guṇā bhavanti …) | pāda d *samudram āsādya bhavanty upeyāḥ*: the sense needs *apeyāḥ* ("undrinkable") | lāṭānuprāsa shown on Mammaṭa's example (W5) and NŚ 1.19d (W6) |
| KedV 3.97 (mandākrāntā) | Kedāra's e-text reads *tād guru cet*, where syllable 16 must be heavy | the same verse in Sulhaṇa's text, KVrk 3.100 (*tādgurū cet*), printed instead (W20), credited as Kedāra's verse in Sulhaṇa's text; its unjoined visarga (*agaiḥ mbhau*) is a departure from full sandhi, noted, not a wrong word (§4.0.5 b) |
| RV 1.164.20 padapāṭha (the words of Śvet. 4.6) | `rvpp_01u.htm` 3044 reads *samanam* for *samānam* and *sasvajāteiti* | the padapāṭha shown on RV 3.62.10 instead; Śvet. 4.6 read with its AV witness (W3) |
| Rām 1.2.14 (mā niṣāda …) | not a wrong reading: the e-text's *śāśvatīṃ* agrees with *pratiṣṭhām* ("lasting standing, for years", `ram_01_u.htm` 290); the familiar reading, which the Kāvyamīmāṃsā's quotation also has (`rajkmimu.htm` 315, spelt *śāsvatīḥ*), is *śāśvatīḥ*, with *samāḥ*; the course does not choose between two readings in a metre lesson | named, not printed, for that stated reason; W4 (1.2.15–17), the text's own account of the śloka, read instead |
| Rājaśekhara KM, the line after W9 | *anyatāsthitasya* for *anyathā-* | W9 stops before it |
| Ragh 9.3 (the verse before W7) | *arthpatiṃ* | not used; W7 (9.4) checked clean |

### 4.0.7 Every metre and figure taught, with its definition and source

| Item | Status | Definition / formula (TRADITION, attributed) | Source line | Texts |
|---|---|---|---|---|

### 4.0.10 The scripts (every count in this plan is re-derivable)

All in `v5/work/` (copied at synthesis from the base design's work folder, then extended):
`v5tools.py` (IAST → Devanagari; Kedāra's weight rule) · `render.py` (joined Devanagari and
pause forms) · `scan5.py` (every scansion line of §5, verse by verse, with syllable-count and
cadence checks; `splice_scan.py` writes them into §5) · `metres.py` (gaṇa formulas) ·
`measure.py` with `texts_state.py` (token statuses and gloss ratios, by token) · `vipcount.py`
(the Gītā and Nala cadence counts of §4.4) · `mbhcount.py` (73,434) · `ramcount.py` (18,760) ·
`megh.py` (Meghadūta: 111 verses, 444 pādas) · `cover5.py` with `corpusmeasure.py`,
`dcsread.py`, `own_entry.pkl` (the DCS argument of §2.2) · `samasya.py` (the unread verses whose
fourth pāda the learner owns, W10–W11) · `ledgercheck.py` (headwords per lesson against §3 and
§4.0, the chain from 627, duplicates, ownership at entry) · `aptecheck.py` (rewritten at the repair: the c02.l02
procedure run over every word not owned — stems, nominatives, anusvāra spelt M, the root recovered from an -अय-
stem, upasarga + root — and the Apte headwords it reaches, §2.3, §5). The repair's scripts are in `work/rep/`:
`repair_state.py` (the re-coded statuses, from `texts_state.synth.py`), `figures.py` (every ratio and its
aggregates), `render5.py` (the §5 status lines, regenerated from the statuses and `aptecheck.py`), `wnew.py`
(Devanagari and scansion of W12–W24, R1–R2), `checks.py` (the arithmetic of §7.4: every ratio against `measure.py`
and against the words its lines list, the lesson figures, the syllable count of every scansion; `ledgercheck.py`
does the headwords and the chain); `scan5.py` and `splice_scan.py` print × at every pāda end since the repair. The
synthesis versions of the rewritten scripts are kept as `*.synth.py`.
---
### 4.1 Chapter 1 — सुभाषितसङ्ग्रहः · The Subhāṣita Canon

**Goals.** Read gnomic verse by performing the anvaya; pair relatives with their correlatives;
compare a verse in two or three books; resolve compounds inside verse; meet a named poet's
collection; close the 1.x subhāṣita set honestly.

**The frame.** The chapter reads the verses with which the Hitopadeśa itself opens its teaching:
the pair King Sudarśana hears (Hit 0.10–0.11; Volume 4 read the sentence "he heard a pair of
verses", N1), the verses that move him to call the assembly, and then the same verses found in
other books (Bhartṛhari, the Yājñavalkyasmṛti, the Narasiṃha Purāṇa), before one lesson on a
named poet. Its unseen exam reads one more Hitopadeśa verse and one of Bhartṛhari.

**Inventory (exact).**
- Texts: S1–S4, X1 (§5.1). Anuṣṭubh: S1, S2, S3a, S3c, X1a; indravajrā: S3b; upajāti: S3b2, S4a, S4b, X1b
  (scanned only in c04; recited by ear in c01).
- Forms: no new paradigm. Re-used and named in the verses: the optative of general statement
  (bhavet, Hit 0.30), the passive of state (dīpyate, śobhate), the -in adjectives (vairī,
  parivartin, kṣayiṇī), the f. -ī of u-adjectives (gurvī, laghvī; a Δ card with listed ✓ cells, c01.l04 §15,
  decision V5-D4).
- Collections named, each with its e-text header's edition statement (§4.0.5): Hitopadeśa; Bhartṛhari's
  Śatakatraya; the Yājñavalkyasmṛti; the Narasiṃha Purāṇa; Sternbach's Mahāsubhāṣitasaṃgraha (as
  a finding tool: its e-text holds the verses 1–9979 without its source apparatus).
- The 1.x subhāṣita set (pp.47–51) closed in c01.l05 (§4.0.3).

**Volume 4 §6 rows carried:** "Anvaya as a commentarial method in depth → v5.c01" (l01–l04, and
every later verse). **1.x assets carried:** the subhāṣita set and three śānti (pp.47–51), as the
ROADMAP row places them (§4.0.3).

**Exam shape (l05, 45 min).** A — recite V1 and write it joined from memory (5 min). B — X1a and X1b
(unseen, 34 words, 8 glossed in the margin, 1 in 4.2): pause form, anvaya with brackets,
translation (20 min). C — variant task: given Hit 0.30 and Yāj 1.351 side by side (the l03
pair, now with the course's notes removed), list every difference and say whether it changes the
grammar or the sense (5 min). D — the derivation spiral: "Derive, according to Pāṇini, नयति",
toolbox card (8 min). E — production: write four sentences of your own on the pattern yaḥ … saḥ
… and yathā … tathā …, joined with pause form (7 min). Pass: 70% overall, and every task at half its points.

#### v5.c01.l01 — अन्वयः · "The Pair of Verses the King Heard"

- **lesson_type:** reading. **Station 0:** v4.c06.l04 +1 day; v4.c06.l03 +1 week.
  **review_of:** v4.c06.l04 (X-a, X-b), v4.c03.l04 (N1: śloka-dvayaṃ śuśrāva), v3.c06.l04 (three
  verses in prose order), v3.c04.l04 (padaccheda).
- **The one teaching point:** the anvaya as a procedure the learner performs: the six moves of
  §1.1 principle 3, with the check "every word once; every bracket required".
- **Text: S1** (Hit 0.10–0.11, §5.1). Gloss ratio 4 of 23 (1 in 5.8) with the lesson's headwords;
  11 of 23 (1 in 2.1) without them.
- **Forms met:** दर्शकम् (agent in -अक, L: Volume 4's कारकः), प्रभुत्वम् / अविवेकिता (taught
  taddhitas on this lesson's headwords, L), एकैकम्, the dative of result अनर्थाय "for harm"
  (the dative of purpose, owned use since v3.c01.l03).
- **The worked anvaya** (S1a): move 2 finds no finite verb → (अस्ति) supplied; move 3 finds the
  subject शास्त्रम् by the neuter of its three predicates; move 5 joins यस्य … सः. S1b: move 2
  supplies (भवति); the four subjects take a singular predicate one by one (एकैकम्); किमु closes a
  sentence with no verb at all.
- **The tradition's name for the bracket (W1, TRADITION, display):** Śaṅkara, commenting on BG
  4.17, supplies a word in three clauses and says so: त्रिष्वप्यध्याहारः कर्तव्यः "in all three a word
  must be supplied (adhyāhāra)". The learner meets the term, not the commentary; it is not
  assessed.
- **Model sentences** (joined; pause form beneath):
  1. शास्त्रं सर्वस्य लोचनम्। — शास्त्रम् सर्वस्य लोचनम्। "Learning is everyone's eye."
  2. यस्य विवेको नास्ति सोऽन्ध एव। — यस्य विवेकः न अस्ति सः अन्धः एव। "Whoever has no
     discernment is simply blind."
  3. पितुः परोक्षे पुत्रः किं करोति? — पितुः परोक्षे पुत्रः किम् करोति? "What does the son do
     when his father is not there?"
  4. संशयेषु शास्त्रं पृच्छ। — संशयेषु शास्त्रम् पृच्छ। "In doubt, ask the śāstra."
  5. एको मूर्खो दुःखाय भवति, किमु शतम्! — एकः मूर्खः दुःखाय भवति, किम् उ शतम्! "One fool
     makes for grief — how much more a hundred!"
- **New headwords (8; 627 → 635):** संशयः saṃśayaḥ m. a "doubt" (S1a; Hit DCS frequent) ·
  परोक्ष parokṣa adj. -क्षा -क्षम् "out of sight; (loc.) in the absence of" (S1a; Volume 4's
  3.2.115 परोक्षे लिट् now a word the learner owns) · लोचनम् locanam n. a "eye" · अन्धः andhaḥ adj.
  -न्धा -न्धम् "blind" · यौवनम् yauvanam n. a "youth" · प्रभुः prabhuḥ m. u "lord, master" (Hit DCS
  12; prabhutvam L) · विवेकः vivekaḥ m. a "discernment" (avivekitā L) · किमु kimu indecl. "how much
  more / less" (MW s.v. kim; rule 29).
- **Previewed:** the metre of the two verses (heard and recited; from c01.l02 the printed
  recitation line marks each pāda end and syllables 5–8, ○, not assessed; scanned in c04.l01).
  **Deferred:** the agent noun in -अक as a taught process (◐: L on Volume 4's कारकः, 3.1.133).
- **Payoff:** In v4.c03.l04 (N1) the king "heard a pair of verses being recited by someone";
  today the learner reads the two verses, and the story of the Hitopadeśa begins where the king's
  worry begins.
- **Main risk:** translating the verse word by word in its own order. Fix: the anvaya is written
  first, every time; the translation is checked against the anvaya, not the verse.
- **§17:** the Hitopadeśa's frame told in English: the verses trouble the king, whose sons have
  no learning (TRADITION: the work's own story; no date).
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: the anvaya of S1b written from memory; +1 week (c01.l03): recite S1.

#### v5.c01.l02 — यत् तत् · "Who … He: the Relative Verse"

- **lesson_type:** reading. **Station 0:** c01.l01 +1 day; v4.c06.l04 +1 week. **review_of:**
  c01.l01, v3.c05.l02–l03 (the correlative grid, the relative sentence), v4.c05.l02 (पाठयति).
- **The one teaching point:** a relative clause in verse is joined to its correlative in the
  anvaya; where the verse leaves the correlative out, the learner supplies it in brackets (यः →
  (सः), येन → (तेन), यथा → तथा, यत्र → (तत्र)), and decides which clause comes first in prose.
- **Text: S2** (Hit 0.12, 0.37, 0.45; §5.1). Ratios: S2a 1 of 17; S2b 1 of 14 (vairī G); S2c 5
  of 10 (द्रव्यम् G: not owned, and no Volume 5 headword). Together 7 of 41 (1 in 5.9) with the headwords; 16 of 41
  without.
- **Forms met:** पाठितः (-त of the causative पाठयति, L; the causative kṛt forms are c02.l01's
  card), जातेन (-त of जायते), चक्षुषा (s-stem instrumental on this lesson's headword, Volume 4's
  -अस् table), दीप्यते (Ā class 4, G).
- **Model sentences:**
  1. यः शास्त्रं न पठति स धार्मिकोऽपि मूर्खः। — यः शास्त्रम् न पठति सः धार्मिकः अपि मूर्खः।
     "He who does not read the śāstra is a fool, righteous though he be."
  2. येन बालो न पाठितः स पिता वैरी। — येन बालः न पाठितः सः पिता वैरी। "The father by whom the child
     was not taught is an enemy." (the construction of Hit 0.37, S2b: येन … सः; वैरी glossed: vairam + -in)
  3. यथा हंसमध्ये बको न शोभते तथा सभायां मूर्खः। — यथा हंस-मध्ये बकः न शोभते तथा सभायाम्
     मूर्खः। "As a heron does not shine among swans, so a fool in an assembly."
  4. यत्र गिरिस्तत्र नदी। — यत्र गिरिः तत्र नदी। "Where there is a mountain there is a river."
  5. हीनैः सह मा वस। — हीनैः सह मा वस। "Do not live with the low."
- **New headwords (8; 635 → 643):** धार्मिकः dhārmikaḥ adj. -का -कम् "righteous" · चक्षुः
  cakṣuḥ n. (stem चक्षुस्) "eye" (rule 14; the -अस् Δ of Volume 4) · पीडा pīḍā f. ā "pain" ·
  केवलम् kevalam indecl. (adj. केवल) "only, merely" · हंसः haṃsaḥ m. a "swan, goose" (S2b; met
  again in E2, the Nala haṃsa) · बकः bakaḥ m. a "heron" (S2b; Hit DCS 31; P4's herons) · गिरिः
  giriḥ m. i "mountain" · हीनः hīnaḥ adj. -ना -नम् "deprived of; low" (a lexicalised -न participle
  of जहाति, rule 18; S2c, S3b).
- **Previewed:** upamā with यथा (S2b): the learner fills a four-column table in English (what is
  compared · with what · in what · by which word) — ○, the parts unnamed; named in c04.l04.
  **Deferred:** the -in adjective's full declension beyond nominatives (owned from Volume 4).
- **Payoff:** the owned relatives of Volume 3 (यः, येन, यथा, यत्र) turn out to be the hinges on
  which gnomic verse turns; the thread verse यस्य बुद्धिर्बलं तस्य (v3.c06.l04) is re-read as the
  model of the pattern.
- **Main risk:** pairing a relative with the wrong correlative across the half-verse (S2b: येन is
  the agent of पाठितः, and its correlatives are माता and पिता, not बालः). Fix: move 4 before move
  5: first find what each case form depends on.
- **§17:** the king asks the assembly for a teacher (English bridge; Hit 0, prose after 0.39).
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: three relatives supplied in three new sentences; +1 week (c01.l04):
  the anvaya of S2b without notes.

#### v5.c01.l03 — पाठभेदः · "One Verse, Many Books"

- **lesson_type:** reading. **Station 0:** c01.l02 +1 day; c01.l01 +1 week. **review_of:**
  c01.l01–l02, v3.c04.l04 ("editions vary"), v4.c06.l01 (the data file as one witness).
- **The one teaching point:** a subhāṣita travels: the same verse stands in several books, with
  variants. The learner sets two witnesses side by side, lists every difference by kind (the
  order of the halves; a word; word order; spelling), and says whether it changes the grammar, the
  sense, or nothing; then cites the verse by each book and number.
- **Texts: S3** (§5.1), three pairs, every witness a local e-text:
  - S3a Hit 0.15 / S3a2 Nītiśataka 1.32 (GRETIL numbering): the same two halves in reverse order;
  - S3b Hit 0.25 / S3b2 Narasiṃha Purāṇa 15.13: सामान्यम् / समानम्; अधिको विशेषो / अधिकं हि लोके;
  - S3c Hit 0.30 / S3c2 Yājñavalkyasmṛti 1.351: तथा / एवम्; न रथस्य / रथस्य न; सिद्ध्यति /
    सिध्यति. The course says plainly that the Hitopadeśa e-text's सिद्ध्यति is not the verb's
    spelling (the owned thread verse has सिध्यन्ति, Hit 0.36), and that the Yājñavalkya e-text's
    सिध्यति is (§5.1 S3c).
  Ratios: S3a 2 of 14; S3b 1 of 13; S3c 1 of 14 (with the headwords); 4 of 41 together.
- **Forms met:** the nominal sentence with a predicate noun of another gender (ज्ञानं … अधिको
  विशेषः: m. predicate, n. subject; the anvaya keeps both), the optative of general statement
  (भवेत्).
- **Model sentences:**
  1. अस्मिन्पुस्तके पाठभेदो नास्ति। — अस्मिन् पुस्तके पाठ-भेदः न अस्ति। "In this book there is
     no variant." (पाठभेदः display: पाठः + भेदः, the latter glossed)
  2. हितोपदेशे तथेति पठ्यते, याज्ञवल्क्य एवमिति। — हितोपदेशे "तथा" इति पठ्यते,
     याज्ञवल्क्ये "एवम्" इति। "The Hitopadeśa reads tathā, Yājñavalkya evam." (a quoted word is joined to the इति
     after it, STYLE_GUIDE §3.1: तथेति, एवमिति)
  3. आहारो निद्रा भयं च पशूनां नराणां च सामान्यम्। — आहारः निद्रा भयम् च पशूनाम् नराणाम् च
     सामान्यम्। "Food, sleep and fear are common to animals and to people."
  4. ज्ञानेन नरः पशोरधिकः। — ज्ञानेन नरः पशोः अधिकः। "By knowledge a man is more than an
     animal." (the ablative of comparison, owned use since v3.c06.l01: बलात् श्रेष्ठा)
  5. संसारे को न जायते? — संसारे कः न जायते? "In the world, who is not born?"
- **New headwords (8; 643 → 651):** वंशः vaṃśaḥ m. a "family line" (S3a's sense; X1a's "bamboo" is a new sense,
  glossed there, rule 27) · संसारः saṃsāraḥ m. a "the round of births; the world" · आहारः āhāraḥ m. a
  "food" (Hit DCS 35) · निद्रा nidrā f. ā "sleep" · सामान्यम् sāmānyam n. (adj. सामान्य) "what is
  common" · पशुः paśuḥ m. u "animal" (Hit DCS 14) · अधिकः adhikaḥ adj. -का -कम् "more, exceeding" ·
  विशेषः viśeṣaḥ m. a "distinction, what marks off".
- **Previewed:** metre as a test between variants (both readings of every pair are metrical, though some pādas
  differ in pattern: S3b2's pāda b is upendravajrā where S3b's is indravajrā; S3c and S3c2 differ in pādas b and c;
  c04.l02 shows a case where metre decides). **Deferred:** a critical apparatus (§6).
- **Payoff:** the learner's own thread verse उद्यमेन हि सिध्यन्ति … (Hit 0.36) is shown in its
  variant उद्यमेनैव कार्याणि सिध्यन्ति न मनोरथैः … विशन्ति वदने मृगाः (MSS 6890, W12, display, read
  not memorised): the verse the learner has carried since Volume 2 is one witness among several.
- **Main risk:** treating the variant the learner met first as "correct". Fix: the lesson's
  rule: the course prints what an e-text has, names its edition, and decides nothing it cannot
  check.
- **§17:** HISTORY-free: the course names the books as books; no dates (§7.3 Q4).
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: list the differences of a new pair (Hit 0.37 / Garuḍa Purāṇa 1.115.80, W13,
  `garup1_u.htm` 8485–8486, display: बालो … पाठितः / बाला … पाठिताः); +1 week (c01.l05): recite S1 and S3a.

#### v5.c01.l04 — भर्तृहरिः · "A Poet's Collection"

- **lesson_type:** reading. **Station 0:** c01.l03 +1 day; c01.l02 +1 week. **review_of:**
  c01.l01–l03, v4.c04.l04 (compounds as derivations), v3.c05.l04 (vigraha).
- **The one teaching point:** in verse, compounds carry most of the meaning; the anvaya writes
  each compound's vigraha in brackets beside it (move 5a), so that the prose order states every
  relation the compound hides.
- **Text: S4** (Nītiśataka 1.13, 1.60; §5.1). Ratios 1 of 21 and 2 of 14 with the headwords; 6
  and 5 without them. S4b is the respective construction: two subjects, two similes, in order
  (खल … पूर्वार्ध … आरम्भगुर्वी; सज्जन … परार्ध … वृद्धिमती) — shown as a reading note, named in
  c04.l04.
- **Forms met (Δ card with listed ✓ cells, §15; decision V5-D4, as Volume 4's V4-D6):** the f. of u-adjectives
  in -वी (गुर्वी, लघ्वी: Whitney §344b), the -इन् adjective's f. -इनी (क्षयिणी), -मत् f. -मती (वृद्धिमती).
  Production: these three f. forms ✓ on owned adjectives (गुरु, लघु, बलिन्, धनवत्); not the lesson's point.
- **Model sentences:**
  1. यस्य शीलं नास्ति स भुवि भारः। — यस्य शीलम् न अस्ति सः भुवि भारः। "Whoever has no good
     conduct is a burden on the earth."
  2. खलानां मैत्र्यारम्भगुर्वी। — खलानाम् मैत्री आरम्भ-गुर्वी। "The friendship of the wicked is
     heavy at the start." (vigraha: आरम्भे गुर्वी)
  3. मध्याह्ने छाया लघ्वी भवति। — मध्याह्ने छाया लघ्वी भवति। "At noon the shadow is short."
  4. तपसा विद्यया च मनुष्यः शोभते। — तपसा विद्यया च मनुष्यः शोभते। "A person shines by
     austerity and learning."
  5. गुरुः शिष्याय विद्याया भारं ददाति। — गुरुः शिष्याय विद्यायाः भारम् ददाति। "The teacher
     gives the pupil the weight of learning." (-आः before a voiced sound → -आ, v3.c04.l02)
- **New headwords (8; 651 → 659):** शीलम् śīlam n. a "conduct, character" · भूः bhūḥ f. (stem भू)
  "earth" (rule 14; loc. भुवि, the root-noun cell taught) · भारः bhāraḥ m. a "burden, weight" ·
  मनुष्यः manuṣyaḥ m. a "human being" (Hit DCS 10) · आरम्भः ārambhaḥ m. a "beginning" · छाया
  chāyā f. ā "shadow, shade" (Hit DCS 10) · मैत्री maitrī f. ī "friendship" · तपः tapaḥ n. (stem
  तपस्) "austerity" (rule 14).
- **Previewed:** the upajāti metre of both verses (recited by ear; c04.l03); S4b's छायेव: the four-column likeness table of c01.l02 again (○). **Deferred:** the
  respective construction as a figure (yathāsaṅkhya: Daṇḍin names it; not taught, §6).
- **Payoff:** the compound habits of Volume 4 (vigraha, sup-lopa) are what makes Bhartṛhari
  readable; खल (owned since v1.c03.l01, "wicked") and गुरु / लघु (owned) return with the senses
  "heavy" / "light" that c04 will need for syllables.
- **Main risk:** one translation for both halves of the respective construction. Fix: the anvaya
  is written as two sentences, one for each subject, before translating.
- **§17:** the Śatakatraya named as a collection of three "centuries" attributed to Bhartṛhari
  (TRADITION: the attribution as the e-text's header gives it; the e-text header says, in the inputter's own
  remark, that "there seem to be several extant editions with different numberings": quoted as his remark, not as
  FACT).
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: three compounds of S4 resolved in brackets; +1 week (c01.l05 →
  c02.l01, by §4.0.1): the anvaya of S4b.

#### v5.c01.l05 — प्रथमपरीक्षा · "Chapter 1 Exam"

- **lesson_type:** review. **review_of:** c01.l01–l04. **Shape:** §4.1 (45 min). Ledger stays 659.
- **Unseen text: X1** (Hit 0.23, Nītiśataka 1.41; §5.1): 8 of 34 words glossed in the margin,

- **Assembly (§15): the 1.x subhāṣita set closed** (ROADMAP row "Subhāṣita set + three śānti
  (pp.47–51) → v2.c06 (first three), v5.c01"; §4.0.3 gives each item):
  - BG 4.24 (MBh 6.26.24, R1) and the śānti सह नाववतु … (R2, as printed at the opening and close of the
    Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad in the edition its e-text follows, `svetugau.htm` lines 116–119,
    593–596) are read, recited, and parsed: करवावहै and विद्विषावहै are 1st-person dual Ā forms of the so-called
    imperative, whose first persons are, in Whitney's words, "a remnant of the old subjunctive" (FACT: Whitney
    §533b, §578); the second stands after मा: मा with a 1st-person form, an old subjunctive (cf. §579d, where the
    older language has a single such example); नौ is the enclitic dual of अहम् (8.1.20, D, read in c03.l04).
  - "गुरुर्ब्रह्मा …" is **not printed**: no text the project can open contains it (searched in the
    local GRETIL mirror); the 1.x book gave no locus.
  - The 1.x line "शान्तिः is chanted thrice for 3 types of suffering", quizzed as fact (p.50), is
    quoted as an **unsourced link**: the triad ādhyātmika / ādhibhautika / ādhidaivika is a classification of
    suffering found in the Yogasūtrabhāṣya on YS 1.31 (`patyogbu.htm` 243, *duḥkham ādhyātmikam ādhibhautikam
    ādhidaivikaṃ ca*; TRADITION, Teacher Notes only; key `yogasutrabhashya`; the e-text was "proof read" by its
    inputter, its header says), but no source the course opened ties it to the threefold śāntiḥ. In its place the
    learner reads the reason a commentator gives for the threefold utterance, **TRADITION, tier R** (W14):
    शान्तिः शान्तिः शान्तिरिति त्रिर्वचनं सर्वदोषोपशमनार्थम् "the threefold utterance 'śāntiḥ śāntiḥ śāntiḥ' is for
    the allaying of all faults" (Śaṅkara (attrib.) on Kaṭha 6.19, where the same śānti closes the Kaṭha,
    `samkaubh_u.htm` 796; key `kathopanishadbhashya`). Assessed only "According to Śaṅkara …"; the departure from
    IR §9 item 4 and the preservation map, which class the 1.x explanation as correct, is put to the editor (§7.3
    Q16).
- **Payoff:** the first unseen verses read without the course's anvaya; the 1.x set accounted for
  item by item.
- **S0 → next:** +1 day (c02.l01): X1b's anvaya re-written; +1 week (c02.l02): recite S1.
