### 4.2 Chapter 2 — पञ्चतन्त्रम् · Panchatantra

**Goals.** Read continuous narrative of 80–90 words with its inset verse; follow the frame-story
mechanics; find any word in Apte from the form on the page; read every past form the narrators
use; make productive the causative's kṛt forms, the -अनीय gerundive and the genitive absolute;
close the Volume 3–4 serial (the monkey and the crocodile) and the Vīravara thread.

**Inventory (exact).**
- Texts: P1–P4, X2 (§5.2). P1 is the story the Volume 3 verse यस्य बुद्धिर्बलं तस्य (Panc 1.237,
  v3.c06.l04) introduces; P2 is the end of the Pañcatantra 4 frame, after the trick Volume 4 read
  (N7); P3 is the unread middle of the Vīravara story (Hit 3, story 8); P4 is a story, abridged (the crab's part
  told in English bridges), with its verse; X2 is the next story of book 4.
- Skill ✓: the lookup procedure (c02.l02), used in every later lesson's gloss line.
- Forms ✓: the causative's -इत, -इत्वा / -य, passive (c02.l01 card, decision V5-D1); -अनीय
  (c02.l03; 3.1.96 Q); the laṅ passive (c02.l03 §15, Δ card, decision V5-D5); the genitive absolute (c02.l04;
  2.3.38 Q).
  The periphrastic perfect is recognised and looked up here (c02.l02) and made ✓ in the Nala
  reading (c03.l01, V5-D2).
- Forms ◐ on cards: the aorist (agāt; agamat, abhūt from Volume 4's c03.l05 card; 3.2.110 K), the benedictive,
  conditional and periphrastic future (names and one form each), the desiderative (बुभुक्षितः, Panc 4.16, W16, in
  §17) and the denominative (P2's आकर्ण्य, of the owned आकर्णयति), the -य gerundive (3.1.97 D, 3.1.110 D,
  3.1.124 D).

**Volume 4 §6 rows carried:** "The Pañcatantra 4 frame beyond the trick; the full Vīravara story →
v5.c02" (l02; l03); "Pañcatantra 1, the lion Bhāsuraka and the hare → v5.c02, as its opening text"
(l01); "Production of the aorist … : reading only" (l02 card); "The voice forms beyond the laṅ/loṭ
passive and the present-system causative" (l01, l03); "The -अनीय and -य gerundives" (l03);
"The genitive absolute in production" (l04); "Desiderative, intensive, denominative → readings"
(l02 card); "The class-3 and athematic Ā cells (ब्रूते)" (ब्रूते throughout, ◐; the class-3 present beyond ददाति not planned in Volume 5, §4.0.2).

**1.x assets carried and corrected:** the Pañcatantra card (p.56): the five books' names are right
and are kept; "Written ~300 BCE — oldest fable collection!" is quoted and withdrawn (IR-030: no date prints,
because no confirmed source gives one, `olivelle1997` not confirmed, §4.0.9; "oldest" withdrawn); "by Vishnu
Sharma ✓ CORRECT" becomes TRADITION, "attributed to Viṣṇuśarman", and "Five strategies/principles ✓" the course's
labelled analysis of the title (§4.0.4); "विद्वान् सर्वत्र पूज्यते" is verified as a
Pañcatantra verse (Panc 2.58 in the GRETIL numbering, `vispancu.htm` 2976–2977, W15) and kept, with
its first half; the four other "Panchatantra morals" of p.57 (सङ्घे शक्तिः, धैर्येण सर्वं सिध्यति,
अपरीक्षितं न कुर्यात्, विद्या धनं सर्वधनप्रधानम्) were not found in the local e-texts and are not
printed (§4.0.4).

**Exam shape (l05, 45 min).** A — X2 (unseen, 69 words, 13 not owned: 8 glossed in the margin, 5
looked up in a printed Apte extract with 5 decoys, by the c02.l02 procedure): eight questions;
parse every past form and every participle, naming the lakāra or formation of each (20 min). B —
transformations: 5 causative sentences turned into the causative -त (मया गृहं दर्शितम्); the
headword, gender or class and sense of 3 forms of X2 written from the extract (8 min).
C — gerundives: 6 sentences with -तव्य or -अनीय (5 min). D — the derivation spiral: "Derive,
according to Pāṇini, कृतम्" (7 min). E — production: five separate sentences of the learner's own, each using
one form the chapter made productive (a causative -त, a causative -य, the laṅ passive, an -अनीय gerundive, a
genitive absolute), joined with pause form (5 min); no connected composition before c05.l02 teaches it
(principle 11), so the end of X2's story waits for c05.l02's production. Pass: 70% overall, and every task at half
its points.

#### v5.c02.l01 — कथामुखम् · "The Story Inside the Story"

- **lesson_type:** reading. **Station 0:** c01.l05 +1 day; c01.l04 +1 week. **review_of:**
  v3.c06.l04 (Panc 1.237), v4.c05.l02 (causative), v4.c04.l01–l02 (-त, absolutive).
- **The one teaching point:** how a Pañcatantra story is told: a speaker cites a verse as a
  thesis (उक्तं च / यतः), the listener asks कथमेतत्?, the speaker tells the story, and closes it
  with अतोऽहं ब्रवीमि and the verse's first words (ring composition). The learner marks the frame
  of P1 and of two more framed stories from earlier volumes (Hit 1.2 → the doves; Panc 4.13 → the
  monkey's escape).
- **Card, decision V5-D1 (listed ✓ cells, as Volume 4's V4-D6):** the causative's kṛt forms:
  -इत (दर्शितः, पाठितः, कारितः), -इत्वा after a simple causative (दर्शयित्वा), -य after a
  prefix: the -अय- is dropped before ल्यप् (प्रकोप्य, आनन्द्य, प्रबोध्य: 6.4.51 णेरनिटि, TN, as in Volume 4's
  आलोच्य), except after a light syllable, where it stays as -अय् (प्रणमय्य: 6.4.56 ल्यपि लघुपूर्वात्, TN; the
  Kāśikā, `jvkasipu.htm` 64370); the passive (कार्यते, दर्श्यते). Production ✓ on owned causatives (every cell
  re-checked against the corrected rule: प्रकोप्य, आनन्द्य and प्रबोध्य have a heavy root syllable; of the three only प्रबोध्य, on the owned बोधयति, is a ✓ cell: प्रकोप्य and आनन्द्य are on verbs not owned, read, not produced).
- **Text: P1** (Panc 1, story 8, with its frame: Damanaka's उक्तं च and the verse Panc 1.237, Karaṭaka's कथमेतत्?,
  and स आह; the opening and the end, the middle told in an English bridge; §5.2). 10 of 107 words glossed
  (1 in 10.7); 18 of 107 without the lesson's headwords (1 in 5.9). The verse that introduces the story (Panc 1.237)
  is the owned Volume 3 verse, now read in its place as the thesis the story proves.
- **Forms met:** procuḥ, mumoca, upararāma (perfect 3rd persons, ✓ Volume 4); vyāpādayan (-अत् of
  the owned व्यापादयति); nipātitaḥ (causative -त, this lesson's card); ānandya (causative -य, this lesson's card,
  on a verb not owned: glossed); praśasyamānaḥ (passive -यमान, G).
- **Model sentences:**
  1. सिंहेन कूपे स्वं प्रतिबिम्बं दृष्टम्। — सिंहेन कूपे स्वम् प्रतिबिम्बम् दृष्टम्। "The lion saw
     his own reflection in the well."
  2. शशकेन सिंहः कूपं दर्शितः। — शशकेन सिंहः कूपम् दर्शितः। "The lion was shown the well by the
     hare." (the causative's -त with two objects: the causee becomes the subject, Volume 4's
     1.4.52 pattern)
  3. सिंहस्य वधं सकलान्मृगान्प्रबोध्य शशको वने न्यवसत्। — सिंहस्य वधम् सकलान् मृगान् प्रबोध्य शशकः वने
     न्यवसत्। "Having told all the animals of the lion's killing, the hare lived in the forest." (प्रबोध्य: the
     owned बोधयति with प्र, -य, the -अय- dropped after a heavy syllable; two objects, as with Volume 4's 1.4.52)
  4. नित्यमेको मृगः सिंहस्य तृप्त्यै गच्छति। — नित्यम् एकः मृगः सिंहस्य तृप्त्यै गच्छति।
     "One animal goes regularly for the lion's satisfaction."
  5. सिंहनादं श्रुत्वा मृगाः प्राणान्न त्यजन्ति। — सिंह-नादम् श्रुत्वा मृगाः प्राणान् न त्यजन्ति।
     "Hearing the lion's roar, the animals do not lose their lives."
- **New headwords (8; 659 → 667):** प्राणः prāṇaḥ m. a (usually pl.) "breath; life" (Hit DCS 24)
  · तृप्तिः tṛptiḥ f. i "satisfaction" · प्रतिबिम्बम् pratibimbam n. a "reflection" · नादः nādaḥ
  m. a "roar, sound" · हृष्टः hṛṣṭaḥ adj. -ष्टा -ष्टम् "glad" (lexicalised -त, rule 28) · सकलः
  sakalaḥ adj. -ला -लम् "all, whole" · अनेकः anekaḥ adj. -का -कम् "many" (rule 10: a lexicalised
  nañ compound) · वधः vadhaḥ m. a "killing".
- **Previewed:** the aorist (c02.l02 card). **Deferred:** the -यमान participle as a
  formation (L only after c02.l03's §15 line: passive stem + -मान).
- **Payoff:** Volume 3 read the verse "In the forest a lion, mad with pride, was brought down by a
  hare"; today the learner reads how. The Volume 4 headword व्यापादयति meets its own story.
- **Main risk:** losing the frame (who is speaking to whom: Damanaka tells Karaṭaka). Fix: a
  speaker column in the reading worksheet, filled at every उक्तं च, आह and इति.
- **§17:** the 1.x "~300 BCE — oldest fable collection!" quoted and withdrawn in the open: no date prints, because
  the course has no confirmed source for one (`olivelle1997` not confirmed, §4.0.9), and "oldest" is a claim of rank
  without a method; the work is "attributed to Viṣṇuśarman" (TRADITION, the e-text header's "Visnusarma"), and its
  title is analysed, labelled the course's, as पञ्च + तन्त्र, "five books" (the five tantras of §4.0.4).
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: five causative -त sentences; +1 week (c02.l03): retell P1 in six
  sentences.

#### v5.c02.l02 — कोशः · "Reading with Apte"

- **lesson_type:** vocabulary. **Station 0:** c02.l01 +1 day; c01.l05 +1 week. **review_of:**
  v4.c03.l04 (perfect), v3.c03.l03 (imperfect), v4.c04.l01 (-त, -तवत्), v4.c03.l05 (the card
  of the other lakāras), v4.c05.l05 (N7, the trick), v4.c02.l01 (prakṛti + pratyaya: the stem
  a dictionary lists).
- **The one teaching point:** the way from a form on the page to its Apte entry, in six steps
  (from the design "skill first"): (1) peel the ending with the owned tables backwards (a noun to
  its stem, a verb to its root); (2) split a compound, last member first; (3) take off an
  upasarga and look under the root, then for the upasarga's sub-entry; (4) reduce a kṛt or
  taddhita form, or a past form, to its base; (5) read the entry's first senses and choose the
  one the sentence needs, checking it back in the sentence; (6) write headword, gender or class,
  and sense in the gloss line. Apte gives vowel-stem nouns in the nominative (गृहम्, मतिः, रजकः) and
  consonant-stem nouns as stems (राजन्, मनस्), adjectives as stems (निर्वृत, घातक); verbs stand under the root, and
  many prefixed verbs have their own headword (आसद्, निकृत्); a nasal before a consonant is spelt with anusvāra
  (आनंदः for आनन्दः), so a word not found is tried so. Step (4) is practised on P2's narrative pasts, which the learner
  sorts into six — imperfect (augment off), perfect (reduplication off), periphrastic perfect
  (-आम् + आस off, then the -अय- stem to its root), aorist (augment off, no present-stem sign:
  the card), -तवत् and -त (suffix off) — and translates alike. From this lesson every gloss line
  is split "look up" / "glossed" (§1.4.7).
- **Card (no production target): the aorist and the rare lakāras.** अगात् (P2), and अभूत्, अगमत् from Volume 4's
  c03.l05 card (no seen text of this volume prints them before c03.l02's अगमन्): an augment, no present-stem sign,
  and the secondary endings; 3.2.110 लुङ् (K). The
  benedictive, conditional and periphrastic future, one form each (भूयात्, अभविष्यत्, भविता: the
  Volume 4 √भू table). The secondary conjugations named on the same card, each where a text prints one: the
  desiderative बुभुक्षितः (W16, §17) and the denominative आकर्ण्य (P2: the owned आकर्णयति is a denominative of
  कर्णः, Volume 4 rule 17). FACT beside it: Whitney §826 counts the aorist "only twenty-one times in the
  Nala, eight in the Hitopadeśa … six each in the Bhagavad-Gītā and śakuntalā" (the Volume 4 DCS
  count: nine tokens in the Hitopadeśa).
- **Recognised, not yet produced:** चिन्तयामास (P2), the periphrastic perfect: recognised by its
  -आम् and looked up under चिन्त्; it becomes ✓ in c03.l01 (decision V5-D2), where the Nala text
  uses it.
- **Text: P2** (Pañcatantra 4, the end of the frame, §5.2), with its verse Panc 4.14 (anuṣṭubh).
  9 of 84 not owned (1 in 9.3); 21 of 84 without the lesson's headwords (1 in 4.0). **After
  Apte:** 8 of the 9 are reached by the procedure (the long bahuvrīhi member by member, चङ्क्रमणम्, भर्त्स्, the
  आनन्द of सानन्दम् spelt आनंदः, रुह् or आरूढ, वृत्, आसद्, घातक); अर्पय stays glossed (its -प्- hides the root ऋ):
  **1 of 84** (`work/aptecheck.py`, rewritten at the repair; निकृन्तति is now L, the owned कृन्तति with नि).
- **FACT, a travelling verse:** Panc 4.14 (न विश्वसेदविश्वस्ते …) stands word for word as Panc 2.44
  in the same e-text (`vispancu.htm` 2920–2921 and 5295–5296): one book uses one verse in two
  stories (from the design "tradition first").
- **Forms met:** agāt (aorist, card), cintayāmāsa (recognised), āsāditavān (-तवत्), ārūḍhaḥ
  (-त, looked up under रुह्), labdhāḥ (-त), uktvā, nivartya, vihasya (absolutives), gamyatām,
  āgantavyam (passive imperative, -तव्य).
- **Model sentences:**
  1. वानरस्तीरं प्राप्य चिन्तयामास। — वानरः तीरम् प्राप्य चिन्तयामास। "The monkey reached the
     shore and thought." (read, not produced: c03.l01)
  2. मकरो जम्बूतलमगात्। — मकरः जम्बू-तलम् अगात्। "The crocodile went to the foot of the
     rose-apple." (read, not produced)
  3. अगादिति रूपम्, "इ" इति धातुः। — "अगात्" इति रूपम्, "इ" इति धातुः। "'Agāt' is the form;
     'i' is the root." (a quoted form is joined to its इति; a quoted sound is left unjoined, STYLE_GUIDE §3.1)
  4. अहो! किं कस्यचिद्धृदयद्वयं भवति? — अहो! किम् कस्यचित् हृदय-द्वयम् भवति? "Ha! Does anyone
     have two hearts?"
  5. न विश्वसेदविश्वस्ते। — न विश्वसेत् अविश्वस्ते। "One should not trust the untrustworthy."
  6. भूयोऽपि तस्मिन्न विश्वसेत्। — भूयः अपि तस्मिन् न विश्वसेत्। "One should not trust him again."
- **Production:** (a) twelve forms of P2 looked up by the six steps, headword, gender or class and
  sense written (K1: the key gives the Apte headword and page column); (b) Panc 2.58 (W15, §5.6:
  the 1.x "moral", now with its locus) glossed alone with Apte and put in prose order (K1); (c)
  six sentences of the learner's own with अहो, अथवा, भूयः, आशु, धिक्, each joined with its pause
  form (K2, K5).
- **New headwords (8; 667 → 675):** विश्वसिति viśvasiti "trusts" (वि + श्वस्, class 2; the epic and
  the Pañcatantra also inflect it in class 1, as P2's optative विश्वसेत् shows — both in MW; the
  owned विश्वासः is its noun) · अहो aho indecl. "ah! oh!" · अथवा athavā indecl. "or rather" ·
  भूयः bhūyaḥ indecl. "again; further" · आशु āśu indecl. "quickly" · अधस्तात् adhastāt indecl. (+
  gen.) "below" · धिक् dhik indecl. "shame! fie!" · उत्पन्नः utpannaḥ adj. -न्ना -न्नम् "arisen,
  born" (lexicalised -न, rule 28).
- **Previewed:** the periphrastic perfect's production (c03.l01). **Deferred:** aorist production
  (not planned, §6).
- **Payoff:** the serial that began in v3.c06.l03 ends in the original's own words: "Does anyone
  have two hearts?" And the consonant stems of Volume 4 (राजन्, मनस्, the aṅga) turn out to be what a
  dictionary lists for them.
- **Main risk:** taking the first sense in Apte, and reading the aorist as a present (अगात् ≈
  "goes"). Fix: step (5) — every looked-up word is checked back in its sentence; the sorting
  table, augment first.
- **§17:** the next story, Gaṅgadatta the frog, is introduced by the verse Panc 4.16
  (W16, §5.6: बुभुक्षितः किं न करोति पापम् …), read with the desiderative बुभुक्षितः glossed "hungry
  (wishing to eat)" (◐ card) — the upajāti the learner will scan in c04.l03.
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: ten forms looked up again, timed; +1 week (c02.l04): sort ten past forms
  of X-b and P1 with their headwords.

#### v5.c02.l03 — कृत्याः · "What Must Be Done"

- **lesson_type:** reading. **Station 0:** c02.l02 +1 day; c02.l01 +1 week. **review_of:**
  v4.c05.l01 (-तव्य; the gerundive card), v4.c03.l06 (N8, Vīravara), v4.c06.l04 (X-a).
- **The one teaching point:** the gerundive family: -तव्य (owned ✓), -अनीय (✓ now: guṇa where
  -तृ has it + -अनीय: करणीयम्, दर्शनीयः, स्मरणीयः; P3's अनुकम्पनीयः is the same formation on a verb not owned,
  glossed), and -य (◐, parse with the gloss line, three affixes: कार्यम् (ṇyat, 3.1.124 ऋहलोर्ण्यत्, D: vṛddhi);
  दृश्यः, P3's अदृश्या (kyap, 3.1.110 ऋदुपधाच्चाकॢपिचृतेः, D: after a root with penultimate ṛ, the k-marker blocking
  guṇa; the Kāśikā, `jvkasipu.htm` 15925); -य after a vowel (3.1.97 अचो यत्, D: देयम्)); 3.1.96 तव्यत्तव्यानीयरः
  (Q: "tavyat, tavya and anīyar"). The agent is in the instrumental, as with -तव्य.
- **§15, Δ card with listed ✓ cells (decision V5-D5, as Volume 4's V4-D6; no new rule; not the lesson's
  point):** the laṅ passive — augment + passive stem + Ā secondary endings (अदृश्यत, अक्रियत): the imperfect of
  T13, ✓ on owned verbs. And the passive -यमान participle (प्रशस्यमानः, P1) as the passive stem + -मान (◐).
- **Text: P3** (Hit 3, story 8: Lakṣmī's speech, the offering, the king's verse Hit 3.103 and his
  prayer; §5.2). 13 of 85 glossed (1 in 6.5); 24 of 85 without the headwords (1 in 3.5). Names
  excluded: 7 tokens.
- **Teacher Notes:** 8.1.24 न चवाहाहैवयुक्ते, on the king's words किं मे राज्येन? जीवितेन वा मम किं
  प्रयोजनम्?: the enclitic मे in the first question, the full मम after वा in the second (the
  Volume 4 §4.0.6 row: "8.1.24 in the Teacher Notes of the first Volume 5 reading that needs it").
- **Model sentences:**
  1. अयं राजा स्मरणीयः। — अयम् राजा स्मरणीयः। "This king is to be remembered." (स्मरति, owned: स्मर् + -अनीय)
  2. देव्योपहारो गृह्यताम्। — देव्या उपहारः गृह्यताम्। "Let the offering be accepted by the
     goddess."
  3. सेवकेन स्वामिनो राज्यं रक्षणीयम्। — सेवकेन स्वामिनः राज्यम् रक्षणीयम्। "The servant must
     protect his master's kingdom."
  4. लक्ष्मीरदृश्यत न पुनः। — लक्ष्मीः अदृश्यत न पुनः। "Lakṣmī was not seen again." (laṅ passive)
  5. चिरं जीव। — चिरम् जीव। "Live long."
  6. प्रसीद, देवि। — प्रसीद, देवि। "Be gracious, goddess."
- **New headwords (8; 675 → 683):** लक्ष्मीः lakṣmīḥ f. ī "fortune; Lakṣmī" (Hit DCS 12; one of
  "a very few ī-stems" that keep -ः in the nominative, Whitney §363a; the nominative cell only) · उपहारः upahāraḥ m. a
  "offering" · शिरः śiraḥ n. (stem शिरस्) "head" (Hit DCS 10) · प्रसीदति prasīdati "is gracious;
  becomes clear" (class 1/6, प्र + सद्) · सदृशः sadṛśaḥ adj. -शी -शम् "like, equal to" · राज्यम्
  rājyam n. a "kingdom" (Hit DCS 29) · जीवितम् jīvitam n. a "life" (Hit DCS 15) · चिरम् ciram
  indecl. "long, for a long time" (Hit DCS 11; चिरात् L).
- **Previewed:** the cvi compound उपहारीकरोषि "you make into an offering" (◐ with a gloss; Volume 4's
  gati card). **Deferred:** the -य gerundive's formation (◐: §6).
- **Payoff:** the Vīravara thread, met at the palace door (N6), at the salary (N8) and at its end
  (X-a), is read whole: X-a's "she looked at me and vanished" (अदृश्याभवत्) is now a gerundive the
  learner can parse.
- **Main risk:** the content (a father sacrifices his son). Fix: Teacher Notes prepare it as the
  Hitopadeśa's own exemplum of loyalty, read in the frame the work gives (the king of the geese
  tells it); the goddess restores all. The course says what the text says and does not endorse it.
- **§17:** the Hitopadeśa's book 3 (Vigraha) named as the war book (the work's own section titles,
  FACT about the e-text's divisions).
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: six -अनीय forms of owned verbs; +1 week (c02.l05): the laṅ passive of
  five owned verbs (the V5-D5 cells).

#### v5.c02.l04 — षष्ठी चानादरे · "While the Heron Looked On"

- **lesson_type:** reading. **Station 0:** c02.l03 +1 day; c02.l02 +1 week. **review_of:**
  v4.c05.l04 (the locative absolute; 2.3.37 Q, 2.3.38 D), v4.c05.l03 (T11, the -अत् cells).
- **The one teaching point:** the genitive absolute: a participle and its noun in the genitive,
  "while X was Y-ing / though X …", usually in disregard of the person: 2.3.38 षष्ठी चानादरे (Q,
  re-read from Volume 4's D: "the genitive too, in the sense of disregard", with यस्य च भावेन
  भावलक्षणम् carried from 2.3.37). P4's verse: पश्यतो बकमूर्खस्य नकुलैर्भक्षिताः सुताः "while the
  foolish heron looked on, his young were eaten by mongooses". Production ✓ (a Δ of the owned
  locative absolute).
- **Text: P4** (Panc 1, story 21, the story abridged, with the second half of its verse 1.439; §5.2): the crab
  (कुलीरकः, the herons' old enemy) is introduced in lines the course does not print (2490 end, 2493–2496; 2490 also has the slip
  *bāṣpa-pūraita*), so two English bridges name him where the cuts fall, before his printed advice (आह च — माम …). The
  e-text's first half of the verse has nine syllables in its second quarter (तथापायमपि
  चिन्तयेत्), so it is not printed (principle 2; §4.0.5 c). 11 of 83 glossed (1 in 7.5); 20 of 83
  without the headwords (1 in 4.2). The e-text heads the story with the title of the next one
  (*jīrṇadhana-nāma-vaṇik-putra-kathā*); the course gives no title and says why in Teacher Notes.
- **Model sentences:**
  1. पश्यतो बकस्य सर्पः सुतानभक्षयत्। — पश्यतः बकस्य सर्पः सुतान् अभक्षयत्। "While the heron
     looked on, the snake ate his young."
  2. रुदत्या मातुः पुत्रो वनं गतः। — रुदत्याः मातुः पुत्रः वनम् गतः। "Though his mother wept, the son went to the forest."
  3. नकुलो बिलात्सर्पकोटरं गत्वा सर्पमहन्। — नकुलः बिलात् सर्प-कोटरम् गत्वा सर्पम् अहन्। "The
     mongoose went from its burrow to the snake's hollow and killed the snake."
  4. उपायं चिन्तयन्प्राज्ञोऽपायमपि चिन्तयेत्। — उपायम् चिन्तयन् प्राज्ञः अपायम् अपि चिन्तयेत्।
     "A wise man, thinking of a plan, should think of the harm too." (the course's sentence, not
     the e-text's verse)
  5. मत्स्यमांसं नकुलाय रोचते। — मत्स्य-मांसम् नकुलाय रोचते। "The mongoose likes fish."
  6. पश्यतां जनानां सोऽहसत्। — पश्यताम् जनानाम् सः अहसत्। "While the people looked on, he laughed." (no noun
     left in the sentence could take जनानाम्: the absolute alone)
- **New headwords (8; 683 → 691):** अपायः apāyaḥ m. a "harm, danger" · प्राज्ञः prājñaḥ adj. -ज्ञा
  -ज्ञम् "wise" · नकुलः nakulaḥ m. a "mongoose" · सुतः sutaḥ m. a "son" (f. सुता L) · बिलम् bilam n.
  a "hole, burrow" · मत्स्यः matsyaḥ m. a "fish" (Hit DCS 15) · कोटरम् koṭaram n. a "hollow (of a
  tree)" (glossed in Volume 4's N7) · मांसम् māṃsam n. a "flesh, meat" (Hit DCS 16).
- **Previewed:** none. **Deferred:** nothing; the genitive absolute is complete.
- **Payoff:** Volume 4 taught the absolute that sets a scene (सूर्ये उदिते); this one sets it
  against someone. The heron's grief (रोदिमि) uses Volume 4's रोदिति.
- **Main risk:** reading every genitive participle as an absolute, or none. Fix: the test: a genitive noun +
  participle is an absolute when it states a circumstance in spite of which the main action happens (2.3.38 षष्ठी
  चानादरे, "in disregard"); where a noun in the sentence could also take the genitive as a possessive (models 1 and
  2: सुतान्, पुत्रः; P4's verse: सुताः), both readings are possible and the sense decides; model 6 has no such noun.
  The counter-example comes in c03.l02 (E2, 3.50.18: वने विचरतां तेषामेकं जग्राह पक्षिणम् — एकम् takes तेषाम्,
  "one of them": partitive, and the sense decides against the absolute; from the design "tradition first").
- **§17:** none (the story is itself a story in a story: Pāpabuddhi's case, told by the king's
  men; English bridge).
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: five genitive absolutes; +1 week (c03.l01): retell P4 in five sentences.

#### v5.c02.l05 — द्वितीयपरीक्षा · "Chapter 2 Exam"

- **lesson_type:** review. **review_of:** c02.l01–l04, v4.c04.l05. **Shape:** §4.2 (45 min).
  Ledger stays 691.
- **Unseen text: X2** (Panc 4, story 1, Gaṅgadatta and the snake; §5.2): the story's first sentence names the
  frog-king Gaṅgadatta (`vispancu.htm` 5323), so that the questions on who did what have their subject. 13 of 69 not
  owned (1 in 5.3): 8 glossed in the margin (1 in 8.6), 5 looked up in a printed Apte extract with 5 decoys (उच्छेदम्,
  स्वजातीयः, सर्पवाणी, सन्धानम्, मर्त्यलोके; all reached by the procedure, `work/aptecheck.py`; of the 8 glossed, the
  procedure would also reach 5, and the exam keeps the lookup to five). No
  chapter headword was chosen for X2; भूयः and बिलम् (c02.l02, l04) occur in it (three tokens):
  without them 16 of 69 (1 in 4.3). X2 is the next story of Pañcatantra 4, read in no volume.
- **Payoff:** a new story read alone, with a frog who argues in verses like Damanaka.
- **S0 → next:** +1 day (c03.l01): X2's last sentence translated again; +1 week (c03.l02): the
  sorting of past forms in X2.
