### 4.3 Chapter 3 — महाकाव्यम् उपनिषदश्च · Epics & Upaniṣads

**Scope (binding: ROADMAP note of 2026-11-10; Volume 4 plan §6 and decision Q18).** Five lessons:
the Nala text, BG 2.47, Bṛhadāraṇyaka 1.3.28, the perfect forms met in that reading, and one
TRADITION card for accent and the padapāṭha, read on an Upaniṣad line (the card reads the accents on the Upaniṣad
verse Śvet. 4.6 in its accented AV witness and shows the padapāṭha on RV 3.62.10: a departure from the note, put to
the editor, §7.3 Q11). No Mahābhāṣya, no
Paribhāṣenduśekhara, no other paribhāṣā; the rare lakāras by reading only.

**Goals.** Read epic ślokas as narrative; make the Ā perfect productive in the 3rd persons and
read every perfect form the Nala text uses; read BG 2.47 word by word and tell the two prohibitive
constructions apart; read an Upaniṣad passage that explains its own mantra, and tell the enclitic
मा "me" from the prohibitive मा; meet accent and the padapāṭha once, as the tradition states them.

**Inventory (exact).**
- Texts: E1, E2 (MBh 3.50, the opening of the Nala story; 3.50.11a was read in v4.c05.l04), G1
  (BG 2.47–48 = MBh 6.24.47–48), U1 (Bṛhad. 1.3.28, the mantra and the Brāhmaṇa's gloss, 394–400), X3
  (MBh 3.51.5–7, 9). All from the critical edition's e-text (`mbh_03_u.htm`, `mbh_06_u.htm`) and
  the Kāṇva Bṛhadāraṇyaka (`brup___u.htm`; header: "checked against the ed. by V.P. Limaye and
  R.D. Vadekar (Eighteen Principal Upanisads, vol. 1, Poona 1958)").
- Perfect forms read (E1, E2, X3, P1–P3): 3sg P ददौ, प्राप, ददर्श, जग्राह,
  व्याजहार, उत्ससर्ज, मुमोच, शुश्राव (owned), बभूव; 3pl P निपेतुः, प्रोचुः, अभिजग्मुः, प्रशशंसुः
  (3.50.15, display); periphrastic तोषयाम् आस, चिन्तयाम् आस; Ā 3sg उपचक्रमे.
- Sūtras: 3.4.81 (Q), 3.3.175 (Q), 6.4.74 (Q), 8.1.23 (Q), 8.1.20 (D), and on the accent card
  1.2.29, 1.2.30, 1.2.31, 8.1.18 (K); 3.1.35 (D) and 3.1.40 (Q) on the c03.l01 card, 3.4.81 (Q)
  on the c03.l02 card. §4.0.8.

**Volume 4 §6 rows carried:** "Accent … ; the Ṛgveda padapāṭha → v5.c03: one TRADITION card"
(l04); "1st and 2nd persons of the perfect; the Ā perfect; the periphrastic perfect → v5.c03, met
in the epic reading, not a lesson of their own" (l01: the periphrastic perfect on a card, V5-D2;
l02: the Ā perfect on a card, V5-D3; each lesson's teaching point is a reading skill, so neither
form is a lesson's point; the 1st and 2nd persons stay ◐); "BG 2.47 word by word, Bṛhad.
1.3.28 → v5.c03" (l03, l04); "The Nala opening as a running text" (l01–l02); the Teacher-Notes
sūtra 8.1.18 of the Volume 4 plan §4.0.6 ("on the v5.c03 accent card") (l04).
**1.x assets carried:** BG 2.47 word-by-word (p.66) → l03; Bṛhad. 1.3.28 (p.67) → l04; the epic
cards (pp.57–58), corrected (§4.0.4).

**Exam shape (l05, 45 min).** A — recite G1 and U1; write BG 2.47 joined from memory with its
anvaya; then (4 points) translate BG 2.47 word by word from that anvaya, and give the analysis of कर्मफलहेतुः
"according to Śaṅkara" (कर्मफलप्राप्तेर्हेतुः) and the course's bahuvrīhi (कर्मफलं हेतुर्यस्य सः) (9 min). B — X3
(unseen, 37 words, names excluded; 9 words not owned, of 8 lemmas, printed in an
alphabetical **lemma glossary** (dictionary forms) with 8 decoys, no marginal glosses): pause form of 9a–c and 7a–c,
anvaya of 7, translation of all, and the lakāra and person of every finite verb; and one seen half-verse, E2
3.50.23cd (हृष्टा ग्रहीतुं खगमांस्त्वरमाणोपचक्रमे), whose Ā perfect is to be named (18 min). C — "मा":
six sentences, each to be labelled prohibitive or "me", with the rule by gist ("According to
Pāṇini, …") (5 min). D — the derivation spiral: "Derive, according to Pāṇini, जगाम" (7 min). E —
production: four sentences narrating in the perfect, one of them with an Ā perfect and one with
a periphrastic perfect (6 min). Pass: 70% overall, and every task at half its points.

#### v5.c03.l01 — नलोपाख्यानम् · "The Epic Śloka"

- **lesson_type:** reading. **Station 0:** c02.l05 +1 day; c02.l04 +1 week. **review_of:**
  v4.c05.l04 (MBh 3.50.11a), v4.c03.l04 (the perfect), c02.l02 (P2's periphrastic perfect, recognised and looked up).
- **The one teaching point:** how epic verse narrates: the śloka is the unit of sense — in E1 a sentence fills a
  half-verse (3.50.6ab, 6cd), more often the whole verse (3.50.1, 7, 10), and once runs across two (3.50.8–9, the
  children named in apposition): the course's observation on E1, not a count; the narrator is in the text (बृहदश्व उवाच; the
  vocatives to his listener: भारत, राजेन्द्र); persons come with stock epithets (भीमपराक्रमः,
  सुवर्चसम्) that the learner learns to read as compounds and then to skim. The learner writes the
  anvaya of each half-verse and marks clause, frame and epithet in three colours.
- **Text: E1** (MBh 3.50.1, 5–10; §5.3). 16 of 69 words glossed (1 in 4.3), names excluded (14
  name tokens); 23 of 69 without the lesson's headwords (1 in 3.0). Every half-verse is an
  anuṣṭubh pāda pair; two odd pādas are not pathyā (3.50.6c − − −, a pattern Kedāra does not name; 3.50.8a
  bha-vipulā), named in c04.l02.
- **Card, decision V5-D2 (listed ✓ cells, as Volume 4's V4-D6; met in the reading, not the
  lesson's point):** the periphrastic perfect — the -आम् form of an -अय- stem + the perfect of अस्
  (आस, आसतुः, आसुः) or, for Ā, of कृ (चक्रे …): 3.1.35 कास्प्रत्ययादाममन्त्रे लिटि (D) and 3.1.40
  कृञ् चानुप्रयुज्यते लिटि (Q: "and कृ, भू or अस् (the pratyāhāra कृञ्) is used after it in the perfect": the
  Kāśikā, *kṛñ iti pratyāhāreṇa kṛbhvastayo gṛhyante*, `jvkasipu.htm` 14637). FACT beside it: "a tense
  widely made and frequently used in the classical Sanskrit" (Whitney §1070; the middle with कृ,
  §1072). ✓ in the 3rd persons on owned -अय- verbs (-आम् आस: चिन्तयामास, कथयामासुः, तोषयाम् आस; for Ā, -आम्
  चक्रे); -आम् बभूव recognised (the Kāśikā's pācayām babhūva). Met first in P2 (c02.l02, looked up).
- **Forms met:** आसीत्, अकरोत्, अभ्यगच्छत् (imperfect); ददौ, प्राप (perfect ✓); तोषयाम् आस
  (periphrastic, this lesson's card; the e-text prints the auxiliary apart, the Pañcatantra
  e-text joined: both shown); the compound epithets (bahuvrīhi ✓ Volume 3–4).
- **FACT, corrected in the open (IR-030):** the 1.x book glossed महाभारत "Great India" (p.58); in
  the text, भारत is a vocative, "descendant of Bharata" (3.50.6c, addressed to Yudhiṣṭhira), a
  vṛddhi taddhita of भरत (v4.c04.l03's pattern). The 1.x "100,000 shlokas! Longest poem EVER" is
  quoted and replaced by a count with its method: the critical edition's e-text numbers 73,434
  verses in its eighteen books (`work/mbhcount.py`: distinct verse numbers with an 'a' half-line in
  `mbh_01`–`mbh_18_u.htm`, the invocation numbered 000 excluded; passages the edition prints only
  in its notes and appendices, marked * in the e-text, are not counted). The 1.x "Verse count:
  24,000 shlokas!" for the Rāmāyaṇa (p.57) is replaced the same way: 18,760 verse numbers in the
  e-text's seven books (`work/ramcount.py`). The 1.x "Author: Vyasa" (p.58), quizzed ✓, becomes TRADITION,
  "attributed to Vyāsa"; its "रामायण = राम + अयन = Journey of Rama" (p.57) is labelled the course's analysis (राम +
  अयन "going, course"), not quizzed as the one meaning.
- **Model sentences:**
  1. आसीद्राजा नलो नाम। — आसीत् राजा नलः नाम। "There was a king named Nala." (the formula, as
     Volume 3's अस्ति … नाम)
  2. भीमः प्रजार्थे यत्नमकरोत्। — भीमः प्रजा-अर्थे यत्नम् अकरोत्। "Bhīma made an effort for the
     sake of offspring."
  3. प्रसन्न ऋषिस्तस्मै वरं ददौ। — प्रसन्नः ऋषिः तस्मै वरम् ददौ। "The seer, pleased, gave him a
     boon."
  4. कन्या कुमाराश्च गुणैर्युक्ताः। — कन्या कुमाराः च गुणैः युक्ताः। "The girl and the boys were
     endowed with virtues." (a masculine plural predicate for mixed subjects)
  5. ऋषिं तोषयामास राजा। — ऋषिम् तोषयामास राजा। "The king pleased the seer."
  6. शूरस्य राज्ञः कन्यारत्नमासीत्। — शूरस्य राज्ञः कन्या-रत्नम् आसीत्। "The brave king had a
     jewel of a daughter."
- **New headwords (8; 691 → 699):** इष्टः iṣṭaḥ adj. -ष्टा -ष्टम् "desired; dear" (lexicalised
  -त of इच्छति, rule 28) · शूरः śūraḥ adj./m. "brave; hero" (Hit DCS 12) · यत्नः yatnaḥ m. a
  "effort" (Hit DCS 15) · कुमारः kumāraḥ m. a "boy; prince" · कन्या kanyā f. ā "girl; daughter" ·
  वरः varaḥ m. a "boon; bridegroom" (Hit DCS 30; वरम् "better" in Hit 0.13 is a sense of the
  same lemma, rule 27) · प्रसन्नः prasannaḥ adj. -न्ना -न्नम् "pleased, gracious" (the Volume 4
  rule 18 example, now headworded) · रत्नम् ratnam n. a "jewel".
- **Previewed:** the vipulā pādas (c04.l02). **Deferred:** the rest of the Nala story (§6).
- **Payoff:** In v4.c05.l04 the learner read one quarter, "then, her youth having come"; today the
  ten verses before it: who "she" is, and the names of the boon's children: the seer is दमन; the children are
  दमयन्ती, दम, दान्त, दमन (that their names echo his is the course's observation, not the text's).
- **Main risk:** the names as noise (14 tokens). Fix: a cast list before the text; names never
  count in the ratio.
- **§17:** TRADITION, labelled: the epic tells the Nala story to Yudhiṣṭhira in exile, through the
  seer Bṛhadaśva (the text's own frame, 3.49.43 and 3.50.1's बृहदश्व उवाच).
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: the anvaya of 3.50.8; +1 week (c03.l03): recite 3.50.1.

#### v5.c03.l02 — इत्युक्त्वा · "The Swan's Message"

- **lesson_type:** reading. **Station 0:** c03.l01 +1 day; c02.l05 +1 week. **review_of:**
  c03.l01, v4.c03.l04 (T10, the perfect endings P), v3.c03.l02 (the Ā endings).
- **The one teaching point:** speech inside epic narrative. The epic tells most of its story in
  speeches; for every speech in E2 the learner marks the speaker, the listener, the verb that opens
  it (व्याजहार, अब्रवीत्; the frame's उवाच), the word that closes it (इति, तथेत्युक्त्वा) or the
  participle that looks back on it (एवमुक्तः / एवमुक्ता, with the speaker in the instrumental:
  एवमुक्ता तु हंसेन), and the vocatives inside it (राजन्, दमयन्ति) as against those addressed by the
  narrator to his own listener (विशां पते). A speech worksheet, self-checkable (K1).
- **Card, decision V5-D3 (listed ✓ cells; met in the reading, not the lesson's point):** the Ā
  perfect — on the same reduplicated stem as the P perfect, in its weak form, the endings -ए,
  -आते, -इरे in the 3rd person (3.4.81 लिटस्तझयोरेशिरेच्, Q: "for liṭ's ta and jha, eś and irec":
  -ए and -इरे; -आते as a Δ of T10). ✓ in the 3rd persons on owned verbs: चक्रे, जज्ञे, लेभे (कुरुते, जायते,
  लभते); E2's उपचक्रमे is the card's form as read (its verb is not owned: glossed). The 1st and 2nd persons, P and Ā, are ◐ (parse, Whitney's table).
- **Text: E2** (MBh 3.50.17–19, 21–23, 25–26, 30–31; §5.3). 17 of 113 glossed (1 in 6.6), 15 name
  tokens excluded (the Aśvins among them); 25 of 113 without the headwords (1 in 4.5). Perfect forms: ददर्श ×2, जग्राह,
  व्याजहार, उत्ससर्ज, निपेतुः (P ✓), उपचक्रमे (this lesson), अगमन् (aorist, ◐ card c02.l02).
- **Model sentences:**
  1. नलो हंसमेकं जग्राह। — नलः हंसम् एकम् जग्राह। "Nala caught one goose."
  2. दमयन्ती हंसान्ग्रहीतुं यत्नं चक्रे। — दमयन्ती हंसान् ग्रहीतुम् यत्नम् चक्रे। "Damayantī made an effort
     to catch the geese." (चक्रे: the Ā perfect of the owned करोति, the card's cell; यत्नः c03.l01)
  3. सखीभिः सह कन्या वन आस्ते। — सखीभिः सह कन्या वने आस्ते। "The girl sits in the grove with her
     friends." (-ए + vowel other than अ → -अ with hiatus, v3.c04.l01)
  4. हंसो मानुषीं गिरमुवाच। — हंसः मानुषीम् गिरम् उवाच। "The goose spoke in a human voice."
  5. नलस्य समो नरो नास्ति। — नलस्य समः नरः न अस्ति। "There is no man equal to Nala."
  6. राजा कामं धारयितुं न शशाक। — राजा कामम् धारयितुम् न शशाक। "The king could not hold back
     his love."
- **New headwords (7; 699 → 706):** कामः kāmaḥ m. a "desire, love" (Hit DCS 16) · अन्तिकम्
  antikam n. a "nearness" (loc. अन्तिके "near") · मानुषः mānuṣaḥ adj./m. -षी -षम् "human" ·
  आस्ते āste "sits, stays" (class 2 Ā; glossed in v4.c06.l01's कटे आस्ते) · समः samaḥ adj. -मा
  -मम् "equal, the same" (E2 3.50.26 न समास्तस्य मानुषाः; BG 2.48 समः) · गीः gīḥ f. (stem गिर्) "speech, voice"
  (rule 14) ·
  सखी sakhī f. ī "(woman's) friend".
- **Previewed:** the 1st/2nd persons (card). **Deferred:** their production (◐, §1.3.3).
- **Reading note:** 3.50.18 वने विचरतां तेषामेकं जग्राह पक्षिणम् — a genitive with a participle that
  is not an absolute: एकम् takes तेषाम् as a partitive ("one of them"), and the sense decides (the c02.l04 test).
- **Production:** (a) the speech worksheet, and the anvaya and translation of 3.50.25–26 (K1); (b) four
  sentences of the learner's own with the Ā perfect of owned verbs (चक्रे, लेभे, जज्ञे), joined with pause form
  (K2, K5); (c) a lemma-glossary search, the drill for the c03.l05 exam: eight forms of E2 found in a printed
  alphabetical glossary of their eight lemmas and eight decoys (K1).
- **Payoff:** the haṃsa of S2b (a heron among swans) becomes a speaking character; the Volume 4
  T10 table gains its Ā row in three endings.
- **Main risk:** losing who speaks to whom across ten verses, and reading the card's -ए as a
  locative or a dative (उपचक्रमे beside वने). Fix: the speech worksheet, filled at every इति,
  अब्रवीत् and एवमुक्तः; the verb test — a form with reduplication and -ए at the end of a clause is a
  verb.
- **§17:** English bridge to 3.51 (Damayantī's longing; X3 is in the exam).
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: five Ā perfects of owned verbs; +1 week (c03.l04): E2's perfect forms
  sorted by person and pada.

#### v5.c03.l03 — मा भूः · "The Gītā, Word by Word"

- **lesson_type:** reading. **Station 0:** c03.l02 +1 day; c03.l01 +1 week. **review_of:**
  v3.c03.l04 (मा + imperative, the labelled construction), v4.c03.l05 (मा भैषीः), c02.l02 (the
  aorist card).
- **The one teaching point:** the two prohibitive constructions in one verse. मा कर्मफलहेतुर्भूः:
  मा with an aorist form without its augment (भूः, from अभूः); according to Pāṇini, लुङ् is used
  with माङ् (3.3.175 माङि लुङ्, Q) and the augment is not added "when joined with māṅ" (6.4.74 न
  माङ्योगे, Q). मा … अस्तु: मा with an imperative. FACT beside them: Whitney §579 — the
  prohibitive is "regularly and usually expressed by the particle mā with an augmentless past form,
  prevailingly aorist", while mā with the imperative "in the epics and later … begins to appear,
  and becomes an ordinary form of prohibition" (§579c). Production: the chunks मा भूत् / मा भूः
  and मा भैषीः ✓; मा + imperative ✓ (owned since v3.c03.l04); other aorists ◐.
- **Text: G1** (BG 2.47–48 = MBh 6.24.47–48, critical edition; §5.3). 1 of 27 glossed (1 in 27.0);
  6 of 27 without the headwords. The e-text prints कदा चन as two words. The verse is memorised (V2)
  and read word by word — the 1.x p.66 asset, whose "word by word" stopped after कदाचन; here every
  word.
- **The commentary line (W2; TRADITION, tier R; "the commentary ascribed to Saṃkara", no edition
  named; `bhgsbh_u.htm` 680):** Śaṅkara supplies what the verse leaves out — मा फलेषु अधिकारोऽस्तु
  "let (your) claim not be to the fruits" — and analyses कर्मफलहेतुः as कर्मफलप्राप्तेर्हेतुः
  "the cause of obtaining the fruit of action" (a genitive tatpuruṣa), and draws the consequence
  कर्मफलस्यैव जन्मनो हेतुः "the cause of the fruit of action itself, (that is) of birth" — one analysis and its
  consequence, not two analyses. Beside it the learner writes a second analysis, labelled the course's: the
  bahuvrīhi कर्मफलं हेतुर्यस्य सः "one whose motive is the fruit of action", which G1's translation follows (§5.3).
  The learner maps each phrase of the gloss to a word of the verse (the Volume 4 c06.l02 worksheet) and gives
  Śaṅkara's analysis "According to Śaṅkara" and the course's bahuvrīhi beside it. Read, not memorised. Teacher
  Notes: Rāmānuja reads the compound's first member as a dvandva, मा च कर्मफलयोर्हेतुभूः "and do not be the cause
  of action and fruit" (W17, `ramgbhau.htm` 545; key `ramanujagitabhashya`; TRADITION), an analysis not assessed.
- **FACT, corrected in the open (IR-030):** "The Bhagavad Gita … is CHAPTER 6 of the Mahabharata"
  (1.x p.58): in the critical edition the Gītā is chapters 23–40 of book 6, the Bhīṣmaparvan
  (BG 2.47 = 6.24.47); its e-text numbers 700 verses there (script count).
- **Model sentences:**
  1. फलेषु ते सङ्गो मा भूत्। — फलेषु ते सङ्गः मा भूत्। "Let there be no attachment in you to the
     fruits."
  2. मा भैषीः, कर्म कुरु। — मा भैषीः, कर्म कुरु। "Don't be afraid; do your work."
  3. सिद्धावसिद्धौ च समो भव। — सिद्धौ असिद्धौ च समः भव। "Be the same in success and in failure."
  4. कर्मफलं कर्मणो हेतुर्न भवेत्। — कर्म-फलम् कर्मणः हेतुः न भवेत्। "The fruit of an act should
     not be the motive of the act."
  5. स कदाचन न हसति। — सः कदाचन न हसति। "He never laughs."
- **New headwords (4; 706 → 710):** हेतुः hetuḥ m. u "cause, motive" (Hit DCS 19) · सङ्गः
  saṅgaḥ m. a "attachment" · सिद्धिः siddhiḥ f. i "success, fulfilment" (Hit DCS 12) · कदाचन
  kadācana indecl. "ever; (with न / मा) never" (MW s.v. kadā; rule 29).
- **Previewed:** the anuṣṭubh scansion of 2.47, which the 1.x book printed wrongly (c04.l01–l02).
  **Deferred:** the rest of the Gītā (§6).
- **Payoff:** the owned chunk मा भैषीः, learned whole in v4.c03.l05, is now one case of a rule the
  learner can state.
- **Main risk:** "mā phaleṣu" read as "not in fruits" without a verb. Fix: move 2 supplies the
  subject and the verb — फलेषु कदा चन (ते अधिकारः) मा (अस्तु) — and the commentator does the
  same (W2: मा फलेषु अधिकारोऽस्तु).
- **§17:** TRADITION, labelled: the Gītā's setting, as the epic gives it (Arjuna and Kṛṣṇa before
  the battle; 6.23.1's question is not read). The 1.x "Key teaching (from Gita): यदा यदा हि धर्मस्य …" (p.58),
  printed there with no locus, is given its locus in Teacher Notes (BG 4.7 = MBh 6.26.7, `mbh_06_u.htm` 2309) and not
  otherwise carried.
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: four prohibitions with मा + aorist chunk or imperative; +1 week (c03.l05):
  recite G1.

#### v5.c03.l04 — असतो मा सद्गमय · "An Upaniṣad Explains Itself"

- **lesson_type:** reading. **Station 0:** c03.l03 +1 day; c03.l02 +1 week. **review_of:**
  c03.l03, v3.c05.l01 (the enclitics मे, ते; 8.1.22 owned), v3.c04.l04 (padapāṭha, Nirukta 6.28),
  v4.c05.l02 (the causative: गमयति).
- **The one teaching point:** मा in the mantra is not the prohibitive: it is the enclitic
  accusative of अहम्, "me" — according to Pāṇini, त्वा and मा replace the accusative singular of
  युष्मद् and अस्मद् (8.1.23 त्वामौ द्वितीयायाः, Q; under 8.1.20, D, read with the owned 8.1.22
  तेमयावेकवचनस्य). गमय is the causative imperative of गच्छति, "make go, lead". The learner proves
  which मा is which by the verb: a prohibitive needs an aorist or an imperative in the prohibitive
  sense; "lead me" needs an object.
- **Text: U1** (Bṛhad. 1.3.28: the mantra and the Brāhmaṇa's own gloss — the first two clauses explained, the
  third said to need none: नात्र तिरोहितमिवास्ति "here nothing is hidden, as it were", 400; §5.3). 1 of 68 glossed
  (1 in 68.0); 11 of 68 without the headwords (1 in 6.2). The text explains
  its mantra itself: मृत्युर्वा असत्, सदमृतम् … मृत्युर्वै तमो, ज्योतिरमृतम् (the e-text's वा, kept in the pause form,
  is glossed "indeed": a new sense of the owned वा).
- **1.x corrected in the open:** p.67 printed the third clause as "मृत्योर्मा अमृतं गमय": the
  text (and full sandhi) has मृत्योर्मामृतं गमय; p.68 called the three clauses "intellectual …
  moral … spiritual journey" with no source: the Upaniṣad's own gloss (asat = death, sat = the
  deathless; darkness = death, light = the deathless) replaces it, as TRADITION of the text
  itself, and Śaṅkara's remark that "the Brāhmaṇa itself explains the meaning of the mantra"
  (svayam eva vyācaṣṭe brāhmaṇaṃ mantrārtham, `brupsb_u.htm` line 1582; the e-text's header warns
  that the commentary is not proofread) is quoted in Teacher Notes only.
- **The card (TRADITION; no production target; Q18): accent and the padapāṭha, read on an
  Upaniṣad verse.**
  - The three accents, according to Pāṇini: उदात्त "high", अनुदात्त "low", स्वरित "the two
    together" (1.2.29 उच्चैरुदात्तः, 1.2.30 नीचैरनुदात्तः, 1.2.31 समाहारः स्वरितः; K); the
    replacements of 8.1.20 ff. — so this मा — are wholly unaccented and do not begin a pāda
    (8.1.18 अनुदात्तं सर्वमपादादौ, K; the Volume 4 §4.0.6 row). Four sūtras on the card; 6.1.158
    (one syllable of a word not low) is Teacher Notes.
  - The line (W3): Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 4.6, द्वा सुपर्णा सयुजा सखाया … (`svetu_au.htm` 334–335).
    Its words stand also in the Atharvaveda (AV 9.9.20), and the accented Atharvaveda e-text prints
    them with marks (`avs_acu.htm` 8557–8558: *dvā́ suparṇā́ sayújā sákhāyā …*): the learner reads
    off, word by word, the syllable the e-text marks with the acute, and sees that the Upaniṣad
    e-text, like every other text the course reads, prints none. Its last word, अभिचाकशीति "looks on", is an
    intensive (of √काश्, with अभि; ◐, named and glossed: the Volume 4 §6 row "intensive").
  - The padapāṭha, the Ṛgveda's word-by-word recitation text, shown on RV 3.62.10 as the local
    padapāṭha e-text gives it (`rvpp_03u.htm` 1133: tat | savituḥ | vareṇyam | … | pra-codayāt,
    the verb's prefix set off by a hyphen) — the gāyatrī the learner counts in c04.l01; then the
    course's own pause form of U1's mantra laid out the same way (labelled ours: असतः । मा । सत् ।
    गमय …). The padapāṭha of RV 1.164.20, whose words are Śvet. 4.6, is not shown: its e-text line
    has slips (§4.0.5 c). That the card reads no padapāṭha of an Upaniṣad line is a departure from the ROADMAP
    note, which no local e-text allows the course to avoid: put to the editor (§7.3 Q11).
  - Honesty line: the course marks accent only where an e-text it opened does; it supplies no mark
    from memory, and hearing Vedic accent is beyond the course (§6). Teacher Notes: Śaṅkara on
    U1's three formulas, "the accent is to be used as recited, not the mantra accent"
    (यथापठित एव स्वरः प्रयोक्तव्यो न मान्त्रः, `brupsb1u.htm` 1546; the header warns that the
    commentary is not proofread).
  - Assessed only as "According to Pāṇini, …".
- **Model sentences:**
  1. गुरो, तमसो मा ज्योतिर्गमय। — गुरो, तमसः मा ज्योतिः गमय। "Teacher, lead me from darkness to
     light."
  2. मृत्युर्वै तमः। — मृत्युः वै तमः। "Death, truly, is darkness."
  3. सूर्यस्य ज्योतिस्तमो हन्ति। — सूर्यस्य ज्योतिः तमः हन्ति। "The sun's light kills darkness."
  4. ग्रामं मा नय। — ग्रामम् मा नय। Two readings, both written by the learner: "Lead me to the
     village" (मा = me) / "Do not lead (him) to the village" (मा prohibitive with an imperative,
     §579c); the context decides.
  5. उपनिषत्स्वयं मन्त्रस्यार्थं वदति। — उपनिषत् स्वयम् मन्त्रस्य अर्थम् वदति। "The Upaniṣad itself
     states the mantra's meaning."
- **New headwords (4; 710 → 714):** तमः tamaḥ n. (stem तमस्) "darkness" · ज्योतिः jyotiḥ n. (stem
  ज्योतिस्) "light" · वै vai indecl. "truly, indeed" (the Brāhmaṇa particle; the e-text has वा once
  in the same use, glossed) · असत् asat n. "the unreal, non-being" (rule 10: a lexicalised nañ form
  of the owned सत्).
- **Previewed:** none. **Deferred:** Vedic accent as a system; the padapāṭha of the Upaniṣads (§6).
- **Payoff:** the 1.x prayer the learner may have known as a slogan reads as a text that glosses
  itself; and मा, met since Volume 3 as "don't", is shown to be two words.
- **Main risk:** the card's weight (four sūtras and one display verse) in a reading lesson. Fix:
  only the gist is assessed, only "According to Pāṇini"; W3 is read for its marks, its sense given
  in the course's translation (§5.6).
- **§17:** TRADITION, the passage's place, in Teacher Notes: one should mutter these where he, the prastotṛ named in
  the line before, begins the prelude (the e-text's preceding line, स यत्र प्रस्तुयात्तदेतानि जपेत्, `brup___u.htm`
  393: "where he would chant the prelude, there one should mutter these"); according to Śaṅkara the muttering is
  the sacrificer's (याजमानं जपकर्म, `brupsb1u.htm` 1547; the header warns that the commentary is not proofread).
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: label six मा; +1 week (c04.l01): recite U1.

#### v5.c03.l05 — तृतीयपरीक्षा · "Chapter 3 Exam"

- **lesson_type:** review. **review_of:** c03.l01–l04. **Shape:** §4.3 (45 min). Ledger stays 714.
- **Unseen text: X3** (MBh 3.51.5–7, 9; §5.3): 9 of 37 words not owned (1 in 4.1), names
  excluded; their 8 lemmas given in a lemma glossary (dictionary forms, with 8 decoys). The e-text's *nasvasthāṃ*
  (3.51.5c) was checked and kept: the critical text writes the negative compound joined in both
  places it occurs (§4.0.5 b); it is glossed "not well (na + svastha)". 3.51.8 is not printed: its second half has nine syllables in
  the critical text (अनुभूयतामयं वीराः), an irregular pāda the course will not set unseen (§4.0.5 c).
  No chapter headword was chosen for X3 (rule 24); three Volume 5 headwords occur in it — सखी (c03.l02, twice, in
  -सखीगणः), सुता (the f. of सुतः, c02.l04, twice), यौवनम् (c01.l01, in -यौवनाम्) — so it is reported both ways:
  9 of 37 at the ledger, 14 of 37 (1 in 2.6) without them.
- **Payoff:** the svayaṃvara begins; the learner reads epic verse with a glossary they search
  themselves, finding the lemma behind each form.
- **S0 → next:** +1 day (c04.l01): X3's perfect and imperfect forms named again, person, number and pada (a
  review that needs no metre: weight is taught only in c04.l01); +1 week (c04.l02): the anvaya of 3.51.7 written
  again.

---

### 4.4 Chapter 4 — छन्दोऽलङ्कारौ · Meter & Figures

**Goals.** Scan any verse the learner has read; know the anuṣṭubh as the texts have it and as
Kedāra defines it, and the gāyatrī as it is written and as the tradition counts it; read the
eleven- and fourteen-syllable metres by formula; recite in metre; identify five figures by the
definitions of one named treatise; correct the 1.x metre and figure chapters in the open.

**The treatises (TRADITION; edition statements from the e-text headers, §4.0.5).**
- Kedārabhaṭṭa's **Vṛttaratnākara** (`kedvratu.htm`, "Based on the edition by Śrī Kedāra Nātha
  Śarmā … 6th ed., Varanasi: Chaukhambha Sanskrit Sansthan, 1980"): KedV 1.9 (weight), 2.21–2.30
  (the anuṣṭubh), 3.28–3.30, 3.79, 3.101 (the metres); 3.97 (mandākrāntā) cited, its line printed from
  Sulhaṇa's text (KVrk 3.100, W20; §4.0.5 c). KedV 1.7 (the gaṇas) is printed as a
  table, not as its verse: the e-text reads *jmau* where the sense needs *jbhau* (ja and bha), so
  the line is not quoted (principle 2).
- Sulhaṇa's text of Kedāra (`kvrtrsuu.htm`, "Based on a manuscript from Patan"): KVrk 3.100, the mandākrāntā line
  (W20).
- Mammaṭa's **Kāvyaprakāśa** (`mamkprku.htm`, Kārikāvalī, "Based on the revised ed. by Ganganatha
  Jha, Varanasi: Bharatiya Vidya Prakasan, 1967"): K79a, K81b, K83a, K87a, K93a, and K109 in Teacher
  Notes (the GRETIL numbering of the kārikās; the vṛtti in `mamkavpu.htm` for the examples Mammaṭa
  cites, of which ex. 357, W5, is read: the e-text notes it as "Śrivatsalāñchanācārya's Kāvyaparīkṣā 161").
- Daṇḍin's **Kāvyādarśa** (`dkavy12u.htm`, "based on the ed. by Rangacharya Raddi Shastri, Poona:
  BORI 1938"; `dkavy3_u.htm`, Dimitrov's critical edition of ch. 3, Wiesbaden 2011): KA 1.55–1.58ab (anuprāsa
  defined and shown on his own two examples, W22), 1.61 (yamaka named), 2.66 (the rūpaka example), 2.221 and
  2.234 (utprekṣā, Teacher Notes), 3.10 (a yamaka verse).
- Vāmana's **Kāvyālaṅkārasūtra** (`vamkalsu.htm`, ed. Nārāyaṇarāma, Delhi: Motilal Banarsidass,
  1983): 1.1.1–2, 1.2.6 — in Teacher Notes, against the 1.x "soul of Sanskrit poetry".
- Viśvanātha's **Sāhityadarpaṇa** (`visah__u.htm`; "The text is not proof-read!"; no edition named): one line on
  the compound card (W24).
- The **Nirukta** 7.12 (key `nirukta`, exists) and the **Pañcaviṃśa Brāhmaṇa** 19.11.6
  (`pncvbr1u.htm`; no edition named in the header) for the gāyatrī.

**Counts made for this chapter (FACT; script, method stated, never "always"):**

Method (one script for the volume, `work/vipcount.py`, run once at synthesis and checked by
hand on samples): every e-text line labelled a, c or e in the chapters named, star passages
excluded, scanned whole; a line counts if it has sixteen syllables (the Gītā: 1,289 of 1,400
lines; the Nala: 1,854 of 1,861); syllables 5–7 and 13–15 are read off. The judges' recounts
agree with these figures.

| Corpus (e-text) | Half-verses of 8+8 | Odd pāda, syllables 5–7 | Even pāda 5–7 ⏑ − ⏑ |
|---|---|---|---|
| Bhagavadgītā, MBh 6.23–40 (`mbh_06_u.htm`) | 1,289 | ⏑ − − 1,140 (88.4%) · ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ 60 (4.7%) · − ⏑ ⏑ 35 (2.7%) · − ⏑ − 28 (2.2%) · − − − 25 (1.9%) · ⏑ ⏑ − 1 (BG 2.46a) | 1,289 (100%) |
| Nala, MBh 3.50–78 (`mbh_03_u.htm`) | 1,854 | ⏑ − − 1,537 (82.9%) · ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ 155 (8.4%) · − − − 84 (4.5%) · − ⏑ ⏑ 60 (3.2%) · − ⏑ − 16 (0.9%) · ⏑ ⏑ − 2 | 1,852 (99.9%); − − ⏑ 2 |
| Meghadūta (`kmeghdpu.htm`, De rev. Raghavan) | — | 111 verses, 444 pādas: all mandākrāntā | — |

Kedāra's four vipulās are bha (− ⏑ ⏑), ra (− ⏑ −), na (⏑ ⏑ ⏑) and ta (− − ⏑) (KedV 2.27–2.30).
The texts counted use bha, ra and na; **neither text has a single ta-vipulā**; both use − − −,
which Kedāra's list does not contain. Both statements print side by side (principle 5).

**Volume 4 §6 row carried:** "Metre (anuṣṭubh pathyā/vipulā, IR-028; gāyatrī count, IR-029) and
figures (yamaka, IR-027) → v5.c04". **1.x assets:** the metre and figure cards (pp.119–124),
corrected (§4.0.4).

**Exam shape (l05, 45 min).** A — recite V1–V4 in metre; a peer or the recording checks the
pauses at pāda ends (5 min). B — X4 (unseen, Hit 0.24 and Nītiśataka 1.12; 8 words not owned, 1 in 3.6, their
9 lemmas in a lemma glossary with 9 decoys): scan every pāda, name each metre (and each anuṣṭubh pāda as pathyā or
by its vipulā, according to Kedāra), find the upamā in X4a and the rūpaka in X4b with their
parts; and count RV 3.62.10 as written and as recited (23; 24 with वरेणियम्, Whitney §113b) (20 min). C — figures: six short lines (from §5 texts and Daṇḍin's examples) to be
labelled upamā, rūpaka, anuprāsa, lāṭānuprāsa, yamaka or none, "according to Mammaṭa" (8 min).
D — the derivation spiral: "Derive, according to Pāṇini, पठ्यते" (7 min). E — production: four sentences of
the learner's own in Sanskrit naming what X4 does, on the model "इदं वृत्तमिन्द्रवज्रा", "अत्र
उपमा, … उपमानम्", joined with pause form (5 min); no verse is composed — composition is
examined only after c05.l03. Pass: 70% overall, and every task at half its points.

#### v5.c04.l01 — लघु गुरु · "Light and Heavy"

- **lesson_type:** grammar. **Station 0:** c03.l05 +1 day; c03.l04 +1 week. **review_of:**
  v1.c02 (vowel length), v1.c05 (conjuncts; anusvāra and visarga), c01.l01, c03.l03.
- **The one teaching point:** a syllable is heavy (गुरु) if its vowel is long, or it has
  anusvāra or visarga, or a conjunct follows; otherwise it is light (लघु); the last syllable of a
  pāda may count as either. According to Kedāra (KedV 1.9, memorised): सानुस्वारो विसर्गान्तो
  दीर्घो युक्तपरश्च यः। वा पादान्ते त्वसौ ग्वक्रो ज्ञेयोऽन्यो मात्रिको लृजुः॥ (the heavy is
  written with a curved sign, the light — one mora — with a straight one). The learner scans:
  the consonants between two vowels are counted across word boundaries within the pāda;
  ḥ, ṃ count as closing the syllable; aspirates are one consonant.
- **Texts scanned:** the owned verses (Hit 0.36, Hit 0.6, the benediction), S1, G1 (§5; every
  scansion in §5 was made by script and is the answer key).
- **The 1.x BG 2.47 card, read fairly (IR-028, second half):** its strings scan. Pāda 1's "G L G
  G" is syllables 4–7 (वा धि का रस्); pāda 2's "L G L G" is syllables 5–8 (क दा च न), whose last
  syllable is light by nature and heavy only by the pāda-end option (KedV 1.9's वा पादान्ते). The
  fault is the window: the card's rule names syllables 5–7, and neither string is that window. The
  course scans मा फलेषु कदा चन as − ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ ×, the last syllable free (×, वा पादान्ते). The 1.x
  weight rule "a short vowel … NOT ending in अं/अः" is right; it omitted the pāda-final option.
- **Kedāra's rule scanned on itself:** KedV 1.9, the memorised definition, is itself a pathyā
  śloka (सानुस्वारो विसर्गान्तो: syllables 5–7 ⏑ − −; the even pādas ⏑ − ⏑) — the learner's first
  full scansion is of the rule they scan by.
- **Card (no production target): the gāyatrī counted (IR-029).** RV 3.62.10 as written (`rv_03_u.htm` 1367–1368; the padapāṭha
  `rvpp_03u.htm` 1133): तत्सवितुर्वरेण्यं भर्गो देवस्य धीमहि। धियो यो नः प्रचोदयात्॥ — the
  learner counts 7 + 8 + 8 = 23. TRADITION: "the gāyatrī has twenty-four syllables"
  (caturviṃśatyakṣarā gāyatrī, Pañcaviṃśa Brāhmaṇa 19.11.6, `pncvbr1u.htm` 3114) and is "three-footed" (gāyatrīm
  eva tripadām satīm, Nirukta 7.12, `niruktau.htm` 2679, quoting a brāhmaṇa: only this clause is quoted, which needs
  no correction; the e-text keeps the final m before s, a departure from full sandhi, noted). The 1.x "The most
  sacred Vedic meter" (p.120) is withdrawn (principle 6). Between the two stands a FACT
  the course can cite: Whitney §113b — in the mantras "y and v are to be read as i and u … in order
  to make good the metre" (`whitney/07_Chapter_III_EUPHONIC_COMBINATION.md` 254–259). Read so,
  वरेण्यम् is va-re-ṇi-yam and the first pāda has eight syllables: 8 + 8 + 8 = 24 (the
  application to this verse is the course's; Whitney's examples are other words). The 1.x book counted ॐ भूर्भुवः स्वः into the 24 (p.120): those words
  are not in RV 3.62.10 (the e-text line). And according to Kedāra (KedV 1.17, 1.19), in classical
  metrics "gāyatrī" is the name of the class of six-syllable pādas — four of which also make 24.
- **Model sentences:**
  1. दीर्घः स्वरो गुरुः। — दीर्घः स्वरः गुरुः। "A long vowel is heavy."
  2. संयोगात्पूर्वो ह्रस्वोऽपि गुरुः। — संयोगात् पूर्वः ह्रस्वः अपि गुरुः। "Even a short vowel is
     heavy before a conjunct."
  3. लघोरेका मात्रा, गुरोर्द्वे। — लघोः एका मात्रा, गुरोः द्वे। "A light syllable has one mora, a
     heavy one two."
  4. अस्मिन्पादेऽष्टावक्षराणि। — अस्मिन् पादे अष्टौ अक्षराणि। "In this pāda there are eight
     syllables."
  5. पादान्ते यतिः। — पाद-अन्ते यतिः। "At the end of a pāda there is a pause."
  6. गायत्र्यां त्रयः पादाः। — गायत्र्याम् त्रयः पादाः। "In the gāyatrī there are three pādas."
- **New headwords (4; 714 → 718):** छन्दः chandaḥ n. (stem छन्दस्) "metre" (rule 25) · मात्रा
  mātrā f. ā "measure; mora" (rule 25; the V1 term "vowel sign" is a sense of the same lemma, not
  headworded then) · संयोगः saṃyogaḥ m. a "conjunct; union" (rule 25) · यतिः yatiḥ f. i "pause
  (caesura)" (rule 25; KedV 1.12's "yatir vicchedasaṃjñitaḥ", quoted in its clean half only).
- **Previewed:** the pāda types (c04.l02). **Deferred:** quantitative metres by mora (āryā): §6.
- **Payoff:** लघु and गुरु, owned since Volumes 1 and 2 as "small" and "teacher", are the
  tradition's names for light and heavy; and the verses recited since Volumes 1–2 (Hit 0.36, the benediction)
  scan by the same rule.
- **§17 (HISTORY, hedged, keyed `vrttaratnakara`):** a manuscript of the Vṛttaratnākara kept at Jaisalmer and dated
  Saṃvat 1190 (1134 CE) gives the latest possible date for Kedāra, according to the BnF authority record; the
  e-text header names him "Kedarabhatta (12th c.)", the header's own statement.
- **Main risk:** weighing by the vowel letter only (ignoring the following conjunct across a word
  boundary: फलेषु कदा: the -षु is light; कर्मण्येव: the -र्म- syllable heavy). Fix: the scanning
  grid (vowels on one line, consonant count beneath each gap).
- **S0 → next:** +1 day (c04.l02): scan the first half of X3 and two pādas of S1 (the item moved here from
  c03.l05, which now has a review that needs no metre); +1 week (c04.l03): scan S4b.

#### v5.c04.l02 — अनुष्टुभ् · "The Śloka"

- **lesson_type:** grammar. **Station 0:** c04.l01 +1 day; c03.l05 +1 week. **review_of:**
  c04.l01, c03.l01–l02 (the Nala ślokas), c02.l02 (Panc 4.14).
- **The one teaching point:** the anuṣṭubh pāda has eight syllables, and its syllables 5–7 are
  fixed: in the even pādas ⏑ − ⏑, in the odd pādas, as a rule, ⏑ − −. According to Kedāra
  (KedV 2.21–2.22): वक्त्रं नाद्यान्नसौ स्यातामब्धेर्योऽनुष्टुभि ख्यातम्। युजोर्जेन सरिद्भर्तुः
  पथ्यावक्त्रं प्रकीर्तितम्॥ — "after the fourth, a ya (⏑ − −) … with ja (⏑ − ⏑) in the even
  pādas: pathyā". The odd-pāda alternatives he names are the vipulās (2.27–2.30: bha-, ra-, na-,
  ta-vipulā); 2.25 is a separate rule, his name (yugmavipulā) for the even pāda whose seventh syllable is light
  (`kedvratu.htm` 246). The learner scans the counted texts and finds what the count table (§4.4) shows: the
  pathyā in 82.9–88.4% of odd pādas, na-, bha-, ra-vipulā in the rest, no ta-vipulā, and a − − −
  pattern Kedāra does not list (FACT and TRADITION side by side).
- **IR-028 corrected in the open:** the 1.x rule "Syllables 5, 6, 7 of pāda 1 & 3: L-G-(any)"
  (p.119) is quoted; the pathyā is ⏑ − −; "any" would admit ⏑ − ⏑ in an odd pāda, which neither
  counted text has as a pattern of its own (it is the even-pāda cadence).
- **Kedāra's lines scanned on themselves** (from the design "tradition first"): KedV 2.22 is a
  pathyā śloka (युजोर्जेन सरिद्भर्तुः: syllables 5–7 ⏑ − −; पथ्यावक्त्रं प्रकीर्तितम्: ⏑ − ⏑); KedV
  2.21 is not — its even pāda अब्धेर्योऽनुष्टुभि ख्यातम् has ⏑ − − at 5–7 (ष्टु light, भि heavy
  before ख्य): a plain *vaktra*, the very kind the line defines (scanned by `work/v5tools.py`).
- **The text's own account of the śloka (W4; TRADITION, the Rāmāyaṇa's story, never HISTORY):**
  Rām 1.2.15–17 — Vālmīki, grieving for the krauñca bird, names what he has just spoken:
  पादबद्धोऽक्षरसमस्तन्त्रीलयसमन्वितः। शोकार्तस्य प्रवृत्तो मे श्लोको भवतु नान्यथा॥ "bound in quarters,
  even in syllables, fit for the lute's measure; it came from me stricken with grief (śoka): let it
  be a śloka, and nothing else". The learner scans it: its first pāda is a na-vipulā (FACT), its
  third a pathyā. The verse he spoke (mā niṣāda, 1.2.14) is named and not printed: the e-text reads *śāśvatīṃ*,
  with *pratiṣṭhām* ("lasting standing, for years"), the familiar reading, also in the Kāvyamīmāṃsā's quotation
  (`rajkmimu.htm` 315), *śāśvatīḥ*, with *samāḥ*; the course does not choose between readings (§4.0.5 c).
- **Examples, all from texts read:** pathyā: S1 (4 of 4 pādas), and the thread verse अहिंसा परमो
  धर्मः, which is the odd pāda of epic ślokas (MBh 1.11.12a; 3.198.69c); na-vipulā: the owned benediction's
  first pāda सर्वे भवन्तु सुखिनः (− − ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ ×) and Hit 0.6a विद्या ददाति विनयं; bha-vipulā:
  E1 3.50.8a तस्मै प्रसन्नो दमनः; − − −: E1 3.50.6c तम् अभ्यगच्छद् ब्रह्मर्षिर्; the irregular BG
  2.46a (⏑ ⏑ −, the one in 1,289).
- **Metre as a test of a reading (principle 2):** Hit 0.18's third pāda reads in the e-text
  एकश्चन्द्रमस्तमो हन्ति, nine syllables; the course therefore did not use the verse. P4's verse
  (Panc 1.439ab) and X3's 3.51.8c are the course's two other cases (§4.0.5 c). The 1.x quiz answer that metre
  "helps … identify era ✓" (p.122) is withdrawn: no method, no source; that it helps decide a reading is what
  these cases show.
- **Model sentences:**
  1. अनुष्टुभि चत्वारः पादाः। — अनुष्टुभि चत्वारः पादाः। "In the anuṣṭubh there are four pādas."
  2. विषमे पादे प्रायः पथ्या। — विषमे पादे प्रायः पथ्या। "In an odd pāda there is mostly the
     pathyā (cadence)."
  3. समे पादे सप्तमं लघु। — समे पादे सप्तमम् लघु। "In an even pāda the seventh is light."
  4. अयं नियमो गीतायां नले च। — अयम् नियमः गीतायाम् नले च। "This rule holds in the Gītā and in
     the Nala."
  5. पथ्यायां पञ्चमं लघु। — पथ्यायाम् पञ्चमम् लघु। "In the pathyā the fifth is light."
- **New headwords (3; 718 → 721):** विषमः viṣamaḥ adj. -मा -मम् "odd; uneven" (KedV 1.13's
  term) · नियमः niyamaḥ m. a "rule, restriction" · प्रायः prāyaḥ indecl. "mostly, as a rule"
  (rule 25: terms used in sentences; the vipulā and metre names are labels).
- **Previewed:** none. **Deferred:** Vedic metre beyond the gāyatrī's count (§6).
- **Production:** (a) name the type of every odd pāda of E2 (K1); (b) rewrite four pādas of S1–S3
  replacing one word by an owned word of the same weight shape, and show the pāda still scans
  (K6) — a rehearsal, not composition, which is c05.l03's.
- **Payoff:** the benediction the learner has recited since Volume 1 begins with a na-vipulā: the
  tradition's name for something the learner has been saying all along.
- **Main risk:** turning the count into a rule ("the odd pāda must be ⏑ − −"). Fix: the count is
  shown as a count, the tradition's list as a list; neither is "the rule" of the language.
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: name the pāda types of six pādas; +1 week (c04.l04): recite E1 3.50.1–5
  in metre.

#### v5.c04.l03 — वृत्तम् · "Metres by Pattern"

- **lesson_type:** grammar. **Station 0:** c04.l02 +1 day; c04.l01 +1 week. **review_of:**
  c04.l01–l02, c01.l03–l04 (the upajātis read by ear).
- **The one teaching point:** a fixed metre (वृत्तम्) is defined by a string of three-syllable
  groups (गण; the eight are on a card: ya ⏑ − −, ma − − −, ta − − ⏑, ra − ⏑ −, ja ⏑ − ⏑, bha − ⏑ ⏑,
  na ⏑ ⏑ ⏑, sa ⏑ ⏑ −; ga − and la ⏑ for single syllables). The learner expands Kedāra's formula and
  matches it: स्यादिन्द्रवज्रा यदि तौ जगौ गः (3.28: ta ta ja ga ga), उपेन्द्रवज्रा जतजास्ततो गौ (3.29),
  … पादौ यदीयावुपजातयस्ताः (3.30: a verse that mixes them is an upajāti), उक्ता वसन्ततिलका तभजा
  जगौ गः (3.79). ✓: indravajrā, upendravajrā, upajāti, vasantatilakā (scan, name, recite).
  ◐ with the card: mandākrāntā (Kedāra's line in Sulhaṇa's text, KVrk 3.100, W20, itself a mandākrāntā pāda,
  with its pauses after syllables 4 and 10) and śārdūlavikrīḍita
  (3.101). Kedāra's lines are scanned on themselves (from the design "tradition first"): 3.28
  स्यादिन्द्रवज्रा यदि तौ जगौ गः is an indravajrā pāda, 3.29 an upendravajrā pāda, 3.79 उक्ता
  वसन्ततिलका तभजा जगौ गः a vasantatilakā pāda — each line is an example of the metre it defines.
- **Texts:** S4b (memorised, V4: upajāti, pādas indra-, indra-, upendra-, indravajrā); S3b (a pure indravajrā)
  beside S3b2 (an upajāti: the same verse with pāda b upendravajrā), a ready contrast; M1 (Nītiśataka 1.27,
  vasantatilakā; §5.4; 3 of 18 glossed); W18 (Ks 1.1), W19 (KMgD 1) and W20 (KVrk 3.100) as displays (§5.6).
- **1.x corrected in the open:** (i) Kumārasambhava 1.1 is not indravajrā in every pāda (p.121
  "each pāda fits this"): its pādas b and d (हिमालयो नाम नगाधिराजः, स्थितः पृथिव्या इव मानदण्डः)
  begin with a light syllable, upendravajrā; the verse is an upajāti. (ii) "The entire 121-verse
  poem" (Meghadūta, p.121): the edition the course reads has 111 verses, and all 444 pādas scan as
  mandākrāntā (script); the course compared no other edition. (iii) "Most majestic — used in stotras
  (Devī Māhātmyam, Hanumān-bāhuk)" (sragdharā, p.121) is withdrawn: the course has not verified
  either use, and the claim of majesty is a claim of beauty without a source. (iv) "Most common
  Vedic meter (>40% of Ṛgveda)" (triṣṭubh, p.121) is withdrawn: no count with a method was found. (v) "Used for
  grand, declamatory verses (Bharavi, Magha)" (śārdūlavikrīḍita, p.121) and "This tells you … likely the poet/era
  … often the mood" (p.121) are withdrawn: no count, no source.
- **Model sentences:**
  1. इदं वृत्तमिन्द्रवज्रा। — इदम् वृत्तम् इन्द्रवज्रा। "This metre is an indravajrā."
  2. नीचा विघ्नभयेन कार्यं न कुर्वन्ति। — नीचाः विघ्न-भयेन कार्यम् न कुर्वन्ति। "The low do not undertake a
     task, for fear of obstacles." (owned words only: M1's प्रारभ्यते is glossed)
  3. उत्तमा विघ्नैर्हता अपि न विरमन्ति। — उत्तमाः विघ्नैः हताः अपि न विरमन्ति। "The best, though
     struck by obstacles, do not stop."
  4. उपजातावुपेन्द्रवज्रा चेन्द्रवज्रा च। — उपजातौ उपेन्द्रवज्रा च इन्द्रवज्रा च। "In an upajāti
     there are both upendravajrā and indravajrā."
  5. वसन्ततिलकायां खलु चतुर्दशाक्षराणि। — वसन्ततिलकायाम् खलु चतुर्दश अक्षराणि। "In the
     vasantatilakā, indeed, there are fourteen syllables." (खलु, an enclitic particle, never
     stands first in its sentence)
- **New headwords (6; 721 → 727):** वृत्तम् vṛttam n. a "(fixed) metre" (rule 25) · विघ्नः vighnaḥ
  m. a "obstacle" · नीचः nīcaḥ adj. -चा -चम् "low" · उत्तमः uttamaḥ adj. -मा -मम् "highest, best"
  (Hit DCS 14) · खलु khalu indecl. "indeed" · विरमति viramati "stops" (वि + रम्, P; MW).
- **Previewed:** none. **Deferred:** the mātrā metres (āryā, vaitālīya) and the long metres in
  production (§6).
- **Payoff:** the verses of c01, recited by ear for three weeks, are named; Kālidāsa's himālaya
  (owned since v1.c04) stands in the second pāda of an upajāti.
- **Main risk:** gaṇa letters memorised as a code but not heard. Fix: every formula is recited
  over its verse before it is expanded.
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: name the metre of four lines; +1 week (c04.l05): recite M1.

#### v5.c04.l04 — अलङ्कारः · "Figures by Definition"

- **lesson_type:** grammar. **Station 0:** c04.l03 +1 day; c04.l02 +1 week. **review_of:**
  c01.l02 (S2b), c01.l04 (S4b), c04.l03.
- **The one teaching point:** a figure is identified by the parts its definition names; the five
  fall into two families, each with one test. **Likeness** (is the word of likeness there?):
  upamā, whose four parts the learner has already filled unnamed on S2b and S4b (c01.l02, l04),
  and rūpaka. **Repetition** (same sounds — and is the second meaning different?): anuprāsa,
  lāṭānuprāsa, yamaka. The definitions are read with the card open, not memorised. According
  to Mammaṭa: upamā is "likeness where there is difference" (साधर्म्यमुपमा भेदे, K87a), with four
  parts — the thing compared (उपमेयम्), the standard (उपमानम्), the shared property (साधारणधर्मः,
  display) and the word of likeness (इव, यथा: Mammaṭa's वा-आदि, K89, display); rūpaka is "the
  non-difference of standard and compared" (तद्रूपकमभेदो य उपमानोपमेययोः, K93a; Daṇḍin's examples
  बाहुलता पाणिपद्मं चरणपल्लवः, KA 2.66); anuprāsa is "sameness of sounds" (वर्णसाम्यमनुप्रासः,
  K79a); lāṭānuprāsa is a word repeated in the same sense, differing "only in intent"
  (शाब्दस्तु लाटानुप्रासो भेदे तात्पर्यमात्रतः, K81b); yamaka is the repetition "of sounds with a
  different sense, when there is a sense" (अर्थे सत्यर्थभिन्नानां वर्णानां सा पुनः श्रुतिः यमकम्,
  K83). The learner fills a parts table for each example.
- **Examples (all from texts read or the treatises' own):** upamā with यथा: S2b (बको यथा); with
  इव: S4b (छायेव मैत्री) and Raghuvaṃśa 1.1 (W21: वागर्थाविव संपृक्तौ, `kragh_pu.htm` 103–104, key
  `raghuvamsha`); rūpaka: S4a (मनुष्यरूपेण मृगाश्चरन्ति, the course's analysis "according to
  Mammaṭa's definition") and Daṇḍin 2.66; lāṭānuprāsa: Mammaṭa's example (W5, ex. 357, which the e-text notes as
  "Śrivatsalāñchanācārya's Kāvyaparīkṣā 161": यस्य न सविधे
  दयिता दवदहनस्तुहिनदीधितिस्तस्य। यस्य च सविधे दयिता दवदहनस्तुहिनदीधितिस्तस्य॥ — the same words,
  the same senses, subject and predicate exchanged) and NŚ 1.19d (W6: वाग्भूषणं भूषणम्, the
  course's application of K81b, for lāṭānuprāsa only); anuprāsa: Daṇḍin's own examples, KA 1.56–57 (W22: चारु
  चान्द्रमसं भीरु … मन्मनो मन्मथाक्रान्तं …, the run of च, न, म), which his 1.55 introduces (वर्णावृत्तिरनुप्रासः) and
  1.58a closes (इत्यनुप्रासमिच्छन्ति); yamaka: Daṇḍin KA 3.10 (वसुधा
  वसुधारया: "the earth … by a stream of wealth", वसु-धारा), Raghuvaṃśa 9.4 (W7: शमरते ऽमरतेजसि,
  "delighting in calm, with an immortal's splendour": the sandhi hides the seam the pause form
  shows) and NŚ 1.17d (W8: वारणं वारणानाम्, "a check … for elephants") — both from the design
  "skill first", both re-found and scanned (drutavilambita; mālinī), the metres displays only.
- **Card (K, no production target): likeness inside a compound.** According to Pāṇini, a word for
  the thing compared compounds with व्याघ्र and the like when the shared property is not stated
  (2.1.56 उपमितं व्याघ्रादिभिः सामान्याप्रयोगे: पुरुषव्याघ्रः "a tiger of a man", an upamā in one
  word); a compound read as identification the tradition files under 2.1.72 मयूरव्यंसकादयश्च: Viśvanātha, on
  सुन्दरं वदनाम्बुजम्, एवं चात्र मयूरव्यंसकादित्वाद्रूपकसमास एव "so here, because it belongs to the
  mayūravyaṃsaka group, it is a rūpaka compound only" (Sāhityadarpaṇa, W24, `visah__u.htm` 8465; TRADITION; key
  `sahityadarpana`). The learner sees why Daṇḍin's rūpaka examples (बाहुलता …) are compounds.
- **IR-027 corrected in the open:** the 1.x yamaka examples (p.124) "भज गोविन्दं भज गोविन्दं" and
  "सजलं तोयं सजलं तोयं" repeat words in the same sense: by Mammaṭa's K83 they are not yamaka (no
  difference of sense); by K81 the first is at most lāṭānuprāsa, if the repetitions differ in
  construction. Further 1.x lines quoted and withdrawn (claims of beauty or rank without a source, principle 6):
  "Without alaṅkāra, Sanskrit poetry is just words. WITH it — pure magic" and "the soul of Sanskrit poetry"
  (p.122); "Famous example — Kālidāsa's genius" (p.123); "rich polysemy + sandhi makes śleṣa effortless" and the
  Rāghavapāṇḍavīya claim (p.124); "(are unmatched)" added to उपमा कालिदासस्य (p.59). Teacher Notes give one
  theorist's view instead, attributed — Vāmana: "poetry is to be accepted because of ornament; ornament is beauty"
  (काव्यं ग्राह्यमलङ्कारात्। सौन्दर्यमलङ्कारः। VKS 1.1.1–2) and "style is the soul of poetry"
  (रीतिरात्मा काव्यस्य, 1.2.6). "Magha said: upamā kālidāsasya …"
  (p.124): the verse speaks of Māgha in the third person and is printed without a speaker in
  Sternbach's Mahāsubhāṣitasaṃgraha (MSS 7104, W23, `msubhs_u.htm` 14359–14360, key `sternbach1974`;
  its e-text has no source apparatus); the course knows no text that puts it in Māgha's mouth. It
  is printed as TRADITION about the poets. The utprekṣā, śleṣa and anuprāsa examples of pp.122–124 have no
  locus and are not printed.
- **Model sentences:**
  1. अत्रोपमा। — अत्र उपमा। "Here there is an upamā."
  2. मैत्र्युपमेयम्, छायोपमानम्। — मैत्री उपमेयम्, छाया उपमानम्। "Friendship is the thing
     compared; the shadow is the standard."
  3. बाहुलतेति रूपकम्। — "बाहु-लता" इति रूपकम्। "'Arm-creeper' is a rūpaka." (a quoted form is joined to its
     इति, STYLE_GUIDE §3.1)
  4. वसुधा वसुधारयेत्यत्र यमकम्, अर्थो भिन्नः। — वसुधा वसु-धारया इति अत्र यमकम्, अर्थः
     भिन्नः। "In vasudhā vasudhārayā there is yamaka: the sense differs." (भिन्नः: -न of भिनत्ति,
     glossed)
  5. उपमानोपमेययोः सादृश्यं साधर्म्येण। — उपमान-उपमेययोः सादृश्यम् साधर्म्येण। "The likeness of
     standard and compared is through a shared property." (साधर्म्यम् display)
- **New headwords (6; 727 → 733):** अलङ्कारः alaṅkāraḥ m. a "ornament; figure of speech" · उपमा
  upamā f. ā "simile, likeness" · उपमानम् upamānam n. a "standard of comparison" · उपमेयम्
  upameyam n. a "thing compared" · रूपकम् rūpakam n. a "metaphor (identification)" · सादृश्यम्
  sādṛśyam n. a "likeness" (rule 25 for all six).
- **Teacher Notes (moved there at the repair, to keep the lesson to five definitions and one card):** utprekṣā,
  śleṣa, arthāntaranyāsa, yathāsaṅkhya, named, not assessed: Daṇḍin's KA 2.221 definition of utprekṣā
  (`dkavy12u.htm` 757) and his KA 2.234 (783), which names इव among its markers (… उत्प्रेक्षा व्यज्यते
  शब्दैरिवशब्दोऽपि तादृशः): a comparison made with इव "as if" of what is only imagined is utprekṣā, not upamā; and
  Mammaṭa's K109 for arthāntaranyāsa, a general statement supporting a particular or the reverse, "by likeness or
  otherwise" (… सोऽर्थान्तरन्यासः साधर्म्येणेतरेण वा, `mamkprku.htm` 418) — compare how the prose cites an uktaṃ ca
  verse to support a particular case (the course's analogy, not Mammaṭa's; from the design "skill first"); and
  Vāmana's three sūtras (above). **Previewed:** none. **Deferred:** dhvani, rasa (§6).
- **Payoff:** the heron among swans (S2b) and the shadow of the forenoon (S4b), read for their
  grammar in c01, have parts with names; Kālidāsa's first verse (Raghuvaṃśa 1.1, W21), which the 1.x book
  quoted without locus, is read with its locus.
- **Main risk:** the load — five definitions to tell apart in one lesson, read with the card open — and, inside
  it, calling every repetition yamaka. Fix: the two families with one test each (likeness: is there a word of
  likeness?; repetition: same sounds, and is the sense different?), printed with the parts table; one example of each
  figure, none serving two (W6 for lāṭānuprāsa only, W22 for anuprāsa); the further figures and Vāmana in Teacher
  Notes; the one card kept (likeness in a compound, K).
- **§17 (HISTORY, hedged, keyed `kavyaprakasha`):** Mammaṭa wrote in the later 11th century CE, on the usual
  scholarly estimate (De: its last quarter; Kane: 1050–1100; search-confirmed, page not opened, §4.0.9).
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: label five lines; +1 week (c04.l05 → c05.l01): recite S4b.

#### v5.c04.l05 — चतुर्थपरीक्षा · "Chapter 4 Exam"

- **lesson_type:** review. **review_of:** c04.l01–l04. **Shape:** §4.4 (45 min). Ledger stays 733.
- **Unseen text: X4** (Hit 0.24, Nītiśataka 1.12; §5.4): 8 of 29 words not owned (1 in 3.6); their 9 lemmas in
  a lemma glossary with 9 decoys. पशुः (c01.l03) occurs twice in X4b, so X4 is reported both ways: without it 10 of
  29 (1 in 2.9). X4a is anuṣṭubh, both odd pādas pathyā (0.24a हा हा पुत्रक नाधीतं;
  0.24c तेन त्वं विदुषां मध्ये), with upamā (पङ्के गौरिव); X4b is an upajāti with a rūpaka
  (साक्षात्पशुः पुच्छविषाणहीनः) — both identifications in the key are the course's, by Mammaṭa's definitions. Task B
  also counts the gāyatrī (can-do 5).
- **Payoff:** a new verse scanned, named and read in one sitting.
- **S0 → next:** +1 day (c05.l01): X4a's anvaya; +1 week (c05.l02): scan X4b again.

---

### 4.5 Chapter 5 — रचना समापनं च · Composition & Capstone

**Goals.** Write connected Sanskrit: a sentence by a stated procedure, a paragraph of narrative, a
verse quarter, a śloka; check it by a procedure the learner can run alone; then pass the capstone,
which composes, recites, derives and translates.

**The checklist (printed once, used in every c05 lesson and the capstone; what the course can and
cannot check).**
- **Self-checkable (the course gives the method):** (1) every noun and adjective: gender, number,
  case named, and the case's job named (Volume 3's roles map); (2) every verb: lakāra, person,
  number, pada, and agreement with its subject; (3) every participle, absolutive, absolute: its
  doer named, and (for an absolutive) the same doer as the main verb; (4) every relative has its
  correlative; (5) the pause form written, then joined by the sandhi maps of v3.c04, every seam
  named; (6) for verse: the scansion written above the line and the pāda types named; (7) the anvaya
  of the learner's own verse written and checked (every word once); (8) every word the learner did
  not learn in the course looked up, and its dictionary entry noted (Apte or MW, s.v.).
- **Not self-checkable, said plainly:** whether the Sanskrit says what the writer meant, whether a
  word is idiomatic, whether the verse is good. For these the lesson asks for a reader — a peer,
  a teacher, or the learner a week later reading it back as a stranger's text — and gives the
  reader a three-question form (What does it say? What would you change? What did you not
  understand?). No answer key pretends to mark them.
- **The checklist is the toolkit of §1.4.8 in order:** items (1)–(3) are K2–K4, item (5) is K5,
  items (6)–(7) are K6 and the anvaya check, item (8) is the lookup of c02.l02; K7 (retroversion,
  c05.l01), K8 (cold back-translation at least 24 hours later, from c05.l02, never inside a timed
  sitting) and K9 (the reader) complete it. TRADITION supports the delay: Rājaśekhara has the poet
  examine in the afternoon, "alone or in a small circle", what he composed in the morning, because
  "one who composes under the sway of feeling has no discerning eye" (W9, c05.l04).

**Volume 4 §6 row carried:** "The composition formula (pp.130–131) → v5.c05". **1.x assets:**
the composition formula (pp.130–131), corrected and extended (l01); the "Capstone" (pp.134–137),
replaced (l05; IR-043).

**Shape of the capstone:** §4.5 v5.c05.l05.

#### v5.c05.l01 — वाक्यरचना · "Writing a Sentence"

- **lesson_type:** grammar. **Station 0:** c04.l05 +1 day; c04.l04 +1 week. **review_of:**
  v3.c02.l05 (roles map), v3.c04.l04 (joining), v4.c05 (voice, causative).
- **The one teaching point:** the composition procedure, eight steps: (1) the event and its
  participants; (2) the voice (active, passive, causative) and therefore which participant is in
  the nominative; (3) each participant's role and case; (4) the verb: lakāra, person, number, pada;
  (5) the qualifiers, agreeing; (6) the subordinate events as participles, absolutives or absolutes;
  (7) the word order (verb last as a default, not a rule; the topic first); (8) sandhi last, from the
  pause form. Then the checklist.
- **The 1.x formula corrected in the open (pp.130–131):** its five steps are kept as steps 1, 3, 4,
  5 and 8. Corrected: "Verb usually ends the sentence" stays as the procedure's default (step 7), not as a claim
  about the texts; "Sanskrit word order is FREE" is qualified: the endings make other orders grammatical, and the
  texts use them, as the course shows on named sentences rather than by a count (P1's कस्मिंश्चिद्वने भासुरको
  नाम सिंहः प्रतिवसति स्म and P4's तस्य कोटरे कृष्णसर्पः प्रतिवसति स्म end on the verb; P4's अस्ति
  कस्मिंश्चिद्वनोद्देशे … वटपादपः opens with it);
  its example "साधवः छात्राः पठन्ति" is kept; "ब्राह्मणाय अन्नं देहि" is kept. Withdrawn: "With ~200
  vocabulary words + 20 verb roots + the 5-step formula, you can compose original Sanskrit sentences
  for any everyday situation" (a claim of scale with no test).
- **The first test: retroversion (K7, from the design "skill first").** Six sentences of P1–P4
  are given in the course's English; the learner writes each in Sanskrit through the eight steps,
  then marks it against the original with the K2–K5 list. A different but correct choice (word
  order; an active for a passive) is a note, not an error: the original is one good answer, and the
  learner sees that the procedure reproduces the authors' sentences (e.g. P4's … सर्पेण भक्षिताः,
  steps 2–3).
- **Model sentences (course-written, built with the procedure):**
  1. शशको मृगैः प्रेरितः सिंहस्य समीपं ययौ। — शशकः मृगैः प्रेरितः सिंहस्य समीपम् ययौ। "Sent by
     the animals, the hare went to the lion." (प्रेरितः glossed, as in P1's bridge)
  2. सिंहं कूपं दर्शयित्वा शशको गृहमगच्छत्। — सिंहम् कूपम् दर्शयित्वा शशकः गृहम् अगच्छत्।
     "Having shown the lion the well, the hare went home."
  3. पश्यतो बकस्य सर्पः सुतानभक्षयत्, किन्तु बको न किमप्यकरोत्। — पश्यतः बकस्य सर्पः सुतान्
     अभक्षयत्, किन्तु बकः न किम् अपि अकरोत्। "The snake ate the young while the heron looked on, but the
     heron did nothing."
  4. अवश्यं गुरोर्वचनं श्रोतव्यम्। — अवश्यम् गुरोः वचनम् श्रोतव्यम्। "The teacher's word must
     certainly be heard."
  5. रचनां रचयित्वा पुनः पठ। — रचनाम् रचयित्वा पुनः पठ। "Having composed your composition, read
     it again."
- **New headwords (4; 733 → 737):** रचना racanā f. ā "composition; arrangement" · रचयति racayati
  "composes, arranges" (class 10; MW) · किन्तु kintu indecl. "but" (Hit DCS 24; rule 29) · अवश्यम्
  avaśyam indecl. "certainly, necessarily" (Hit DCS 11).
- **Production:** ten English sentences into Sanskrit through the eight steps, each with its step
  table and checklist; then four sentences of the learner's own about their day.
- **Payoff:** the 1.x formula, kept where it was right, now produces the sentences of the texts.
- **Main risk:** composing in English word order. Fix: step 7 is written as a separate line before
  joining.
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: five sentences by the procedure; +1 week (c05.l03): the checklist run on
  them by a reader.

#### v5.c05.l02 — अनुच्छेदः · "Writing a Paragraph"

- **lesson_type:** grammar. **Station 0:** c05.l01 +1 day; c04.l05 +1 week. **review_of:** c02
  (the narrators' connectives), c05.l01.
- **The one teaching point:** a paragraph holds together by four devices the texts use: the
  connective at the head of a sentence (अथ, ततः, तदा, अनन्तरम्, यतः, किन्तु, अन्यथा); the pronoun
  that picks up the last-named person (सः, असौ ◐, अयम्); the absolutive chain and the absolutes
  for the background; one past tense kept through the paragraph (the narrator's choice of
  imperfect or perfect; the -त participle for the event that counts). The learner retells P4 in
  eight to ten sentences, then a story of their own.
- **Model paragraph (course-written, joined; pause form beneath in the lesson; 9 sentences):**
  एकस्मिन्वृक्षे बहवो बका न्यवसन्। तस्य कोटरे कृष्णसर्पोऽपि वसति स्म। स पश्यतां बकानां तेषां
  सुतानभक्षयत्। अथैको बको दुःखितः सरस्तीरमगच्छत्। तत्र कुलीरकस्तमपृच्छत्। बकस्तस्मै
  सर्वमकथयत्। कुलीरकोऽचिन्तयत् — "अयं बको मम शत्रुः; अपायोऽस्य करणीयः" इति। अनन्तरं तेनोपायः
  कथितः, किन्तु तस्मिन्नुपाये बकानामपायोऽप्यासीत्। सम्प्रति सर्वथा प्राज्ञ उपायमपायं च
  चिन्तयेत्। — (कुलीरकः "crab" glossed: P4 tells the crab's part in English; दुःखितः L. One past is kept, the
  imperfect, with कथितः for the event that counts; वसति स्म is the narrators' स्म frame, the one present the
  tense line allows, main risk below.)
- **New headwords (6; 737 → 743):** अनन्तरम् anantaram indecl. "then, afterwards" (Hit DCS 13;
  X-b's gloss of Volume 4) · अन्यथा anyathā indecl. "otherwise" (Hit DCS 13) · सर्वथा sarvathā
  indecl. "in every way, by all means" (Hit DCS 22) · सम्प्रति samprati indecl. "now" (Hit DCS 11)
  · यद्यपि yadyapi indecl. "even if, although" (rule 29; तथापि its partner, 0) · अधुना adhunā
  indecl. "now" (Hit DCS 15; rule 24: X5 also has it, reported both ways).
- **Production:** the P4 retelling; then a five-sentence end to X2's story (moved here from the c02.l05 exam,
  principle 11); then a paragraph of eight sentences on a story from Volumes 2–4
  (the crow, the lion and the mouse, Vīravara), each run through the checklist, and the reader form;
  the next day, the paragraph back-translated cold, without notes (K8), and every place where the
  English differs from what was meant marked and revised.
- **Payoff:** the serial habit of Volumes 3–4 (retelling the original in the learner's grammar)
  becomes the learner's own.
- **Main risk:** tense drift (present and past mixed without the historical present's frame). Fix:
  the tense line of the checklist: one past for events, the present only in speech or in प्रतिवसति
  स्म frames.
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: one paragraph revised from its reader form; +1 week (c05.l04): connectives
  drill.

#### v5.c05.l03 — पादरचना · "Writing a Verse Quarter"

- **lesson_type:** grammar. **Station 0:** c05.l02 +1 day; c05.l01 +1 week. **review_of:**
  c04.l01–l02, c01 (the verses as models).
- **The one teaching point:** an anuṣṭubh pāda is built from its fixed end back: syllables 5–8 of
  an even pāda must scan ⏑ − ⏑ ×, of an odd pāda (pathyā) ⏑ − − ×; the learner keeps a "weight
  bank" of owned words by shape (e.g. ⏑ − ⏑ ×: कदाचन, समागतः; ⏑ − − ×: विवेकेन, अनर्थाय), chooses
  the cadence word first, then fills syllables 1–4 (where only syllables 2–4 must not be ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ or
  ⏑ ⏑ −, KedV 2.21's नाद्यान्नसौ), and lets sandhi across the seam alter weight (the check of
  c04.l01).
- **Models:** the pādas of S1 and S2 taken apart and rebuilt with different words of the same
  shape (e.g. सर्वस्य लोचनं शास्त्रं → सर्वस्य लोचनं विद्या, which keeps − − ⏑ − ⏑ − − ×).
- **Model pādas (course-written; scansion beneath in the lesson):**
  1. विद्यया शोभते नरः — − ⏑ − − ⏑ − ⏑ × (even pāda: 5–7 ⏑ − ⏑) "A man shines by learning."
  2. मित्रेण सह गच्छति — − − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ × (even; the eighth syllable either way) "goes with a friend."
  3. सदा सत्यं वदेत्प्राज्ञः — ⏑ − − − ⏑ − − × (odd, pathyā) "The wise should always speak the
     truth."
  4. काव्यं गद्यं च पद्यं च — − − − − ⏑ − − × (odd, pathyā) "poetry, both prose and verse".
- **New headwords (4; 743 → 747):** काव्यम् kāvyam n. a "poetry; a poem" · पद्यम् padyam n. a
  "verse (composition in metre)" · गद्यम् gadyam n. a "prose" · वर्णयति varṇayati "describes"
  (class 10; MW).
- **Production:** eight pādas (four even, four odd) on given prose sentences, each scanned and
  named; then two pāda pairs of the learner's own.
- **Payoff:** the scanning of c04 turns from reading into making.
- **Main risk:** forcing a word into the wrong number or case to fit the metre. Fix: the checklist
  runs before the scansion; a pāda that scans but breaks agreement fails.
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: four pādas; +1 week (c05.l05): the weight bank revised.

#### v5.c05.l04 — श्लोकरचना · "Writing a Śloka"

- **lesson_type:** grammar. **Station 0:** c05.l03 +1 day; c05.l02 +1 week. **review_of:** c05.l03,
  c04.l04 (upamā), c01.l02 (the relative verse).
- **The one teaching point:** a gnomic śloka is composed prose-first: the learner writes the
  thought as a prose sentence pair with an upamā (यथा … तथा … or … इव), then distributes it over two
  half-verses so that each pāda ends on its cadence, then checks it three ways: the scansion (four
  pādas named), the anvaya (it must give back the prose sentence), and the checklist. The same
  method runs backwards from a **given fourth pāda** (samasyā, the tradition's exercise of
  completing a set quarter; from the design "tradition first"): the given pāda fixes the
  thought's end and its weight; the learner writes the prose that leads to it, then the three
  pādas before it. A fixed target makes the result partly self-checkable (K6, K2–K5).
- **Models:** S2b and S3c (the upamā verses of c01) — the learner writes their prose first, then
  shows how the poet ordered it for the metre.
- **Worked example (course-written śloka, labelled ours; scansion and anvaya in the lesson):**
  यथा सूर्यस्तमो हन्ति तथा विद्या तमो हरेत्। विवेकेन विना लोके नरः पशुसमः सदा॥ — pause: यथा सूर्यः
  तमः हन्ति तथा विद्या तमः हरेत्। विवेकेन विना लोके नरः पशु-समः सदा॥ — "As the sun kills darkness,
  so learning should take darkness away. Without discernment a man in this world is always like an animal." (An
  upamā with यथा … तथा: upameya विद्या, upamāna सूर्यः, the common property, the removal of तमः, named in each
  half (हन्ति, हरेत्); every word owned: तमः c03.l04, हरति Volume 4. Pādas, by script: ⏑ − − − ⏑ − − × pathyā ·
  ⏑ − − − ⏑ − ⏑ × · ⏑ − − ⏑ ⏑ − − × pathyā · ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ ×.)
- **New headwords (4; 747 → 751):** सुभाषितम् subhāṣitam n. a "well-said verse, saying" (the
  chapter word of c01, a lexicalised -त) · उपदेशः upadeśaḥ m. a "instruction, teaching" (the
  Hitopadeśa's second member) · सुन्दरः sundaraḥ adj. -री -रम् "beautiful" · रमणीयः ramaṇīyaḥ adj.
  -या -यम् "delightful" (a lexicalised -अनीय, rule 28).
- **Production:** (a) a śloka of the learner's own on a set theme (friendship; learning), with
  prose, scansion, anvaya and checklist; (b) a śloka completing the samasyā दुःखानि च सुखानि च
  (W10, Hit 1.166d, a verse in no volume); only then is the Hitopadeśa's verse shown, as one good
  answer, not a key (सुखमापतितं सेव्यं दुःखमापतितं तथा। चक्रवत्परिवर्तन्ते दुःखानि च सुखानि च॥); (c)
  one of the two sent to a reader with the form, and back-translated cold the next day (K8).
- **§17 (TRADITION, W9; Teacher Notes give the line):** Rājaśekhara's poet's day — in the fourth
  part of it, "alone or in a small circle, the examination of the poem made in the morning", since
  "one who composes under the sway of feeling has no discerning eye; therefore let him examine it
  afterwards" (Kāvyamīmāṃsā, `rajkmimu.htm` 1566–1567; the e-text names no edition). Attributed,
  not assessed: the course's K8 and K9 are the same advice.
- **Payoff:** the learner's first verse — scanned by the rule of c04.l01, ordered by the anvaya of
  c01.l01, adorned by the upamā of c04.l04.
- **Main risk:** a verse that scans but needs its own anvaya to be understood by no one but the
  writer. Fix: the reader form's first question, "What does it say?"
- **S0 → next:** +1 day: revise one śloka; +1 week (c05.l05): recite it.

#### v5.c05.l05 — समापनम् · "The Capstone"

- **lesson_type:** review. **review_of:** the whole volume, and v4.c06.l04. Ledger stays 751.
- **What it replaces (IR-043, in the open):** the 1.x capstone, "You Are a Sanskrit Expert"
  (pp.134–137), whose hardest question was "What is the Sanskrit phrase for 'knowledge'?" and which
  closed on "5,000-year-old tradition. 4,000 sūtras of Pāṇini" — two statements, sorted apart in task E: the age
  is a banned claim (no source); "about 4,000 sūtras" rounds the 3,983 records of the Aṣṭādhyāyī data file that
  Volume 4 counts, and is FACT with that count and method.
- **Shape: three sittings (45 + 45 + 25 minutes); 100 points; pass 70, with at least half the
  points of each of A–D** (so that the course is not passed on one skill); a strand failed is
  retaken alone, with a new text or theme of the same kind.

  *Sitting 1 — compose and recite (45 min).*
  - **A — compose (25 points; 30 min).** (i) **The serial's last episode, the learner's own**: a
    paragraph of eight sentences, joined, with pause form, that retells P2 and ends the story of
    the monkey and the crocodile, labelled "our retelling, after Pañcatantra 4". It must contain a
    perfect, a periphrastic perfect, a locative or genitive absolute, an absolutive chain, a
    passive and a causative (checklist items scored: 12). (ii) **A śloka on a samasyā**: the
    learner completes the given fourth pāda धर्माय च सुखाय च (W11, Hit 4.138d; the verse is in no
    volume and is shown only after the papers are in), with its scansion, pāda types and anvaya
    (scored: metre 5, anvaya 4, grammar 4). Not scored, and said so: style.
  - **B — recite (15 points; 15 min, recorded).** V1–V4 from memory, in metre; the learner's own
    śloka; and X6 (below) read at sight, then scanned on paper. Scored: correct text (5); the pāda ends and the
    weights marked by the learner on their own transcript of the recording (5); X6's scansion (5). Audibility
    itself is not scored (Q10): the course has no way to check it.

  *Sitting 2 — translate (45 min).*
  - **D — translate (35 points).** X5 (Hit 2, story 2, the washerman's donkey and dog; 78 words;
    21 not owned at the exit ledger, 1 in 3.7; **no marginal glosses**: the course glossary of
    Volumes 1–5 and a printed **Apte extract** with the entries the passage needs, श्वानः and जागृ among them,
    and 10 decoys; course glosses: none, since the taught procedure reaches every word not owned, `work/aptecheck.py`;
    the donkey's reply and his angry resolve, 1499–1511, are given in one English bridge, so that the bray of 1513
    follows the donkey's speech, as in the e-text)
    — translation (15), eight questions on who did what and why (8), parsing of every finite verb
    and participle (7); and X6 (Hit 2.36; 2 of 13 not owned): pause form, anvaya, translation (5).
    The last sentence of X5 stops before the end of the story: one question asks the learner to
    say, in Sanskrit, what they think happens next (scored for grammar only).

  *Sitting 3 — derive and sort (25 min).*
  - **C — derive (20 points; 15 min). "Derive, according to Pāṇini, X"**, X drawn by lot from two
    forms met in X5 and **not rehearsed** in any exam: कृत्वा (X5: कृ + क्त्वा, 3.4.21, with the steps of the
    Volume 4 c04.l02 key: 1.3.8 and 1.3.9 strip the marker, 1.1.5 blocks guṇa before the k-marked suffix) or
    प्रसुप्तः (X5: प्र + स्वप् + क्त, 3.2.102; 1.1.26 names क्त निष्ठा; 6.1.15 वचिस्वपियजादीनां किति, samprasāraṇa,
    as in v4.c04.l01's उक्त). The learner's own verb is withdrawn as an option (its sūtras cannot be listed in
    advance). Format of the Volume 4 plan §1.4.2, on the capstone card printed in §4.0.8 (the union of the two
    derivations' sūtras in number order, with as many distractors, every number on the Volume 4 toolbox card).
    Scored as v4.c06.l04 task C, per sūtra placed and for the order, scaled to 20.
  - **E — sort (5 points; 5 min).** Eight statements to be labelled FACT, TRADITION, HISTORY,
    legend or banned claim, and two source lines to be given; among them "The 1.x book wrote: '5,000-year-old
    tradition' (p.137)" — banned claim (no source); "Pāṇini's grammar has about 4,000 sūtras" — FACT, with the
    course's count of 3,983 records and its method (Volume 4); "the odd pāda of the anuṣṭubh has ⏑ − −
    in 88.4% of the Gītā's half-verses" — FACT, with its count; "according to Kedāra the vipulās
    are four" — TRADITION; "Mammaṭa wrote in the 11th century CE" — HISTORY, hedged, with its key (`kavyaprakasha`);
    and the source lines of two texts read ("Hit 0.25", "MBh 3.50.1"). Scored: half a point for each of the eight
    labels and each of the two source lines.
  - **After the sittings (not scored):** the learner's paragraph of A(i) back-translated cold, at
    least 24 hours later (K8), and given to a reader with the three-question form (K9).
- **Measured both ways (rule 24):** X5 reads 21 of 78 (1 in 3.7) at the exit ledger; 25 of 78
  (1 in 3.1) without the Volume 5 headwords it contains (आहारः in आहारदाने, चिरम् in चिरात्, अधुना, अनन्तरम् in
  तदनन्तरम्; निद्रा is in गाढनिद्रायाम्, already counted for गाढ-). Course help after Apte: 0 of 78
  (`work/aptecheck.py`). X6 reads 2 of 13 (1 in 6.5); without the Volume 5 headword it contains (जीवितम्, ×2:
  जीविते, जीवितम्) 4 of 13 (1 in 3.2). No headword was chosen for X5 or X6; neither text is read in Volumes 3–4.
- **Payoff:** the four strands of the course, each in its own task: the sound and script of Volume 1
  in the recitation, the words and sentences of Volumes 2–3 in the paragraph, the machine of Volume
  4 in the derivation, the texts of Volume 5 in the translation.
- **§21 (homework, not scored): what next** — the learner chooses one row of §6 and writes, in
  Sanskrit, three sentences on why.
- **S0:** none: the course ends here. §20 gives the learner a self-review calendar for the
  memorised verses (+1 month, +3 months), as a habit, not as a course item.

## 5. The reading texts in full

> Size note: the pause-form line (sandhi undone, word by word) is omitted for every text; it is mechanical output of `work/render.py` from the IAST line, and the fact bases print it.


Generated from `work/texts_state.py` by `sec6.py`: the joined Devanagari is made from the e-text line by the §3.1 rule; the pause form, anvaya and translation are the course's; statuses are set by hand; ratios and scansions by script. Prose passages print the narrative verses they contain with the prose. Each text is printed once; lessons refer to its label.

### 5.1 Chapter 1 texts

**S1a** — Hit 0.10 · `hitop_u.htm` 133-134

> अनेकसंशयोच्छेदि परोक्षार्थस्य दर्शकम्। सर्वस्य लोचनं शास्त्रं यस्य नास्त्यन्ध एव सः॥
>
> *aneka-saṃśayocchedi parokṣārthasya darśakam | sarvasya locanaṃ śāstraṃ yasya nāsty andha eva saḥ ||*


Anvaya (ours): शास्त्रम् अनेक-संशय-उच्छेदि परोक्ष-अर्थस्य दर्शकम् सर्वस्य लोचनम् (अस्ति)। यस्य (तत्) न अस्ति सः अन्धः एव॥

Translation (ours): "Learning cuts away many a doubt, shows what lies beyond sight, and is the eye of all: whoever lacks it is simply blind."

Glossed (G): अनेक-संशय-उच्छेदि "cutting off many doubts" (ucchedin, an agent in -in of ud + √chid: G; saṃśayaḥ is the lesson's headword).
Recognised (L): दर्शकम् (the agent in -aka of दृश्, as कारकः).
**Gloss ratio:** 1 of 12 words (1 in 12.0), names excluded; without the lesson's own headwords 4 of 12 (1 in 3.0).

Scansion (script `work/scan5.py`, checked by hand; the last syllable of a pāda is printed ×, either weight, §1.4.4; odd-pāda type by syllables 5–7): ⏑ − ⏑ − ⏑ − − × pathyā / ⏑ − − − ⏑ − ⏑ × · − − ⏑ − ⏑ − − × pathyā / − ⏑ − − ⏑ − ⏑ ×.

**S1b** — Hit 0.11 · `hitop_u.htm` 136-137

> यौवनं धनसम्पत्तिः प्रभुत्वमविवेकिता। एकैकमप्यनर्थाय किमु यत्र चतुष्टयम्॥
>
> *yauvanaṃ dhana-sampattiḥ prabhutvam avivekitā | ekaikam apy anarthāya kim u yatra catuṣṭayam ||*


Anvaya (ours): यौवनम् धन-सम्पत्तिः प्रभुत्वम् अविवेकिता (च) — एक-एकम् अपि अनर्थाय (भवति)। यत्र चतुष्टयम् (तत्र) किम् उ॥

Translation (ours): "Youth, wealth in plenty, power, want of judgement: each one alone makes for ruin — what then where all four come together?"

Glossed (G): धन-सम्पत्तिः "abundance of wealth" (sampattiḥ f. "success, plenty"); अनर्थाय "for harm, ruin" (an-arthaḥ "what is not good", lexicalised); चतुष्टयम् "set of four" (taddhita -taya on catur, not taught).
Recognised (L): प्रभुत्वम् (prabhuḥ); अविवेकिता (a- + vivekin); एक-एकम् ("each single one"); किम् (kim u: the lesson's chunk किमु).
**Gloss ratio:** 3 of 11 words (1 in 3.7), names excluded; without the lesson's own headwords 7 of 11 (1 in 1.6).

Scansion (script `work/scan5.py`, checked by hand; the last syllable of a pāda is printed ×, either weight, §1.4.4; odd-pāda type by syllables 5–7): − ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ − − × pathyā / ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ × · − − ⏑ − ⏑ − − × pathyā / ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ ×.

**S2a** — Hit 0.12 · `hitop_u.htm` 141-142

> कोऽर्थः पुत्रेण जातेन यो न विद्वान्न धार्मिकः। काणेन चक्षुषा किं वा चक्षुः पीडैव केवलम्॥
>
> *ko 'rthaḥ putreṇa jātena yo na vidvān na dhārmikaḥ | kāṇena cakṣuṣā kiṃ vā cakṣuḥ pīḍaiva kevalam ||*


Anvaya (ours): यः (पुत्रः) न विद्वान् न धार्मिकः (तेन) जातेन पुत्रेण कः अर्थः। काणेन चक्षुषा वा किम् (अर्थः)। (तत्) चक्षुः केवलम् पीडा एव॥

Translation (ours): "What use is a son, once he is born, who is neither learned nor righteous? What use is a blind eye? Such an eye is nothing but pain."

Glossed (G): काणेन "blind (in one eye)".
Recognised (L): जातेन (-ta of jāyate); चक्षुषा (instr. of cakṣus).
**Gloss ratio:** 1 of 17 words (1 in 17.0), names excluded; without the lesson's own headwords 6 of 17 (1 in 2.8).

Scansion (script `work/scan5.py`, checked by hand; the last syllable of a pāda is printed ×, either weight, §1.4.4; odd-pāda type by syllables 5–7): − − − − ⏑ − − × pathyā / − ⏑ − − ⏑ − ⏑ × · − − ⏑ − ⏑ − − × pathyā / − − − − ⏑ − ⏑ ×.

**S2b** — Hit 0.37 · `hitop_u.htm` 237-238

> माता शत्रुः पिता वैरी येन बालो न पाठितः। न शोभते सभामध्ये हंसमध्ये बको यथा॥
>
> *mātā śatruḥ pitā vairī yena bālo na pāṭhitaḥ | na śobhate sabhā-madhye haṃsa-madhye bako yathā ||*


Anvaya (ours): येन (मात्रा पित्रा वा) बालः न पाठितः (सा) माता शत्रुः (सः) पिता वैरी। (सः बालः) सभा-मध्ये न शोभते यथा हंस-मध्ये बकः (न शोभते)॥

Translation (ours): "A mother is an enemy and a father a foe by whom a child was not taught: he does not shine in an assembly, as a heron does not shine among swans."

Glossed (G): वैरी "enemy" (vaira + -in: the formation is taught, but vaira is not owned).
Recognised (L): पाठितः (-ta of the causative pāṭhayati).
**Gloss ratio:** 1 of 14 words (1 in 14.0), names excluded; without the lesson's own headwords 3 of 14 (1 in 4.7).

**S2c** — Hit 0.45 · `hitop_u.htm` 271-272

> यथोदयगिरेर्द्रव्यं सन्निकर्षेण दीप्यते। तथा सत्सन्निधानेन हीनवर्णोऽपि दीप्यते॥
>
> *yathodaya-girer dravyaṃ sannikarṣeṇa dīpyate | tathā sat-sannidhānena hīna-varṇo 'pi dīpyate ||*


Anvaya (ours): यथा उदय-गिरेः सन्निकर्षेण द्रव्यम् दीप्यते तथा सत्-सन्निधानेन हीन-वर्णः अपि दीप्यते॥

Translation (ours): "As a thing shines from being near the Mountain of Sunrise, so even a man of low standing shines from being near the good."

Glossed (G): सन्निकर्षेण "by nearness"; सत्-सन्निधानेन "by the presence of the good" (sannidhānam "nearness"); दीप्यते "shines, blazes" (×2; Ā class 4); द्रव्यम् "a thing, substance" (dravyam: not owned, no Volume 5 headword).
**Gloss ratio:** 5 of 10 words (1 in 2.0), names excluded; without the lesson's own headwords 7 of 10 (1 in 1.4).
