**S3a** — Hit 0.15 · `hitop_u.htm` 153-154

> स जातो येन जातेन याति वंशः समुन्नतिम्। परिवर्तिनि संसारे मृतः को वा न जायते॥
>
> *sa jāto yena jātena yāti vaṃśaḥ samunnatim | parivartini saṃsāre mṛtaḥ ko vā na jāyate ||*


Anvaya (ours): परिवर्तिनि संसारे मृतः कः वा न जायते। सः जातः येन जातेन वंशः समुन्नतिम् याति॥

Translation (ours): "He alone is truly born by whose birth his family rises high: in the turning round of the world, who that has died is not born again?"

Glossed (G): समुन्नतिम् "to eminence" (sam-unnatiḥ); परिवर्तिनि "turning, revolving" (loc. of the -in adjective parivartin).
Recognised (L): जातः (-ta of jāyate); जातेन (-ta of jāyate).
**Gloss ratio:** 2 of 14 words (1 in 7.0), names excluded; without the lesson's own headwords 4 of 14 (1 in 3.5).

**S3b** — Hit 0.25 · `hitop_u.htm` 191-192

> आहारनिद्राभयमैथुनानि सामान्यमेतत्पशुभिर्नराणाम्। ज्ञानं नराणामधिको विशेषो ज्ञानेन हीनाः पशुभिः समानाः॥
>
> *āhāra-nidrā-bhaya-maithunāni sāmānyam etat paśubhir narāṇām | jñānaṃ narāṇām adhiko viśeṣo jñānena hīnāḥ paśubhiḥ samānāḥ ||*


Anvaya (ours): आहार-निद्रा-भय-मैथुनानि — एतत् नराणाम् पशुभिः सामान्यम् (अस्ति)। ज्ञानम् नराणाम् अधिकः विशेषः। ज्ञानेन हीनाः (नराः) पशुभिः समानाः॥

Translation (ours): "Food, sleep, fear and mating: this people have in common with animals. Knowledge is what people have over and above; without knowledge they are the equals of animals."

> यथा ह्येकेन चक्रेण न रथस्य गतिर्भवेत्। तथा पुरुषकारेण विना दैवं न सिद्ध्यति॥

Anvaya (ours): यथा हि एकेन चक्रेण रथस्य गतिः न भवेत् तथा पुरुष-कारेण विना दैवम् न सिद्ध्यति॥

**S4a** — Bhartṛhari, Nītiśataka 1.13 · `bharst_u.htm` 160-163

> येषां न विद्या न तपो न दानं ज्ञानं न शीलं न गुणो न धर्मः। ते मर्त्यलोके भुवि भारभूता मनुष्यरूपेण मृगाश्चरन्ति॥
>
> *yeṣāṃ na vidyā na tapo na dānaṃ jñānaṃ na śīlaṃ na guṇo na dharmaḥ | te martya-loke bhuvi bhāra-bhūtā manuṣya-rūpeṇa mṛgāś caranti ||*


Anvaya (ours): येषाम् विद्या न तपः न दानम् न ज्ञानम् न शीलम् न गुणः न धर्मः न (अस्ति) ते मर्त्य-लोके भुवि भार-भूताः (सन्तः) मनुष्य-रूपेण मृगाः चरन्ति॥

Translation (ours): "Those who have neither learning nor austerity nor generosity, neither knowledge nor good conduct, neither virtue nor dharma, are a burden on the earth in the world of mortals: beasts that walk about in human shape."

Glossed (G): मर्त्य-लोके "in the world of mortals".
**Gloss ratio:** 1 of 21 words (1 in 21.0), names excluded; without the lesson's own headwords 6 of 21 (1 in 3.5).

**S4b** — Bhartṛhari, Nītiśataka 1.60 · `bharst_u.htm` 348-351

> आरम्भगुर्वी क्षयिणी क्रमेण लघ्वी पुरा वृद्धिमती च पश्चात्। दिनस्य पूर्वार्धपरार्धभिन्ना छायेव मैत्री खलसज्जनानाम्॥
>
> *ārambha-gurvī kṣayiṇī krameṇa laghvī purā vṛddhimatī ca paścāt | dinasya pūrvārdha-parārdha-bhinnā chāyeva maitrī khala-saj-janānām ||*


Anvaya (ours): खल-सत्-जनानाम् [खलानाम् सत्-जनानाम् च] मैत्री दिनस्य पूर्व-अर्ध-पर-अर्ध-भिन्ना [पूर्व-अर्ध-पर-अर्धाभ्याम् भिन्ना] छाया इव आरम्भ-गुर्वी [आरम्भे गुर्वी] क्रमेण क्षयिणी पुरा लघ्वी पश्चात् वृद्धिमती च (भवति)॥

Reading note (the respective construction, c01.l04; the course's analysis): the first pair of predicates, आरम्भगुर्वी … क्रमेण क्षयिणी, belongs to खलानाम् and to the shadow of the forenoon (पूर्वार्ध-); the second, पुरा लघ्वी पश्चाद्वृद्धिमती, to सज्जनानाम् and to the shadow of the afternoon (परार्ध-).

Translation (ours): "Like the shadow of the first and of the second half of the day is the friendship of the wicked and of the good: the one heavy at the start and shrinking by degrees, the other slight at first and growing afterwards."

Glossed (G): क्षयिणी "waning" (f. of the -in adjective kṣayin); पूर्व-अर्ध-पर-अर्ध-भिन्ना "divided into the forenoon and the afternoon".
Recognised (L): लघ्वी — f. of the owned laghu (as gurvī) · वृद्धिमती — vṛddhiḥ (V4) + -mat (v4.c04.l03), f. -ī · खल-सत्-जनानाम् — khalaḥ (owned since v1.c03.l01) + sat-janaḥ (dvandva).
**Gloss ratio:** 2 of 14 words (1 in 7.0), names excluded; without the lesson's own headwords 5 of 14 (1 in 2.8).

**X1a** — Hit 0.23 · `hitop_u.htm` 183-184

> यस्य कस्य प्रसूतोऽपि गुणवान्पूज्यते नरः। धनुर्वंशविशुद्धोऽपि निर्गुणः किं करिष्यति॥
>
> *yasya kasya prasūto 'pi guṇavān pūjyate naraḥ | dhanur vaṃśa-viśuddho 'pi nirguṇaḥ kiṃ kariṣyati ||*


Anvaya (ours): यस्य कस्य (अपि कुले) प्रसूतः अपि गुणवान् नरः पूज्यते। वंश-विशुद्धः अपि निर्गुणः धनुः किम् करिष्यति॥

Translation (ours): "A man of merit is honoured, born of whoever he may be; a bow of flawless bamboo, if it has no string, what will it do?"

Glossed in the margin (G): प्रसूतः "born" (-ta of pra + sūte); धनुः "bow" (masculine here, agreeing with -viśuddhaḥ and nirguṇaḥ: MW dhanu m., citing Hit.; the common stem is neuter dhanus); पूज्यते "is honoured" (passive of pūjayati, not owned; Volume 4 glossed it).
Recognised (L): गुणवान् — guṇaḥ + -vat; here also "having a bowstring" (guṇaḥ "virtue; string": the double sense is glossed) · वंश-विशुद्धः — vaṃśaḥ (c01.l03, "family line") in a new sense, "bamboo" (rule 27, adds 0; glossed) + vi-śuddha "pure" · निर्गुणः — nir + guṇa.
**Gloss ratio:** 3 of 13 words not owned at the ledger of that point (1 in 4.3), names excluded; without the Volume 5 headwords it contains (वंशः, c01.l03, in वंशविशुद्धः, here in a new sense, glossed) 4 of 13 (1 in 3.2).

**X1b** — Bhartṛhari, Nītiśataka 1.41 · `bharst_u.htm` 272-275

> यस्यास्ति वित्तं स नरः कुलीनः स पण्डितः स श्रुतवान्गुणज्ञः। स एव वक्ता स च दर्शनीयः सर्वे गुणाः काञ्चनमाश्रयन्ति॥
>
> *yasyāsti vittaṃ sa naraḥ kulīnaḥ sa paṇḍitaḥ sa śrutavān guṇajñaḥ | sa eva vaktā sa ca darśanīyaḥ sarve guṇāḥ kāñcanam āśrayanti ||*


Anvaya (ours): यस्य वित्तम् अस्ति सः नरः कुलीनः सः पण्डितः सः श्रुतवान् गुण-ज्ञः सः एव वक्ता सः च दर्शनीयः (भवति)। सर्वे गुणाः काञ्चनम् आश्रयन्ति॥

Translation (ours): "Whoever has money is a man of good family; he is a pandit, learned and a judge of merit; he alone is an orator, and he is handsome: all virtues take refuge in gold."

Glossed in the margin (G): कुलीनः "of good family"; काञ्चनम् "gold"; आश्रयन्ति "resort to, take refuge in"; वित्तम् "wealth"; दर्शनीयः "handsome" (-anīya gerundive of dṛś: taught only in c02.l03).
Recognised (L): श्रुतवान् — -tavat of śṛṇoti (v4.c04.l01) · वक्ता — -tṛ agent of vakti (v4.c04.l02).
**Gloss ratio:** 5 of 21 words not owned at the ledger of that point (1 in 4.2), names excluded; no Volume 5 headword occurs in it.

### 5.2 Chapter 2 texts

**P1** — Pañcatantra 1, story 8 (Bhāsuraka), with its frame: the thesis verse, the opening and the end · `vispancu.htm` 1371 (end); 1373–1374; 1376; 1378; 1383 (to tṛptir bhavati); 1453 (from tataḥ so 'pi); 1455

> … उक्तं च — यस्य बुद्धिर्बलं तस्य निर्बुद्धेस्तु कुतो बलम्। वने सिंहो मदोन्मत्तः शशकेन निपातितः॥ करटक आह — कथमेतत्? स आह — कस्मिंश्चिद्वने भासुरको नाम सिंहः प्रतिवसति स्म। अथासौ वीर्यातिरेकान्नित्यमेवानेकान्मृगशशकादीन्व्यापादयन्नोपरराम। अथान्येद्युस्तद्वनजाः सर्वे सारङ्गवराहमहिषशशकादयो मिलित्वा तमभ्युपेत्य प्रोचुः — स्वामिन्! किमनेन सकलमृगवधेन नित्यमेव, यतस्तवैकेनापि मृगेण तृप्तिर्भवति … ततः सोऽपि मूर्खः सिंहः कूपमध्य आत्मप्रतिबिम्बं जलमध्यगतं दृष्ट्वा सिंहनादं मुमोच। ततः प्रतिशब्देन कूपमध्याद्द्विगुणतरो नादः समुत्थितः। अथ तेन तं शत्रुं मत्वात्मानं तस्योपरि प्रक्षिप्य प्राणाः परित्यक्ताः। शशकोऽपि हृष्टमनाः सर्वमृगानानन्द्य तैः सह प्रशस्यमानो यथासुखं तत्र वने निवसति स्म। अतोऽहं ब्रवीमि — यस्य बुद्धिर्बलं तस्य इति।
>
> *… uktaṃ ca — yasya buddhir balaṃ tasya nirbuddhes tu kuto balam | vane siṃho madonmattaḥ śaśakena nipātitaḥ || karaṭaka āha — katham etat ? sa āha — kasmiṃścid vane bhāsurako nāma siṃhaḥ prativasati sma | athāsau vīryātirekān nityam evānekān mṛga-śaśakādīn vyāpādayan nopararāma | athānyedyus tad-vanajāḥ sarve sāraṅga-varāha-mahiṣa-śaśakādayo militvā tam abhyupetya procuḥ — svāmin ! kim anena sakala-mṛga-vadhena nityam eva, yatas tavaikenāpi mṛgeṇa tṛptir bhavati … tataḥ so 'pi mūrkhaḥ siṃhaḥ kūpa-madhya ātma-pratibimbaṃ jala-madhya-gataṃ dṛṣṭvā siṃha-nādaṃ mumoca | tataḥ pratiśabdena kūpa-madhyād dvi-guṇataro nādaḥ samutthitaḥ | atha tena taṃ śatruṃ matvātmānaṃ tasyopari prakṣipya prāṇāḥ parityaktāḥ | śaśako 'pi hṛṣṭa-manāḥ sarva-mṛgān ānandya taiḥ saha praśasyamāno yathā-sukhaṃ tatra vane nivasati sma | ato 'haṃ bravīmi — yasya buddhir balaṃ tasya iti |*


Glossed (G): वीर्य-अतिरेकात् "from excess of strength" (vīryam glossed); सारङ्ग-वराह-महिष-शशक-आदयः "deer, boars, buffaloes, hares and the rest"; प्रतिशब्देन "by the echo"; द्वि-गुणतरः "twice as loud"; प्रशस्यमानः "being praised" (passive participle -yamāna, L only after c02.l03 §15); अन्येद्युः "next day"; मिलित्वा "having met, gathered" (milati; the owned chunk is milāmaḥ); उपरराम "stopped" (perfect 3sg of upa + ramate; ramate not owned); आनन्द्य "having gladdened" (causative of ā + nand, -ya: the c02.l01 card's formation on a verb not owned); मद-उन्मत्तः "mad with pride" (madaḥ owned; unmatta not owned).

**P2** — Pañcatantra 4, frame story: the escape · `vispancu.htm` 5293; 5295-5296; 5302

> तदाकर्ण्य मकरः सानन्दमाह — भद्र! यद्येवं तदर्पय मे हृदयम्। … अहं त्वां तमेव जम्बूपादपं प्रापयामि। एवमुक्त्वा निवर्त्य जम्बूतलमगात्। वानरोऽपि कथमपि जल्पितविविधदेवतोपचारपूजस्तीरमासादितवान्। ततश्च दीर्घतरचङ्क्रमणेन तमेव जम्बूपादपमारूढश्चिन्तयामास — अहो! लब्धास्तावत्प्राणाः। अथवा साध्विदमुच्यते — न विश्वसेदविश्वस्ते विश्वस्तेऽपि न विश्वसेत्। विश्वासाद्भयमुत्पन्नं मूलान्यपि निकृन्तति॥ … अथ विहस्य निर्भर्त्सयन्वानरस्तमाह — धिग्धिङ्मूर्ख विश्वासघातक! किं कस्यचिद्धृदयद्वयं भवति? तदाशु गम्यतां जम्बूवृक्षस्याधस्तान्न भूयोऽपि त्वयात्रागन्तव्यम्।
>
> *tad ākarṇya makaraḥ sānandam āha — bhadra ! yady evaṃ tad arpaya me hṛdayam | … ahaṃ tvāṃ tam eva jambū-pādapaṃ prāpayāmi | evam uktvā nivartya jambū-talam agāt | vānaro 'pi katham api jalpita-vividha-devatopacāra-pūjas tīram āsāditavān | tataś ca dīrghatara-caṅkramaṇena tam eva jambū-pādapam ārūḍhaś cintayāmāsa — aho ! labdhās tāvat prāṇāḥ | athavā sādhv idam ucyate — na viśvased aviśvaste viśvaste 'pi na viśvaset | viśvāsād bhayam utpannaṃ mūlāny api nikṛntati || … atha vihasya nirbhartsayan vānaras tam āha — dhig dhiṅ mūrkha viśvāsa-ghātaka ! kiṃ kasyacid dhṛdaya-dvayaṃ bhavati ? tad āśu gamyatāṃ jambū-vṛkṣasyādhastān na bhūyo 'pi tvayātrāgantavyam |*

> स्त्रियोक्तम् — अहमेतस्य शूद्रकस्य राजलक्ष्मीः। चिरादेतस्य भुजच्छायायां महता सुखेन विश्रान्ता। इदानीमन्यत्र गमिष्यामि। … लक्ष्मीरुवाच — यदि त्वमात्मनः पुत्रं शक्तिधरं द्वात्रिंशल्लक्षणोपेतं भगवत्याः सर्वमङ्गलाया उपहारीकरोषि, तदाहं पुनरत्र सुचिरं निवसामि। इत्युक्त्वादृश्याभवत्। … तत्र सर्वमङ्गलां सम्पूज्य वीरवरो ब्रूते — देवि! प्रसीद। विजयतां शूद्रको महाराजः। गृह्यतामयमुपहारः। इत्युक्त्वा पुत्रस्य शिरश्चिच्छेद। … जायन्ते च म्रियन्ते च मद्विधाः क्षुद्रजन्तवः। अनेन सदृशो लोके न भूतो न भविष्यति॥ … राजा च साष्टाङ्गपातं प्रणम्योवाच — देवि! किं मे राज्येन? जीवितेन वा मम किं प्रयोजनम्? यद्यहमनुकम्पनीयस्तदा ममायुःशेषेणाप्ययं सदारपुत्रो वीरवरो जीवतु।
>
> *striyoktam — aham etasya śūdrakasya rāja-lakṣmīḥ | cirād etasya bhuja-cchāyāyāṃ mahatā sukhena viśrāntā | idānīm anyatra gamiṣyāmi | … lakṣmīr uvāca — yadi tvam ātmanaḥ putraṃ śaktidharaṃ dvātriṃśal-lakṣaṇopetaṃ bhagavatyāḥ sarva-maṅgalāyā upahārīkaroṣi, tadāhaṃ punar atra suciraṃ nivasāmi | ity uktvādṛśyābhavat | … tatra sarvamaṅgalāṃ sampūjya vīravaro brūte — devi ! prasīda | vijayatāṃ śūdrako mahārājaḥ | gṛhyatām ayam upahāraḥ | ity uktvā putrasya śiraś ciccheda | … jāyante ca mriyante ca mad-vidhāḥ kṣudra-jantavaḥ | anena sadṛśo loke na bhūto na bhaviṣyati || … rājā ca sāṣṭāṅga-pātaṃ praṇamyovāca — devi ! kiṃ me rājyena ? jīvitena vā mama kiṃ prayojanam ? yady aham anukampanīyas tadā mamāyuḥ-śeṣeṇāpy ayaṃ sa-dāra-putro vīravaro jīvatu |*

> … पश्यतो बकमूर्खस्य नकुलैर्भक्षिताः सुताः॥ … अस्ति कस्मिंश्चिद्वनोद्देशे बहुबकसनाथो वटपादपः। तस्य कोटरे कृष्णसर्पः प्रतिवसति स्म। स च बकबालकानजातपक्षानपि सदैव भक्षयन्कालं नयति। … स आह — भद्र किं करोमि? मम मन्दभाग्यस्य बालकाः कोटरनिवासिना सर्पेण भक्षिताः। तद्दुःखदुःखितोऽहं रोदिमि। तत्कथय मे यद्यस्ति कश्चिदुपायस्तद्विनाशाय। … आह च — माम, यद्येवं तन्मत्स्यमांसखण्डानि नकुलस्य बिलद्वारात्सर्पकोटरं यावत्प्रक्षिप यथा नकुलस्तन्मार्गेण गत्वा तं दुष्टसर्पं विनाशयति। अथ तथानुष्ठिते मत्स्यमांसानुसारिणा नकुलेन तं कृष्णसर्पं निहत्य तेऽपि तद्वृक्षाश्रयाः सर्वे बकाश्च शनैः शनैर्भक्षिताः।
>
> *… paśyato baka-mūrkhasya nakulair bhakṣitāḥ sutāḥ || … asti kasmiṃścid vanoddeśe bahu-baka-sanātho vaṭa-pādapaḥ | tasya koṭare kṛṣṇa-sarpaḥ prativasati sma | sa ca baka-bālakān ajāta-pakṣān api sadaiva bhakṣayan kālaṃ nayati | … sa āha — bhadra kiṃ karomi ? mama manda-bhāgyasya bālakāḥ koṭara-nivāsinā sarpeṇa bhakṣitāḥ | tad-duḥkha-duḥkhito 'haṃ rodimi | tat kathaya me yady asti kaścid upāyas tad-vināśāya | … āha ca — māma, yady evaṃ tan matsya-māṃsa-khaṇḍāni nakulasya bila-dvārāt sarpa-koṭaraṃ yāvat prakṣipa yathā nakulas tan-mārgeṇa gatvā taṃ duṣṭa-sarpaṃ vināśayati | atha tathānuṣṭhite matsya-māṃsānusāriṇā nakulena taṃ kṛṣṇa-sarpaṃ nihatya te 'pi tad-vṛkṣāśrayāḥ sarve bakāś ca śanaiḥ śanair bhakṣitāḥ |*

English bridges (the course's; the crab's part, cut from the excerpt): at the second cut, "One heron, seeing his young eaten, stood weeping on the shore of the lake; a crab saw him and asked, 'Uncle, why are you weeping today?'" (2490, end); at the third, "The crab thought: 'This is our born enemy. I will give him advice, true and false together, that will destroy all the herons'" (2493–2496, with Panc 1.440, which is not printed). The speaker of स आह is the heron; of आह च, the crab.

> कस्मिंश्चित्कूपे गङ्गदत्तो नाम मण्डूकराजः प्रतिवसति स्म। … एवं चिन्तयन्बिले प्रविशन्तं कृष्णसर्पमपश्यत्। तं दृष्ट्वा भूयोऽप्यचिन्तयत् — यदेनं तत्र कूपे नीत्वा सकलदायादानामुच्छेदं करोमि। … एवं स विभाव्य बिलद्वारं गत्वा तमाहूतवान् — एह्येहि प्रियदर्शन! एहि! तच्छ्रुत्वा सर्पश्चिन्तयामास — य एवं मामाह्वयति। स्वजातीयो न भवति। यतो नैषा सर्पवाणी। अन्येन केनापि सह मम मर्त्यलोके सन्धानं नास्ति। तदत्रैव दुर्गे स्थितस्तावद्वेद्मि कोऽयं भविष्यति।
>
> *kasmiṃścit kūpe gaṅgadatto nāma maṇḍūka-rājaḥ prativasati sma | … evaṃ cintayan bile praviśantaṃ kṛṣṇasarpam apaśyat | taṃ dṛṣṭvā bhūyo 'py acintayat — yad enaṃ tatra kūpe nītvā sakala-dāyādānām ucchedaṃ karomi | … evaṃ sa vibhāvya bila-dvāraṃ gatvā tam āhūtavān — ehy ehi priya-darśana ! ehi ! tac chrutvā sarpaś cintayāmāsa — ya evaṃ mām āhvayati | svajātīyo na bhavati | yato naiṣā sarpa-vāṇī | anyena kenāpi saha mama martya-loke sandhānaṃ nāsti | tad atraiva durge sthitas tāvad vedmi ko 'yaṃ bhaviṣyati |*

### 5.3 Chapter 3 texts

**E1** — MBh 3.50.1, 5-10 (Nala; Bhīma and Damayantī) · `mbh_03_u.htm` 03,050.001a-010c

> आसीद्राजा नलो नाम वीरसेनसुतो बली। उपपन्नो गुणैरिष्टै रूपवानश्वकोविदः॥ … तथैवासीद्विदर्भेषु भीमो भीमपराक्रमः। शूरः सर्वगुणैर्युक्तः प्रजाकामः स चाप्रजः॥ स प्रजार्थे परं यत्नमकरोत्सुसमाहितः। तमभ्यगच्छद्ब्रह्मर्षिर्दमनो नाम भारत॥ तं स भीमः प्रजाकामस्तोषयामास धर्मवित्। महिष्या सह राजेन्द्र सत्कारेण सुवर्चसम्॥ तस्मै प्रसन्नो दमनः सभार्याय वरं ददौ। कन्यारत्नं कुमारांश्च त्रीनुदारान्महायशाः॥ दमयन्तीं दमं दान्तं दमनं च सुवर्चसम्। उपपन्नान्गुणैः सर्वैर्भीमान्भीमपराक्रमान्॥ दमयन्ती तु रूपेण तेजसा यशसा श्रिया। सौभाग्येन च लोकेषु यशः प्राप सुमध्यमा॥
>
> *āsīd rājā nalo nāma vīrasenasuto balī | upapanno guṇair iṣṭai rūpavān aśvakovidaḥ || … tathaivāsīd vidarbheṣu bhīmo bhīmaparākramaḥ | śūraḥ sarvaguṇair yuktaḥ prajākāmaḥ sa cāprajaḥ || sa prajārthe paraṃ yatnam akarot susamāhitaḥ | tam abhyagacchad brahmarṣir damano nāma bhārata || taṃ sa bhīmaḥ prajākāmas toṣayām āsa dharmavit | mahiṣyā saha rājendra satkāreṇa suvarcasam || tasmai prasanno damanaḥ sabhāryāya varaṃ dadau | kanyāratnaṃ kumārāṃś ca trīn udārān mahāyaśāḥ || damayantīṃ damaṃ dāntaṃ damanaṃ ca suvarcasam | upapannān guṇaiḥ sarvair bhīmān bhīmaparākramān || damayantī tu rūpeṇa tejasā yaśasā śriyā | saubhāgyena ca lokeṣu yaśaḥ prāpa sumadhyamā ||*

**E2** — MBh 3.50.17-19, 21-23, 25-26, 30-31 (Nala and the haṃsa) · `mbh_03_u.htm` 03,050.017a-031c

> अशक्नुवन्नलः कामं तदा धारयितुं हृदा। अन्तःपुरसमीपस्थे वन आस्ते रहोगतः॥ स ददर्श तदा हंसाञ्जातरूपपरिच्छदान्। वने विचरतां तेषामेकं जग्राह पक्षिणम्॥ ततोऽन्तरिक्षगो वाचं व्याजहार तदा नलम्। न हन्तव्योऽस्मि ते राजन्करिष्यामि हि ते प्रियम्॥ … एवमुक्तस्ततो हंसमुत्ससर्ज महीपतिः। ते तु हंसाः समुत्पत्य विदर्भानगमंस्ततः॥ विदर्भनगरीं गत्वा दमयन्त्यास्तदान्तिके। निपेतुस्ते गरुत्मन्तः सा ददर्शाथ तान्खगान्॥ सा तानद्भुतरूपान्वै दृष्ट्वा सखिगणावृता। हृष्टा ग्रहीतुं खगमांस्त्वरमाणोपचक्रमे॥ … दमयन्ती तु यं हंसं समुपाधावदन्तिके। स मानुषीं गिरं कृत्वा दमयन्तीमथाब्रवीत्॥ दमयन्ति नलो नाम निषधेषु महीपतिः। अश्विनोः सदृशो रूपे न समास्तस्य मानुषाः॥ … एवमुक्ता तु हंसेन दमयन्ती विशां पते। अब्रवीत्तत्र तं हंसं तमप्येवं नलं वद॥ तथेत्युक्त्वाण्डजः कन्यां वैदर्भस्य विशां पते। पुनरागम्य निषधान्नले सर्वं न्यवेदयत्॥
>
> *aśaknuvan nalaḥ kāmaṃ tadā dhārayituṃ hṛdā | antaḥpurasamīpasthe vana āste rahogataḥ || sa dadarśa tadā haṃsāñ jātarūpaparicchadān | vane vicaratāṃ teṣām ekaṃ jagrāha pakṣiṇam || tato 'ntarikṣago vācaṃ vyājahāra tadā nalam | na hantavyo 'smi te rājan kariṣyāmi hi te priyam || … evam uktas tato haṃsam utsasarja mahīpatiḥ | te tu haṃsāḥ samutpatya vidarbhān agamaṃs tataḥ || vidarbhanagarīṃ gatvā damayantyās tadāntike | nipetus te garutmantaḥ sā dadarśātha tān khagān || sā tān adbhutarūpān vai dṛṣṭvā sakhigaṇāvṛtā | hṛṣṭā grahītuṃ khagamāṃs tvaramāṇopacakrame || … damayantī tu yaṃ haṃsaṃ samupādhāvad antike | sa mānuṣīṃ giraṃ kṛtvā damayantīm athābravīt || damayanti nalo nāma niṣadheṣu mahīpatiḥ | aśvinoḥ sadṛśo rūpe na samās tasya mānuṣāḥ || … evam uktā tu haṃsena damayantī viśāṃ pate | abravīt tatra taṃ haṃsaṃ tam apy evaṃ nalaṃ vada || tathety uktvāṇḍajaḥ kanyāṃ vaidarbhasya viśāṃ pate | punar āgamya niṣadhān nale sarvaṃ nyavedayat ||*

**G1** — Bhagavadgītā 2.47-48 = MBh 6.24.47-48 (critical edition) · `mbh_06_u.htm` 06,024.047a-048c

> कर्मण्येवाधिकारस्ते मा फलेषु कदा चन। मा कर्मफलहेतुर्भूर्मा ते सङ्गोऽस्त्वकर्मणि॥ योगस्थः कुरु कर्माणि सङ्गं त्यक्त्वा धनंजय। सिद्ध्यसिद्ध्योः समो भूत्वा समत्वं योग उच्यते॥
>
> *karmaṇy evādhikāras te mā phaleṣu kadā cana | mā karmaphalahetur bhūr mā te saṅgo 'stv akarmaṇi || yogasthaḥ kuru karmāṇi saṅgaṃ tyaktvā dhanaṃjaya | siddhyasiddhyoḥ samo bhūtvā samatvaṃ yoga ucyate ||*


Anvaya (ours): ते अधिकारः कर्मणि एव (अस्ति)। फलेषु कदा चन (ते अधिकारः) मा (अस्तु)। (त्वम्) कर्म-फल-हेतुः मा भूः। ते अकर्मणि सङ्गः मा अस्तु॥ धनंजय योग-स्थः (सन्) सङ्गम् त्यक्त्वा सिद्धि-असिद्ध्योः समः भूत्वा कर्माणि कुरु। समत्वम् योगः उच्यते॥

Translation (ours; कर्मफलहेतुः read as the course's bahuvrīhi कर्मफलं हेतुर्यस्य सः, not Śaṅkara's tatpuruṣa, c03.l03): "Your claim is to the deed alone, never at any time to its fruits. Do not be one whose motive is the fruit of action; and let there be in you no attachment to not acting. Steadfast in discipline, do your deeds, Dhanaṃjaya, having let attachment go, the same in success and failure: sameness is called yoga."

**U1** — Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad 1.3.28 (the mantra and the Brāhmaṇa's gloss) · `brup___u.htm` 394–400

> असतो मा सद्गमय, तमसो मा ज्योतिर्गमय, मृत्योर्मामृतं गमयेति। स यदाहासतो मा सद्गमयेति। मृत्युर्वा असत्सदमृतं मृत्योर्मामृतं गमयामृतं मा कुर्वित्येवैतदाह। तमसो मा ज्योतिर्गमयेति। मृत्युर्वै तमो ज्योतिरमृतं मृत्योर्मामृतं गमयामृतं मा कुर्वित्येवैतदाह। मृत्योर्मामृतं गमयेति। नात्र तिरोहितमिवास्ति।
>
> *asato mā sad gamaya, tamaso mā jyotir gamaya, mṛtyor māmṛtaṃ gamayeti | sa yad āhāsato mā sad gamayeti | mṛtyur vā asat sad amṛtaṃ mṛtyor māmṛtaṃ gamayāmṛtaṃ mā kurv ity evaitad āha | tamaso mā jyotir gamayeti | mṛtyur vai tamo jyotir amṛtaṃ mṛtyor māmṛtaṃ gamayāmṛtaṃ mā kurv ity evaitad āha | mṛtyor māmṛtaṃ gamayeti | nātra tirohitam ivāsti |*


Translation (ours): "'From the unreal lead me to the real; from darkness lead me to light; from death lead me to the deathless.' When he says 'from the unreal lead me to the real', the unreal is death and the real is the deathless: 'from death lead me to the deathless; make me deathless' — that is what he says. 'From darkness lead me to light': darkness is death and light the deathless: 'from death lead me to the deathless; make me deathless' — that is what he says. 'From death lead me to the deathless': here there is nothing hidden, as it were." (मृत्युर्वा असत्: the e-text's वा, a particle of emphasis here, and its unjoined vowels, 396, printed as they stand.)

### 5.4 Chapter 4 texts

**M1** — Bhartṛhari, Nītiśataka 1.27 · `bharst_u.htm` 216-219

> प्रारभ्यते न खलु विघ्नभयेन नीचैः प्रारभ्य विघ्नविहता विरमन्ति मध्याः। विघ्नैः पुनः पुनरपि प्रतिहन्यमानाः प्रारब्धमुत्तमजना न परित्यजन्ति॥
>
> *prārabhyate na khalu vighna-bhayena nīcaiḥ prārabhya vighna-vihatā viramanti madhyāḥ | vighnaiḥ punaḥ punar api pratihanyamānāḥ prārabdham uttama-janā na parityajanti ||*


Anvaya (ours): नीचैः विघ्न-भयेन न खलु प्रारभ्यते। मध्याः प्रारभ्य विघ्न-विहताः (सन्तः) विरमन्ति। उत्तम-जनाः विघ्नैः पुनः पुनः अपि प्रतिहन्यमानाः प्रारब्धम् न परित्यजन्ति॥

Translation (ours): "The low do not begin at all, for fear of obstacles; the middling begin, and stop when obstacles strike them; the best, though struck by obstacles again and again, do not abandon what they have begun."

**X4b** — Bhartṛhari, Nītiśataka 1.12 · `bharst_u.htm` 156-159

> साहित्यसङ्गीतकलाविहीनः साक्षात्पशुः पुच्छविषाणहीनः। तृणं न खादन्नपि जीवमानस्तद्भागधेयं परमं पशूनाम्॥
>
> *sāhitya-saṅgīta-kalā-vihīnaḥ sākṣāt paśuḥ puccha-viṣāṇa-hīnaḥ | tṛṇaṃ na khādann api jīvamānas tad bhāga-dheyaṃ paramaṃ paśūnām ||*

### 5.5 Chapter 5 texts (the capstone)

**X5** — Hitopadeśa 2, story 2 (the washerman's donkey and dog) · `hitop_u.htm` 1495; 1497 (to mandādaraḥ); 1513 (first sentence); 1499–1511 in an English bridge

> अस्ति वाराणस्यां कर्पूरपटको नाम रजकः। स रात्रौ गाढनिद्रायां प्रसुप्तः। तदनन्तरं तद्गृहद्रव्याणि हर्तुं चौरः प्रविष्टः। तस्य प्राङ्गणे गर्दभो बद्धस्तिष्ठति। कुक्कुरश्चोपविष्टोऽस्ति। अथ गर्दभः श्वानमाह — सखे! भवतस्तावदयं व्यापारः। तत्किमिति त्वमुच्चैः शब्दं कृत्वा स्वामिनं न जागरयसि। कुक्कुरो ब्रूते — भद्र! मम नियोगस्य चर्चा त्वया न कर्तव्या। त्वमेव किं न जानासि यथा तस्याहर्निशं गृहरक्षां करोमि। यतोऽयं चिरान्निर्वृतो ममोपयोगं न जानाति। तेनाधुनापि ममाहारदाने मन्दादरः। … इत्युक्त्वातीव चीत्कारशब्दं कृतवान्।
>
> *asti vārāṇasyāṃ karpūra-paṭako nāma rajakaḥ | sa rātrau gāḍha-nidrāyāṃ prasuptaḥ | tad-anantaraṃ tad-gṛha-dravyāṇi hartuṃ cauraḥ praviṣṭaḥ | tasya prāṅgaṇe gardabho baddhas tiṣṭhati | kukkuraś copaviṣṭo 'sti | atha gardabhaḥ śvānam āha — sakhe ! bhavatas tāvad ayaṃ vyāpāraḥ | tat kim iti tvam uccaiḥ śabdaṃ kṛtvā svāminaṃ na jāgarayasi | kukkuro brūte — bhadra ! mama niyogasya carcā tvayā na kartavyā | tvam eva kiṃ na jānāsi yathā tasyāharniśaṃ gṛha-rakṣāṃ karomi | yato 'yaṃ cirān nirvṛto mamopayogaṃ na jānāti | tenādhunāpi mamāhāra-dāne mandādaraḥ | … ity uktvātīva cītkāra-śabdaṃ kṛtavān |*

English bridge (the course's) at the cut, for 1499–1511: "The donkey and the dog trade verses on servants and masters (Hit 2.32–2.33). Then the donkey, angry, says that he will wake the master himself, and cites a verse on serving a master (Hit 2.34)." The bray of 1513 (इत्युक्त्वा …) is the donkey's, as in the e-text.

**X6** — Hit 2.36 · `hitop_u.htm` 1522-1523

> जीविते यस्य जीवन्ति विप्रा मित्राणि बान्धवाः। सफलं जीवितं तस्य आत्मार्थे को न जीवति॥
>
> *jīvite yasya jīvanti viprā mitrāṇi bāndhavāḥ | saphalaṃ jīvitaṃ tasya ātmārthe ko na jīvati ||*


Anvaya (ours): यस्य जीविते विप्राः मित्राणि बान्धवाः जीवन्ति तस्य जीवितम् स-फलम्। आत्म-अर्थे कः न जीवति॥

Translation (ours): "He whose living keeps brahmins, friends and kin alive has a fruitful life: who does not live for himself?"

Not owned — **looked up in the capstone's Apte extract** (with the course glossary of Volumes 1–5; no marginal glosses; Apte headword in square brackets, `work/aptecheck.py`): विप्राः "brahmins" (vipraḥ) [Apte: vipraḥ]; बान्धवाः "kinsfolk" (bāndhavaḥ) [Apte: bāṃdhavaḥ]. **Course glosses:** none (the procedure reaches every word not owned).
Recognised (L): स-फलम् — sa- + phalam "fruitful" · आत्म-अर्थे — "for one's own sake" · जीविते — jīvitam (c02.l03), loc.
**Gloss ratio:** 2 of 13 words not owned at the ledger of that point (1 in 6.5), names excluded; without the Volume 5 headwords it contains (जीवितम्, c02.l03: जीविते, जीवितम्) 4 of 13 (1 in 3.2); course help after Apte 0 of 13.

Scansion (script `work/scan5.py`, checked by hand; the last syllable of a pāda is printed ×, either weight, §1.4.4; odd-pāda type by syllables 5–7): − ⏑ − − ⏑ − − × pathyā / − − − − ⏑ − ⏑ × · ⏑ ⏑ − − ⏑ − − × pathyā / − − − − ⏑ − ⏑ ×.

### 5.6 Displays, commentary lines and treatise examples (W1–W24, R1–R2)

Read for one purpose each, with the course's translation beside them; their words are gloss-line
items, never ledger words, and no ratio is measured for them (rule 24). Devanagari by
`work/wtexts.py` (W1–W11) and `work/rep/wnew.py` (W12–W24, R1–R2, added at the repair, critique G39) from the e-text
line; scansions by the same scripts, checked by hand, the last syllable of every pāda printed × (§1.4.4).

**W1** — Śaṅkara on BG 4.17 (excerpt) · `bhgsbh_u.htm` 1523 · c01.l01

> त्रिष्वप्यध्याहारः कर्तव्यः।
>
> *triṣv apy adhyāhāraḥ kartavyaḥ |*

Translation (ours): "In all three, a word must be supplied."

**W2** — Śaṅkara on BG 2.47 (excerpt; "the commentary ascribed to Saṃkara") · `bhgsbh_u.htm` 680 · c03.l03

> कर्मण्येवाधिकारो न ज्ञाननिष्ठायां ते तव। तत्र च कर्म कुर्वतः मा फलेषु अधिकारोऽस्तु …। यदा कर्मफले तृष्णा ते स्यात्तदा कर्मफलप्राप्तेर्हेतुः स्याः, एवं मा कर्मफलहेतुः भूः। यदा हि कर्मफलतृष्णाप्रयुक्तः कर्मणि प्रवर्तते तदा कर्मफलस्यैव जन्मनो हेतुर्भवेत्।
>
> *karmaṇy evādhikāro na jñāna-niṣṭhāyāṃ te tava | tatra ca karma kurvataḥ mā phaleṣu adhikāro 'stu … | yadā karma-phale tṛṣṇā te syāt tadā karma-phala-prāpter hetuḥ syāḥ, evaṃ mā karma-phala-hetuḥ bhūḥ | yadā hi karma-phala-tṛṣṇā-prayuktaḥ karmaṇi pravartate tadā karma-phalasyaiva janmano hetur bhavet |*

Translation (ours): "Your (te = tava) claim is to action alone, not to steadfastness in knowledge.
And there, as you act, let your claim not be to the fruits … When you have thirst for the fruit
of action, you would be the cause of obtaining the fruit of action: so do not be a cause of the
fruit of action. For when a man engages in action driven by thirst for its fruit, he becomes the
cause of the fruit of action itself, that is, of birth." Gloss line: निष्ठा "steadfastness"; तृष्णा
"thirst, craving"; प्रयुक्तः "driven"; प्रवर्तते "engages in"; जन्मन् "birth". **One analysis, and its
consequence:** कर्मफलप्राप्तेर्हेतुः "cause of obtaining the fruit" (a genitive tatpuruṣa), from which follows कर्मफलस्यैव
जन्मनो हेतुः "cause of the fruit itself, that is, of birth". The second reading of can-do 4 is the course's bahuvrīhi
कर्मफलं हेतुर्यस्य सः (G1's translation follows it, labelled); Rāmānuja's dvandva is W17 (Teacher Notes).

**W3** — Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 4.6 · `svetu_au.htm` 334–335; its Atharvaveda witness AV 9.9.20 · `avs_acu.htm` 8557–8558 · c03.l04 card

> द्वा सुपर्णा सयुजा सखाया समानं वृक्षं परिषस्वजाते। तयोरन्यः पिप्पलं स्वाद्वत्त्यनश्नन्नन्यो अभिचाकशीति॥
>
> *dvā suparṇā sayujā sakhāyā samānaṃ vṛkṣaṃ pariṣasvajāte | tayor anyaḥ pippalaṃ svādv atty anaśnann anyo abhicākaśīti ||* (Śvet., no accents)
>
> *dvā́ suparṇā́ sayújā sákhāyā samānáṃ vr̥kṣáṃ pári ṣasvajāte | táyor anyáḥ píppalaṃ svādv átty ánaśnann anyó abhí cākaśīti ||* (AV 9.9.20, as the accented e-text prints it; its word division and its ṛ-sign differ from the Upaniṣad's)

Translation (ours): "Two birds, companions joined together, clasp the same tree; of the two, one
eats the sweet fig, the other, not eating, looks on." The verse is a triṣṭubh: eleven syllables a pāda when
recited with the Vedic restorations (Whitney §113b, as for the gāyatrī: द्वा read duvā, स्वाद्वत्ति read svādu atti);
as written, pādas a and c have 10 and 9 syllables. Displayed, not scanned. Its अभिचाकशीति is an intensive (√काश्),
named on the c03.l04 card.

**W4** — Rāmāyaṇa 1.2.15–17 · `ram_01_u.htm` 292–297 · c04.l02

> तस्यैवं ब्रुवतश्चिन्ता बभूव हृदि वीक्षतः। शोकार्तेनास्य शकुनेः किमिदं व्याहृतं मया॥ चिन्तयन्स महाप्राज्ञश्चकार मतिमान्मतिम्। शिष्यं चैवाब्रवीद्वाक्यमिदं स मुनिपुंगवः॥ पादबद्धोऽक्षरसमस्तन्त्रीलयसमन्वितः। शोकार्तस्य प्रवृत्तो मे श्लोको भवतु नान्यथा॥
>
> *tasyaivaṃ bruvataś cintā babhūva hṛdi vīkṣataḥ | śokārtenāsya śakuneḥ kim idaṃ vyāhṛtaṃ mayā || cintayan sa mahāprājñaś cakāra matimān matim | śiṣyaṃ caivābravīd vākyam idaṃ sa munipuṃgavaḥ || pādabaddho 'kṣarasamas tantrīlayasamanvitaḥ | śokārtasya pravṛtto me śloko bhavatu nānyathā ||*

Translation (ours): "As he spoke so, watching, a thought arose in his heart: 'Stricken with grief
for this bird, what is this I have uttered?' Pondering it, the wise and thoughtful bull among
sages made up his mind and spoke these words to his pupil: 'Bound in quarters, even in its
syllables, fit for the measure of the lute: since it came from me stricken with grief (śoka), let
it be a śloka (śloka), and nothing else.'"

Scansion (`work/wtexts.py`): 1.2.15 − − − ⏑ ⏑ − − × pathyā / ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ × · − − − − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ × na-vipulā / ⏑ ⏑ − − ⏑ − ⏑ × || 1.2.16 − ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ − − × pathyā / ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ × · − − − − ⏑ − − × pathyā / ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ × || 1.2.17 − ⏑ − − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ × na-vipulā / − − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ × · − − − − ⏑ − − × pathyā / − − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ ×.

**W5** — Mammaṭa's example, Kāvyaprakāśa ex. 357 (lāṭānuprāsa); the e-text notes "(Śrivatsalāñchanācārya's Kāvyaparīkṣā 161)" · `mamkavpu.htm` 3735–3736, the note 3737 · c04.l04

> यस्य न सविधे दयिता दवदहनस्तुहिनदीधितिस्तस्य। यस्य च सविधे दयिता दवदहनस्तुहिनदीधितिस्तस्य॥
>
> *yasya na savidhe dayitā davadahanastuhinadīdhitistasya / yasya ca savidhe dayitā davadahanastuhinadīdhitistasya //*

Translation (ours): "For him whose beloved is not near, the cool-rayed moon is a forest fire; for
him whose beloved is near, the forest fire is the cool-rayed moon." Gloss line: सविधे "near"; दयिता
"beloved"; दवदहनः "forest fire"; तुहिनदीधितिः "the moon (cool-rayed)". The verse is a gīti (the āryā's variant
with 30 mātrās in both halves; a mātrā metre, beyond the course): displayed, not scanned. The e-text runs the
last three words of each half together (davadahanastuhinadīdhitistasya); printed as it stands (§1.4.1, Q5), the words parted only in the pause form.

**W6** — Bhartṛhari, Nītiśataka 1.19d · `bharst_u.htm` 187 · c04.l04

> क्षीयन्ते खलु भूषणानि सततं वाग्भूषणं भूषणम्॥
>
> *kṣīyante khalu bhūṣaṇāni satataṃ vāg-bhūṣaṇaṃ bhūṣaṇam ||*

Translation (ours): "Ornaments wear away, all the time; the ornament of speech is the ornament."
Scansion: − − − ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ − − − ⏑ − − ⏑ × (19: śārdūlavikrīḍita, the card metre).

**W7** — Kālidāsa, Raghuvaṃśa 9.4 · `kragh_pu.htm` 2206–2207 · c04.l04

> जनपदे न गदः पदमादधावभिभवः कुत एव सपत्नजः। क्षितिरभूत्फलवत्यजनन्दने शमरतेऽमरतेजसि पार्थिवे॥
>
> *janapade na gadaḥ padam ādadhāv abhibhavaḥ kuta eva sapatnajaḥ | kṣitir abhūt phalavaty ajanandane śamarate 'maratejasi pārthive ||*

Translation (ours): "In the land, disease set no foot — how then defeat born of a rival? The earth
bore fruit while Aja's son was king, delighting in calm (śama-rate), with an immortal's
splendour (amara-tejasi)." Scansion: four pādas ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ × (drutavilambita; display).

**W8** — Bhartṛhari, Nītiśataka 1.17 · `bharst_u.htm` 176–179 · c04.l04

> अधिगतपरमार्थान्पण्डितान्मावमंस्थास्तृणमिव लघु लक्ष्मीर्नैव तान्संरुणद्धि। अभिनवमदलेखाश्यामगण्डस्थलानां न भवति बिसतन्तुर्वारणं वारणानाम्॥
>
> *adhigata-paramārthān paṇḍitān māvamaṃsthās tṛṇam iva laghu lakṣmīr naiva tān saṃruṇaddhi | abhinava-mada-lekhā-śyāma-gaṇḍa-sthalānāṃ na bhavati bisatantur vāraṇaṃ vāraṇānām ||*

Translation (ours): "Do not despise the learned who have grasped the highest truth: fortune, light
as a straw, cannot hold them back. For elephants whose temples are dark with fresh streaks of
ichor, a lotus fibre is no restraint (vāraṇam … vāraṇānām)." मावमंस्थाः is मा + the augmentless
aorist (c03.l03's construction), glossed. Scansion: four pādas ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ − − − ⏑ − − ⏑ − × (mālinī;
display).

**W9** — Rājaśekhara, Kāvyamīmāṃsā (the poet's day) · `rajkmimu.htm` 1566–1567 · c05.l04 (Teacher Notes). The
e-text's header warns: "THE TEXT IS NOT PROOF-READ!" (line 31).

> चतुर्थ एकाकिनः परिमितपरिषदो वा पूर्वाह्नभागविहितस्य काव्यस्य परीक्षा। रसावेशतः काव्यं विरचयतो न च विवेक्त्री दृष्टिस्तस्मादनुपरीक्षेत।
>
> *caturtha ekākinaḥ parimitapariṣado vā pūrvāhnabhāgavihitasya kāvyasya parīkṣā / rasāveśataḥ kāvyaṃ viracayato na ca vivektrī dṛṣṭistasmādanuparīkṣeta /* (as the e-text prints it: compounds unhyphenated, and dṛṣṭistasmādanuparīkṣeta run together)

Translation (ours): "In the fourth (part of the day): the examination, alone or in a small circle,
of the poem made in the forenoon. One who composes a poem under the sway of feeling has no
discerning eye; so let him examine it afterwards."

**W10** — Hitopadeśa 1.166 · `hitop_u.htm` 1134–1135 · c05.l04 (its fourth pāda the practice samasyā; the verse shown after)

> सुखमापतितं सेव्यं दुःखमापतितं तथा। चक्रवत्परिवर्तन्ते दुःखानि च सुखानि च॥

> मृगतृष्णासमं वीक्ष्य संसारं क्षणभङ्गुरम्। सज्जनैः सङ्गतं कुर्याद्धर्माय च सुखाय च॥
>

made only of owned words (`work/samasya.py`).

**W12** — Mahāsubhāṣitasaṃgraha 6890 (Sternbach; the variant of the thread verse उद्यमेन हि) · `5_poetry/5_subhas/msubhs_u.htm` 13930–13931 · c01.l03 (display)

> उद्यमेनैव कार्याणि सिध्यन्ति न मनोरथैः। नहि सुप्तस्य सिंहस्य विशन्ति वदने मृगाः॥
>

Translation (ours): "Tasks succeed by effort alone, not by wishes: deer do not walk into the mouth of a sleeping
lion." Scansion: − ⏑ − − ⏑ − − × pathyā / − − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ × · ⏑ ⏑ − − ⏑ − − × pathyā / ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ ×. The

**W13** — Garuḍa Purāṇa 1.115.80 (the variant of Hit 0.37, S2b) · `3_purana/garup1_u.htm` 8485–8486 · c01.l03 (Station 0)

> माता शत्रुः पिता वैरी बाला येन न पाठिताः। सभामध्ये न शोभन्ते हंसमध्ये बकायथा॥
>

Translation (ours): "Mother an enemy, father a foe, by whom the children were not taught: they do not shine in an

0.37 has the singular बको यथा); printed as it stands. Scansion: − − − − ⏑ − − × pathyā / − − − ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ × · ⏑ − − − ⏑ − − × pathyā / − ⏑ − − ⏑ − ⏑ ×. Key `garudapurana`.

**R2** — the śānti सह नाववतु, at the opening of the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad in its e-text (and again at its close,
593–594, 596) · `1_veda/4_upa/svetugau.htm` 116–117, 119 · c01.l05 (assembly)

> औं। सह नाववतु। सह नौ भुनक्तु। सह वीर्यं करवावहै। तेजस्वि नावधीतमस्तु। मा विद्विषावहै। औं शान्तिः शान्तिः शान्तिः।
>
> *auṃ / saha nāv avatu / saha nau bhunaktu / saha vīryaṃ karavāvahai / tejasvi nāv adhītam astu / mā vidviṣāvahai / auṃ śāntiḥ śāntiḥ śāntiḥ*

Translation (ours): "Om. Together may he guard us two; together may he sustain us two; together may we two do
valiant work; may what we two have studied be full of light; may we two not hate each other. Om. Peace, peace,
peace." (*auṃ* is the e-text's spelling of ॐ here; the close, 593, writes *oṃ*.) Prose mantra: not scanned.

**W14** — Śaṅkara (attrib.), Kaṭhopaniṣadbhāṣya on 6.19 (the end of the comment on the śānti) · `6_sastra/3_phil/vedanta/samkaubh_u.htm` 796 · c01.l05 (§15)

> शान्तिः शान्तिः शान्तिरिति त्रिर्वचनं सर्वदोषोपशमनार्थमित्योमिति॥
>
> *śāntiḥ śāntiḥ śāntir iti trir vacanaṃ sarvadoṣopaśamanārtham ity om iti //*

Translation (ours): "The threefold utterance 'śāntiḥ śāntiḥ śāntiḥ' is for the allaying of all faults. Om." The
same line names the faults just before: those of the pupil and the teacher in receiving and imparting knowledge
(… विद्याग्रहणप्रतिपादननिमित्तदोषप्रशमनार्थेयं शान्तिरुच्यते). TRADITION, tier R; key `kathopanishadbhashya`.

**W15** — Pañcatantra 2.58 (GRETIL numbering; the 1.x "moral") · `5_poetry/4_narr/vispancu.htm` 2976–2977 · c02.l02

> विद्वत्त्वं च नृपत्वं च नैव तुल्यं कदाचन। स्वदेशे पूज्यते राजा विद्वान्सर्वत्र पूज्यते॥
>

Translation (ours): "Learning and kingship are never at all equal: a king is honoured in his own land, a learned man
is honoured everywhere." Scansion: − − − ⏑ ⏑ − − × pathyā / − ⏑ − − ⏑ − ⏑ × · ⏑ − − − ⏑ − − × pathyā / − − − − ⏑ − ⏑ ×.

**W16** — Pañcatantra 4.16 · `vispancu.htm` 5311–5314 · c02.l02 (§17; the desiderative card)

> बुभुक्षितः किं न करोति पापं क्षीणा जना निष्करुणा भवन्ति। आख्याहि भद्रे प्रियदर्शनस्य न गङ्गदत्तः पुनरेति कूपम्॥
>
> *bubhukṣitaḥ kiṃ na karoti pāpaṃ kṣīṇā janā niṣkaruṇā bhavanti | ākhyāhi bhadre priya-darśanasya na gaṅgadattaḥ punar eti kūpam ||*

Translation (ours): "What sin does a hungry man not commit? Starving people become pitiless. Tell Priyadarśana, good
lady: Gaṅgadatta is not coming back to the well." बुभुक्षितः "hungry" is the desiderative's -त (√भुज्), glossed.
Scansion: ⏑ − ⏑ − − ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ − × upendravajrā / − − ⏑ − − ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ − × indravajrā · − − ⏑ − − ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ − × indravajrā /
⏑ − ⏑ − − ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ − × upendravajrā (an upajāti).

**W17** — Rāmānuja, Gītābhāṣya on BG 2.47 (excerpt) · `6_sastra/3_phil/vedanta/ramgbhau.htm` 545 · c03.l03 (Teacher Notes)

> मा च कर्मफलयोर्हेतुभूः।
>
> *mā ca karma-phalayor hetu-bhūḥ /*

Translation (ours): "And do not be the cause of action and fruit." कर्मफलयोः is the genitive dual of the dvandva
कर्मफले "action and fruit": Rāmānuja's reading of कर्मफलहेतुः, beside Śaṅkara's (W2). TRADITION, not assessed; key
`ramanujagitabhashya` (the e-text names no edition).

**W18** — Kālidāsa, Kumārasambhava 1.1 · `5_poetry/2_kavya/kakumspu.htm` 97–98 · c04.l03 (display)

> अस्त्युत्तरस्यां दिशि देवतात्मा हिमालयो नाम नगाधिराजः। पूर्वापरौ तोयनिधी विगाह्य स्थितः पृथिव्या इव मानदण्डः॥
>
> *asty uttarasyāṃ diśi devatātmā himālayo nāma nagādhirājaḥ / pūrvāparau toyanidhī vigāhya sthitaḥ pṛthivyā iva mānadaṇḍaḥ //*

Translation (ours): "In the northern quarter is the king of mountains, Himālaya by name, whose self is divine: plunging
into the eastern and the western oceans, it stands like a measuring rod of the earth." Scansion: − − ⏑ − − ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ − ×
indravajrā / ⏑ − ⏑ − − ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ − × upendravajrā · − − ⏑ − − ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ − × indravajrā / ⏑ − ⏑ − − ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ − ×
upendravajrā (an upajāti).

**W19** — Kālidāsa, Meghadūta 1 · `5_poetry/2_kavya/kmeghdpu.htm` 102–103 · c04.l03 (display)

> कश्चित्कान्ताविरहगुरुणा स्वाधिकारप्रमत्तः शापेनास्तङ्गमितमहिमा वर्षभोग्येण भर्तुः। यक्षश्चक्रे जनकतनयास्नानपुण्योदकेषु स्निग्धच्छायातरुषु वसतिं रामगिर्याश्रमेषु॥
>
> *kaś cit kāntāvirahaguruṇā svādhikārapramattaḥ śāpenāstaṅgamitamahimā varṣabhogyeṇa bhartuḥ / yakṣaś cakre janakatanayāsnānapuṇyodakeṣu snigdhacchāyātaruṣu vasatiṃ rāmagiryāśrameṣu //*

Translation (ours): "A certain yakṣa, careless of his charge, his greatness brought low by his master's curse, to be
borne for a year and heavy with separation from his beloved, made his dwelling in the hermitages of Rāmagiri, whose
waters are holy from the bathing of Janaka's daughter and whose trees give dense shade." Scansion, each pāda: − − − − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ − − ⏑ − − ⏑ − × mandākrāntā (all four; pauses after syllables 4 and 10, W20).

**W20** — Kedāra's mandākrāntā verse, in Sulhaṇa's text of the Vṛttaratnākara, KVrk 3.100 · `5_poetry/1_chandas/kvrtrsuu.htm` 1427 · c04.l03 (◐ card)

> मन्दाक्रान्ता जलधिषडगैः म्भौ नतौ ताद्गुरू चेत्॥
>
> *mandākrāntā jaladhiṣaḍagaiḥ mbhau natau tādgurū cet //*

Translation (ours): "It is the mandākrāntā, with pauses after the oceans (4), six (6) and the mountains (7), if (it
has) ma, bha, na, ta, ta, and then two heavy syllables." Printed as the e-text has it: the visarga of जलधिषडगैः stands
unjoined before म्भौ (joined it would be -गैर्म्भौ); Kedāra's own e-text, KedV 3.97 (`kedvratu.htm` 392), reads *tād guru*
for *tādgurū*, which is why Sulhaṇa's line is printed. Scansion: − − − − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ − − ⏑ − − ⏑ − × mandākrāntā (the
line scans in its own metre). Key `vrttaratnakara`.

**W21** — Kālidāsa, Raghuvaṃśa 1.1 · `5_poetry/2_kavya/kragh_pu.htm` 103–104 · c04.l04

> वागर्थाविव संपृक्तौ वागर्थप्रतिपत्तये। जगतः पितरौ वन्दे पार्वतीपरमेश्वरौ॥
>
> *vāgarthāv iva saṃpṛktau vāgarthapratipattaye / jagataḥ pitarau vande pārvatīparameśvarau //*

Translation (ours): "To the parents of the world, Pārvatī and Parameśvara, joined like word and meaning, I bow, for the
understanding of word and meaning." An upamā with इव (upameya the two parents, upamāna word and meaning, the common
property संपृक्तौ "joined"): the course's identification, by Mammaṭa's K87a. Scansion: − − − ⏑ ⏑ − − × pathyā / − − − ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ × · ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ − − × pathyā / − ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ ×. Key `raghuvamsha`.

**W22** — Daṇḍin, Kāvyādarśa 1.55ab, 1.56–57, 1.58ab (anuprāsa: the definition, his two examples, the close) · `5_poetry/1_alam/dkavy12u.htm` 210; 212–215; 216 · c04.l04

> वर्णावृत्तिरनुप्रासः पादेषु च पदेषु च। … चन्द्रे शरन्निशोत्तंसे कुन्दस्तबकविभ्रमे। इन्द्रनीलनिभं लक्ष्म संदधात्यलिनः श्रियम्॥ चारु चान्द्रमसं भीरु बिम्बं पश्यैतदम्बरे। मन्मनो मन्मथाक्रान्तं निर्दयं हन्तुमुद्यतं॥ इत्यनुप्रासमिच्छन्ति नातिदूरान्तरश्रुतिम्।
>
> *varṇāvṛttir anuprāsaḥ pādeṣu ca padeṣu ca & … candre śaranniśottaṃse kundastabakavibhrame & indranīlanibhaṃ lakṣma saṃdadhāty alinaḥ śriyam // cāru cāndramasaṃ bhīru bimbaṃ paśyaitad ambare & manmano manmathākrāntaṃ nirdayaṃ hantum udyataṃ // ity anuprāsam icchanti nātidūrāntaraśrutim &*

Translation (ours): "Anuprāsa is the repetition of sounds, in pādas and in words. … 'In the moon, crest-ornament of
the autumn night, bright as a cluster of jasmine, the mark, dark as sapphire, takes on the beauty of a bee.' 'Look,
timid one, at this lovely disc of the moon in the sky, pitiless, set on striking my heart, which Love has
overpowered.' Such is the anuprāsa they want: the sounds heard at no great distance." The runs are न in 1.56 and च,
म, न in 1.57 (the course's count of the repeated sounds, by Daṇḍin's definition). *udyataṃ* with ṃ before the pause
is the e-text's, printed as it stands. Scansion: 1.55ab − − − ⏑ ⏑ − − × pathyā / − − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ × || 1.56 − − ⏑ − ⏑ − − × pathyā / − − ⏑ ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ × · − ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ − − × pathyā / − ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ − ⏑ × || 1.57 − ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ − − × pathyā / − − − − ⏑ − ⏑ × · − ⏑ − − ⏑ − − × pathyā / − ⏑ − − ⏑ − ⏑ × || 1.58ab − ⏑ − − ⏑ − − × pathyā / − ⏑ − − ⏑ − ⏑ ×. Key
`kavyadarsha`.

**W23** — Mahāsubhāṣitasaṃgraha 7104 (upamā kālidāsasya …) · `msubhs_u.htm` 14359–14360 · c04.l04 (TRADITION about the poets)

> उपमा कालिदासस्य भारवेरर्थगौरवम्। दण्डिनः पदलालित्यं माघे सन्ति त्रयो गुणाः॥
>
> *upamā kālidāsasya bhāraverarthagauravam / daṇḍinaḥ padalālityaṃ māghe santi trayo guṇāḥ //*

Translation (ours): "Simile is Kālidāsa's, weight of meaning Bhāravi's, grace of words Daṇḍin's: in Māgha are all three
qualities." Printed without a speaker, as Sternbach prints it; the e-text has no source apparatus (key
`sternbach1974`). Scansion: ⏑ ⏑ − − ⏑ − − × pathyā / − ⏑ − − ⏑ − ⏑ × · − ⏑ − ⏑ ⏑ − − × pathyā / − − − − ⏑ − ⏑ ×.

**W24** — Viśvanātha, Sāhityadarpaṇa (the rūpaka compound) · `5_poetry/1_alam/visah__u.htm` 8464–8465 · c04.l04 (card)

> एवं — "सुन्दरं वदनाम्बुजम्" इत्यादौ साधारणधर्मप्रयोगे "उपमितं व्याघ्रादिभिः सामान्याप्रयोगे" इति वचनादुपमासमासो न संभवतीत्युपमाया बाधकः। एवं चात्र मयूरव्यंसकादित्वाद्रूपकसमास एव।
>
> *evaṃ--"sundaraṃ vadanāmbujam" ityādau sādhāraṇadharmaprayoge "upamitaṃ vyāghrādibhiḥ sāmānyāprayoge" iti vacanādupamāsamāso na saṃbhavatītyupamāyā bādhakaḥ / evaṃ cātra mayūravyaṃsakāditvādrūpakasamāsa eva /*

Translation (ours): "Likewise, in 'sundaraṃ vadanāmbujam' ('a lovely lotus-face') and the like, where a common
property is used, an upamā compound is not possible, by the rule 'upamitaṃ vyāghrādibhiḥ sāmānyāprayoge' (2.1.56: only
where no common property is used): this is what bars the upamā. And so here, because it belongs to the
mayūravyaṃsaka group (2.1.72), it is a rūpaka compound only." TRADITION; the e-text's header warns that "The text is
not proof-read!" (key `sahityadarpana`).

## 6. Beyond the course

Nothing follows Volume 5. Each row is named to the learner in c05.l05 (§21), with the next text or
tool the course can point to from its own sources.

| Beyond the course | What the course left at | The next step it names |
|---|---|---|
| Vedic accent as a system; hearing it; Vedic grammar (leṭ, Vedic infinitives, the subjunctive) | one TRADITION card (c03.l04: four sūtras, the accent marks of one AV verse read off); one leṭ line (v4.c03.l05) | the Ṛgveda with its padapāṭha (the GRETIL e-texts the course used); a Vedic grammar (Whitney's Vedic notes, `whitney1889`) |
| The Mahābhāṣya, the Paribhāṣenduśekhara, the other paribhāṣās | named (Volume 4, HISTORY) | the Kāśikā read on, sūtra by sūtra (`kashika`), then the Mahābhāṣya's Paspaśāhnika |
| Production of the aorist, benedictive, conditional, periphrastic future; the secondary conjugations; the perfect's 1st/2nd persons | parse-only (§1.3.4) | Whitney chs. X–XIV, read as reference |
| Forms no Volume 5 lesson text prints: the numeral adverbs in -धा and -शः (एकैकशः), the future participle, the -अम् absolutive, the class-3 present beyond ददाति (met once, in the display W22: संदधाति), the perfect participle in -आन | **not planned in Volume 5** (§4.0.2, added at the repair, critique G10) | Whitney §1104 and §1106 (-dhā, -śas), §939 (future participles), §995 (the gerund in -am), §642 ff. (class 3), §806 (-āna), read as reference |
| Long metres in production (mandākrāntā, śārdūlavikrīḍita); the other syllabic metres (śikhariṇī, vaṃśastha, mālinī, drutavilambita, sragdharā …); mātrā metres (āryā, vaitālīya) | mandākrāntā and śārdūlavikrīḍita on a card (◐); seen as displays: W6–W8, W19 (W5 a gīti); KedV 2.1 ff. named | the Vṛttaratnākara read whole (`vrttaratnakara`) |
| Figures beyond five (utprekṣā, śleṣa, arthāntaranyāsa, yathāsaṅkhya …); dhvani and rasa; the theory of poetry as a system | five figures; the upamita-compound card; the further figures (Daṇḍin 2.221 and 2.234, Mammaṭa K109) and Vāmana's sūtras in Teacher Notes (moved there at the repair, critique G30) | the Kāvyaprakāśa read on (`kavyaprakasha`) and Daṇḍin's Kāvyādarśa 2 |
| Kāvya proper (Raghuvaṃśa, Kumārasambhava, Meghadūta; drama) | single verses as displays (W18, W19, W21; Ragh 9.4, W7) | Raghuvaṃśa 1, with its anvaya, verse by verse |
| The rest of the epics (Nala beyond 3.51; the Gītā beyond 2.48; the Rāmāyaṇa) | E1–E2, X3, G1 | Nala 3.51–78, read with Apte by the c02.l02 procedure (the prepared Nala texts measure 1 in 4.3 to 1 in 6.6 by ledger coverage, and 1 in 35 to 1 in 38 after Apte, E1 2 of 69 and E2 3 of 113, recounted at the repair; whether a learner continues alone is not measured) |
| Upaniṣads beyond one passage and one display verse; Śaṅkara's commentary | U1, W3; W1–W2 read, two lines of the Bṛhadāraṇyaka bhāṣya in Teacher Notes | the Īśa and Kaṭha (local e-texts), then the Bṛhadāraṇyaka with Śaṅkara |
| A critical apparatus; textual criticism | variants of three verses (c01.l03) | Sternbach's Mahāsubhāṣitasaṃgraha with its source lists (`sternbach1974`) |

| Manuscripts and scripts other than Devanagari | — | not named |

## 7. Decisions taken at synthesis, and the repair

The base is the design "texts first" (ranked first by two of the three judges; mean 48.3 of 70).
It was copied, its sections reordered into this structure by `synth-work/restructure.py` (no line
lost: sorted diff checked; 85 cross-references renumbered and re-grepped), and then edited, never
retyped. Every judge's finding below was re-verified in the local file named before anything was
changed. The plan was then repaired against an 80-gap critique (§7.4); rows of §7.1–§7.2 that the repair changed
say so in place, and §7.3 now holds the deputy's decisions on the open questions, for the editor to confirm.

### 7.3 Deputy's decisions on the open questions (for the editor to confirm)

Each question the plan puts to the editor, with the decision the deputy took at the repair. The rules, in order:
correctness first; the ROADMAP's chapters and lesson counts stand; one teaching point per lesson and at most eight
headwords; material moved later or beyond the course rather than overloading a lesson; the ledger chain kept
(627 → 659 · 691 · 714 · 733 · 751, unchanged); the evidence rule never relaxed. Q3 (the gāyatrī's 24) was settled at
synthesis (§7.1 #7); Q15 is closed at the repair; Q16 is new. The numbers are kept so that references stay valid.
Nothing below is final until the editor confirms it.

1. **Q1 — The reading target (returned to the editor at the repair, §7.4 G9).** *Question:* Volume 4's Q10, a
   confirmed decision, handed Volume 5 the target of one gloss in eight to ten words. The plan proposes instead at
   most one course gloss in 20 words after Apte, with ledger coverage reported both ways. Accept?
   *Decision:* accept the replacement, as a proposal. The old target cannot be reached by any means the rules allow.
   The recounted unseen texts run from 1 in 3.6 (X4) to 1 in 6.5 (X6) by ledger coverage (§2.2, §5). In the DCS Hitopadeśa, all 124 headwords move the words not owned only from 1 in 2.8 to 1 in 3.0. Reaching
   one in eight by headwords would take hundreds more, which breaks the eight-headword rule and the chain. After
   Apte, the capstone's X5 needs no course gloss in 78 words, X2 needs 3 in 69, and the prepared texts need 0–3 each
   (§5). §4.0.2 records the Volume 4 row as "not reached (measured, §2.2); replacement proposed (Q1)".
2. **Q2 — Decisions V5-D1 to V5-D5.** *Question:* five cards with listed ✓ cells sit beside reading points: the
   causative's kṛt forms (c02.l01), the periphrastic perfect (c03.l01), the Ā perfect (c03.l02), and, recorded at
   the repair (§7.4 G31), the feminines in -वी, -इनी, -मती (c01.l04) and the laṅ passive (c02.l03), as Volume 4's
   V4-D6 did. Confirm all five, or keep some ◐ at C1?
   *Decision:* confirm all five. No card adds a rule: each lists cells that the readings print, as Δs of owned
   tables, and each lesson keeps its one teaching point. V5-D1's rule was wrong and is corrected (§7.4 G1): 6.4.51
   drops the -अय- before ल्यप् (प्रकोप्य, आनन्द्य), and 6.4.56 keeps it as -अय् after a light syllable (प्रणमय्य, TN).
   Its ✓ cells were re-checked against the corrected rule. If the editor wants less load, V5-D4 and V5-D5 are the
   two cards that can go to ◐ without touching a can-do. Their production would then wait for the c05 checklist,
   and the c02.l05 item on the V5-D5 cells would become a parsing item.
3. **Q4 — Dates.** *Question:* only the dates a confirmed source supports may print. Accept?
   *Decision:* two dates print, both HISTORY, hedged and keyed (§1.1 principle 5). They are Mammaṭa's century
   (c04.l04 §17; the key's search found an edition's introduction reporting De's and Kane's estimates) and the
   manuscript limit for Kedāra (c04.l01 §17; BnF authority record, re-found). The Pañcatantra's estimated date is
   withdrawn: the source that quotes its translator's own words, `olivelle1997`, is not confirmed (§7.4 G38).
   Bhartṛhari, Daṇḍin, Kālidāsa and Vallabhadeva get no date until a fact base finds a source quoting its own page.
4. **Q5 — Typos and departures in e-texts.** *Question:* is the rule "choose another text or cut; correct none"
   the rule for Volume 5 prose as well as verse?
   *Decision:* yes, with one addition. A text with an evident error is replaced or cut (§4.0.5 c). A checked,
   consistent reading is kept and flagged (*nasvasthām*). A departure from full sandhi, or from the house markup, in
   an authentic text is printed as it stands and noted, and the normalised reading stands only in the pause form:
   S3c2's brackets and compound dot, W20's unjoined visarga, U1's वा असत्, W22's *udyataṃ*, W13's *bakāyathā*. The
   Nirukta line is quoted only as far as it needs no correction, with the e-text's *m* kept (*gāyatrīm eva tripadām
   satīm*, §7.4 G55). So the plan corrects no reading.
5. **Q6 — P3's content.** *Question:* keep the Vīravara middle (a father offers his son's head), or choose
   another c02.l03 text?
   *Decision:* keep it. It is the thread's unread part (§6), it carries the lesson's -अनीय forms, and the Teacher
   Notes frame of c02.l03 stays. At the repair its loci were corrected (2958; 2962; 2969 part; 2972–2973; 2976 part),
   and its last sentence is printed in full: राजा च साष्टाङ्गपातं प्रणम्योवाच (§7.4 G14).
6. **Q7 — Hit 0.25's subject.** *Decision:* keep the verse as it is. The verse's own word is printed and translated
   plainly; the variant pair Hit 0.37 / Garuḍa P. 1.115.80 (W13) stays a Station 0 display.
7. **Q8 — The aorist chunks.** *Decision:* confirm that मा भूत् and मा भूः add 0. They are forms of the owned
   भवति (rule 17), as मा भैषीः was counted once in Volume 4.
8. **Q9 — §5 size.** *Decision:* keep §5 in the plan. The repair's recount was checked against it line by line,
   and the plan is now about 428 KB (the synthesis text was about 320 KB). The chapter fact bases may take §5 over
   later and leave the plan only labels,
   loci and ratios. §5 still omits the pause-form line, which `render.py` regenerates.
9. **Q10 — Recitation checking.** *Decision:* confirm that the course promises no check of pronunciation, since
   no reference recordings exist in the project. Capstone task B is rescored (§7.4 G34): correct text 5; the pāda
   ends and weights marked by the learner on their own transcript of the recording 5; X6's scansion 5. Audibility
   itself is not scored.
10. **Q11 — The accent card, and the ROADMAP note (extended at the repair, §7.4 G13).** *Question:* the ROADMAP
    note binds "one TRADITION card for accent and the padapāṭha, read on an Upaniṣad line". No local e-text allows
    that. The card reads the accents on Śvet. 4.6 through its Atharvaveda witness (AV 9.9.20, W3) and the padapāṭha
    on RV 3.62.10 (`rvpp_03u.htm` 1133). Accept this departure, or read RV 1.164.20's padapāṭha (`rvpp_01u.htm`
    3044) with its slips listed?
    *Decision:* accept the departure. RV 1.164.20's padapāṭha prints *samanam* and *sasvajāteiti*, and the evidence
    rule does not print a slip as a reading. §4.0.2 says the same. W3 is read for its marks only.
11. **Q12 — The Apte extract.** *Decision:* print the headword line and the first senses of each entry. MW is not
    needed: the rewritten `work/aptecheck.py` finds an Apte (1890) headword for every word of X5 and X6 that the
    learner does not own, among them श्वानः and जागृ. Of X2's thirteen, it finds ten. The other three (दुर्गे,
    सकलदायादानाम्, एनम्) are glossed in X2's margin (§7.4 G7).
12. **Q13 — The three-sitting capstone (115 minutes).** *Decision:* confirm three sittings. X5 stays at 78 words:
    its cut is bridged in English (1499–1511), so the bray of 1513 follows the donkey's speech as in the e-text
    (§7.4 G5). Printing the Sanskrit of 1509 instead would add about 21 words and six words to look up.
13. **Q14 — The Nītiśataka and Narasiṃha / Yājñavalkya witnesses.** *Decision:* keep the three keys. c01.l03
    teaches the evidence rule itself, and S3c2 is now printed as its e-text stands (§7.4 G16).
14. **Q15 — Mandākrāntā without a line: closed at the repair (§7.4 G58).** Sulhaṇa's KVrk 3.100 (`kvrtrsuu.htm`
    1427) is printed as the e-text has it (W20). Its only departure is the unjoined visarga of जलधिषडगैः, which is
    noted. The discrepancy named is Kedāra's own e-text's *tād guru* (KedV 3.97, `kedvratu.htm` 392), where the
    metre needs the dual *gurū*. The critique asked for the line to be joined; it is printed unjoined and noted, by
    Q5's rule (§7.4, deviations).
15. **Q16 — The three śānti (new at the repair, §7.4 G11).** *Question:* IR §9 item 4 and the ROADMAP preservation
    map class the 1.x three-śānti explanation (pp.47–51) as correct. The course finds its link to three kinds of
    suffering unsourced: the Yogasūtrabhāṣya on YS 1.31 gives the triad but not the link. Print Śaṅkara's reason in
    its place?
    *Decision:* yes, as c01.l05 §15 now prints it. The 1.x line is quoted as an unsourced link, and the
    commentator's reason stands in its place: शान्तिः शान्तिः शान्तिरिति त्रिर्वचनं सर्वदोषोपशमनार्थम्
    (Śaṅkara, attrib., on Kaṭha 6.19, W14; TRADITION, tier R; assessed only "According to Śaṅkara …"). The map's row
    is still met item by item; only the explanation's register changes. The editor confirms the change to IR §9
    item 4's classification.

**Further decisions taken at the repair (for the editor to confirm).**
- **R-1 — X5's cut.** Of the critique's two fixes, the English bridge was chosen over printing 1509. It keeps the
  capstone text at 78 words and its timing, and it leaves the ledger untouched (G5).
- **R-2 — The second analysis of कर्मफलहेतुः.** It is the course's bahuvrīhi (कर्मफलं हेतुर्यस्य सः), labelled as
  the course's, and G1's translation follows it. Rāmānuja's dvandva (W17) is in Teacher Notes, so the lesson gains
  no point to teach and the evidence of a second commentator stays in view (G3).
- **R-3 — Capstone task C.** Two forms are drawn by lot, कृत्वा and प्रसुप्तः, and the card is printed in §4.0.8.
  The option of deriving the learner's own verb is withdrawn, because its sūtras cannot be listed in advance. C is
  scaled to 20, and E is scored 5 (G35, G37).
- **R-4 — Rule 25.** Labels never count, even when a sentence uses one. The three models keep their labels
  unquoted, and the chain stays at 751 (G67).
- **R-5 — Lemma glossaries.** They are counted by lemma: X3 has 8 lemmas and 8 decoys; X4 has 9 and 9 (G57).
- **R-6 — Statuses beyond the critique's list.** The same rule was applied to every token met in the recount, not
  only to the critique's list. The extra recodes are listed under §7.4's deviations. They move five printed figures
  away from the critique's: E1, P4, X2, E2 and M1. P1 and P3 differ for the same reason (अभ्युपेत्य L, सुचिरम् T) and because their texts grew (G24, G14).

correct fix was made and is listed under **Deviations** below. Figures were recounted by `work/measure.py` on the

`work/texts_state.synth.py`), the §5 status lines regenerated by `work/rep/render5.py`, the scansions by
`work/scan5.py` and `work/rep/wnew.py`; the arithmetic checks are listed after the deviations.

**Deviations: where the fix as written was wrong or could not stand, the nearest correct fix was made.**
1. **G4, G6:** the figures differ from the critique's in seven texts. The same rule was applied to every token, and
   two texts grew. P1 is 10 of 107, because the frame was added (G24) and अभ्युपेत्य is L on the owned एति, as एहि is. P3 is 13 of 85, because its last sentence
   is printed in full (G14) and सुचिरम् (su- + the lesson's चिरम्) is known with the headwords, as चिरात् is. P4 is 11 of 83: प्रक्षिप is L, on the owned क्षिपति. X2 is 13 of 69: 5323 was added
   (G53), and एहि ×3 is L, on the owned एति. E1 is 16 of 69: प्रजाकामः ×2 is G, because कामः is headworded only in
   c03.l02, and उदारान् is L, because उदारः is owned. E2 is 17 of 113, 25 without (the critique has 21 and 28):
   समुत्पत्य, समुपाधावत्, त्वरमाणा and व्याजहार are L on owned verbs, अश्विनोः is a name, and आस्ते is a lesson headword. M1 is
   3 of 18: प्रतिहन्यमानाः is L, the taught passive -मान of the owned हन्ति, and विघ्नविहताः is T, on the lesson's
   विघ्नः. Other recodes change no figure the critique printed. S3b's and S3b2's हीनाः left 'pre', because the word
   is owned from c01.l02 (S3b now reads 6 of 13 without). P1's आत्मप्रतिबिम्बम् and P3's उपहारीकरोषि went into 'pre'.
2. **G7:** P2's one word after Apte is अर्पय, not the long bahuvrīhi: every member of that compound is an Apte
   headword.
3. **G10:** the denominative is not recorded as "not planned": P2 prints आकर्ण्य (MW karṇaya, a nominal verb), and
   the c02.l02 card names it.
4. **G24:** the card's अभूत् and अगमत् come from Volume 4's c03.l05 card, because no text read before c02.l02 prints
   either; अगात् is P2's.
5. **G29:** the new model is पश्यतां जनानां सोऽहसत्, not the critique's … नकुलः सर्पमहन्, in which सर्पम् could itself
   take the genitive.

   C is scaled to 20, and the five points go to E (G35).
7. **G38:** task E's HISTORY item is Mammaṭa's century, not the Pañcatantra's date, because the Pañcatantra key is
   not confirmed.
8. **G55:** the Nirukta clause keeps the e-text's *tripadām satīm*; the critique wrote *tripadāṃ*.
9. **G58:** KVrk 3.100 is printed unjoined, as the e-text has it, and the unjoined visarga is noted (principle 2).
   The critique asked for it to be joined.
10. **G67:** rule 25 now says labels never count, even in sentences, but the models were not put in quotation marks.
    They use the labels as words of the sentence, and a quoted form would have to be joined to an इति that the
    sentences do not have (STYLE_GUIDE §3.1).
